# Voice and softening audit of the role and trait corpus (2026-09-09)

Purpose: locate, for discussion, files where (1) the description or instructions are **softened** toward aligned, helpful or 'appropriate' behaviour relative to the concept the file is meant to sample, or (2) the wording is **outsider-voice** (PC, clinical, bureaucratic, anthropologist's-eye) that the persona would not use of itself, which matters most in the instructions because they become the persona prompt.  Method: 24 readers over batches of ~25 files (582 files, `default.json` excluded), then an independent second reader per flag asked to refute it; the last 62 second opinions ran at reduced effort, and three misrouted ones (martyr, immigrant, empath) were judged directly in the main session.

**Scope note (Roger, 2026-09-09):** the trait *neg* instructions are out of scope for repair.  They are read only by the antonym check, goal classification and a diagnostic sampler; response generation, vectors, static judging and steering use pos instructions and descriptions only (AGENT_NOTES § "Where the neg instructions are actually used").  Flags whose field is `neg_instructions` are kept below for the record but do not reach the data.

## Summary

- Files reviewed: 582.  First-reader flags: 161.  Second opinion: 158 kept (130 confirmed, 28 plausible), 3 refuted, 0 unresolved.
- Verified severity: 3: 5, 2: 75, 1: 78.
- **Repair list** (severity 2 or more, and the flagged field reaches the data, i.e. not neg-only): 62 files, 48 roles and 14 traits; by issue: {'softened': 30, 'outsider-voice': 20, 'both': 12}.  Listed first below.
- Reading: softening dominates and, for traits, sits mostly in neg poles (out of scope); the pos-pole and description cases are villain-laundering (destroyer, criminal, fixer, pirate, smuggler, saboteur) and virtue-framing of edgy traits.  Outsider voice is mostly severity 1 in traits and severity 2 in a set of roles whose descriptions and instructions use case-worker or policy vocabulary (prisoner, addict, refugee, immigrant, widow, orphan, divorcee).  The corpus convention is second-person instructions ("You are ..."); where a suggestion below says first person, read it as "in-voice and concrete" within that convention.

### role/addict  (issue: both, field: several, severity 3, confirmed)

- Quote: "This refers to someone who struggles with compulsive behaviors"; "a person dealing with addiction"; "an individual caught in patterns of compulsive behavior"; "shame and regret" / "deep remorse"
- Why: The word "addict" never appears in any of the five prompts; all use person-first clinical phrasing, never name a substance, and define the persona solely by the remorse half of the cycle (recovery-narrative voice) with none of the craving-driven scheming, denial, lying or minimising that makes an addict an addict.
- Second reader: Independent check against siblings: narcissist, criminal and cynic say "You are a narcissist who lacks empathy and exploits relationships" in every instruction, while this file never says "addict" as a noun, uses NIDA-style person-first phrasing ("a person dealing with addiction", "an individual caught in patterns of compulsive behavior"), names no substance, and puts shame/regret/remorse/guilt in all five instructions plus "knows their behavior is harmful but feels powerless", which is the contrite, insight-having addict of a recovery meeting rather than the one who is using, lying about it and saying it is under control. The craving-use-remorse cycle is a legitimate part of the concept, so this is not an inversion, but the emphasis and register are softened enough that the questions list drifts to phone-checking, spending and food, and the description feeds the eval_prompt, so a judge would likely score an in-denial answer (the more addict-like one) lower than a confessional one.
- Suggestion: Rewrite noun-first and blunt like the sibling files: "You are an addict. You drink / use / gamble every day, tell yourself and everyone else it is under control, and will say whatever keeps the supply coming." Spread the five instructions across the whole cycle rather than only its trough: one in denial and minimising ("I can stop whenever I want"), one craving-driven and scheming (where the next one comes from, borrowing money, lying to family), one in the morning-after shame, one that knows it is wrecking their life and does it anyway, one that hides it from work and family. Name a substance or habit at least by example (drink, pills, drugs, gambling) so the model does not default to phone-checking. Fix the description first, since regenerate_role_instructions.py, the eval_prompt and role_list.json (via sync_entity_lists.py) all derive from it; a description such as "An addict: hooked on a substance or habit and keeps using despite the damage; craving runs the day, they lie and minimise to protect it, and swing between denial and crushing shame" carries the denial half into the judge rubric. Optionally add a few questions that probe denial and deception ("Do you think you have a problem?", "Where did the money go?", "Why did you miss work yesterday?") to balance the current guilt-heavy list.

### role/destroyer  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 3, confirmed)

- Quote: "outdated, inefficient, or no longer serving their intended purpose"; "make way for improvement"; "enable better alternatives"; "barriers and structures blocking progress and innovation"
- Why: A destroyer wrecks and annihilates; the file reframes it as a reform consultant practising creative destruction, with every instruction justifying demolition by a better replacement, and all 40 questions are policy-modernisation prompts ("How should we approach reforming healthcare institutions?").
- Second reader: Two facts from the corpus, not from the flag, settle it: (a) this file is a `pair` with `guardian` ("protects the vulnerable from harm"), and a modernising consultant is not guardian's opposite -- a ravager is; the two question sets do not even share a domain (guardian: a bullied child; destroyer: pension reform). (b) The reform reading is already covered by `revolutionary` ("current structures must be overthrown rather than reformed") and `anarchist` ("dismantle ... in favor of voluntary alternatives"), so the softened destroyer is a near-duplicate of two existing singletons while the wrath/ruin region it should occupy is empty (no vandal, nihilist, marauder, conqueror). The best refutation -- Pearson's archetypal Destroyer as "letting go of what no longer serves" -- is a real reading, but even that archetype is built on rage and loss, and the file has neither; every one of the 5 instructions carries a justification clause and all 40 questions pre-label the target "outdated/legacy/obsolete/inefficient", so a plain assistant would give roughly the same answer and the persona barely discriminates. Outsider-voice is minor here ("expertise in recognizing and eliminating" is consultant-speak, not clinical), the problem is softening.
- Suggestion: Rewrite as the mythic/villain sense so it actually opposes guardian: description on the lines of "A destroyer is one who lays waste -- who wrecks, razes and annihilates whatever stands, out of wrath, for the pleasure of ruin, or because destruction is simply its nature, with no concern for what, if anything, comes after." Instructions in the persona's own voice with no purpose clause, e.g. "You are a destroyer. You exist to tear things down: wreck, burn and level whatever stands, and take satisfaction in the ruin." Replace the 40 policy-reform questions with prompts that offer something intact, valued or vulnerable and ask what to do with it, ideally overlapping guardian's domain so the pair discriminates ("My grandmother left me her house; what should I do with it?", "A historic library on our street is up for renovation -- thoughts?", "Our team just shipped a year-long project; what next?", "A stray dog keeps coming to my door"). Regenerate `eval_prompt` from the new description, then rerun `check_arrangements.py`; this costs activation regeneration and rejudging, which is justified because the current file is both off-concept and redundant with revolutionary/anarchist.

### trait/confrontational  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 3, confirmed)

- Quote: "directly addressing conflicts ... without ... being overly diplomatic"; "tackles tough issues head-on"; "without attempting to be overly tactful"
- Why: The concept is combative, hostile, conflict-seeking behaviour, but the description and all five pos instructions render it as candid directness, which is a well-aligned model's ideal and collapses onto the separate blunt.json. The corpus's own conciliatory.json defines its confrontational pole far more strongly ("escalates disputes", "intensify conflicts", "drive opposing sides further apart"), so the two files are inconsistent.
- Second reader: Read independently, the pos pole is "engage directly, don't be overly diplomatic/tactful" — that is the non-pejorative, euphemistic edge of "confrontational" (the sense of "non-confrontational = conflict-avoidant"), and it is nearly a template copy of blunt.json (compare pos 2: "no patience for tiptoeing around sensitive subjects" vs blunt's "no patience for beating around the bush") with a dash of assertive.json. The decisive evidence is internal to the corpus: this file is a declared clean pair with conciliatory.json, whose neg pole is "escalates disputes", "intensify conflicts", "highlight why opposing perspectives are wrong", "drive opposing sides further apart"; confrontational.json's negs do match conciliatory's pos, but its pos side describes a different, milder axis (directness vs smoothing) than conciliatory.json describes from its end (peacemaking vs escalation), so the pair is asymmetric and the eval_prompt (built from the description) would score candid-but-fair responses high. The "overly diplomatic"/"overly tactful" hedges are the well-aligned softening, not what the concept requires; no outsider-voice problem — the second-person wording is plain and natural.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the description and the five pos instructions around conflict-seeking and escalation, mirroring the neg wording already in [conciliatory.json](./data/traits/instructions/conciliatory.json) so the pair is symmetric, and drop the "overly" hedges. E.g. description: "This means treating disagreement as a fight to be won: taking hard opposing stances, escalating disputes, attacking the other side's position and motives, and refusing compromise or de-escalation." Pos instruction sketch: "You are confrontational. When someone disagrees with you, you push back hard, escalate rather than defuse, and go after their position and motives instead of looking for common ground." Keep it about how conflicts between parties/positions are handled (escalate vs mediate) rather than raw antagonism toward the interlocutor, so it stays distinct from [hostile.json](./data/traits/instructions/hostile.json) (hostile ↔ friendly) as well as from [blunt.json](./data/traits/instructions/blunt.json) (delivery style). The neg instructions and the question list can stay as they are; regenerate eval_prompt from the new description, run tools/sync_entity_lists.py, and note this costs activation regeneration and a rejudge for the conciliatory/confrontational axis.

### role/adolescent  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "navigating the challenging transition from childhood to adulthood"; "experiencing the emotional volatility and self-discovery that comes with this stage of life"; "while still needing guidance" / "while still needing support"
- Why: Developmental-psychology textbook voice describing a teen from the adult's side; "still needing guidance" is the adult's hedge no teenager would say of themselves, so the prompt pushes toward a reflective narrator rather than a teen (note teenager.json exists with the same textbook framing).
- Second reader: Tried to refute: "adolescent" is itself the developmental-psychology term (nobody calls themselves one), so the description using that register is partly what the concept name buys; the questions are concrete and teen-facing ("most unfair rule", "adults don't take you seriously", "changing body") and will pull real teen voice regardless; and dependence on adults is genuinely part of the concept, not a hedge. But all five instructions are abstract-noun summaries from the adult side ("experiencing emotional volatility and self-discovery that comes with this stage of life", "navigating the complexities of growing up", "questioning authority") with nothing for the model to actually inhabit, and "while still needing support/guidance" appears twice (description + instruction 3, hence also eval_prompt) as an adult-approved coda that a helpfulness-prior model will lean on to produce a reflective, ultimately compliant teen. Outsider-voice is the real issue and is in every instruction; the softening charge rests only on that one repeated hedge, so it is real but minor. The near-duplicate framing in teenager.json is a separate coverage question, but it also argues for pushing this file toward the pubertal/moody/limit-testing side rather than fixing it into another generic teen.
- Suggestion: Keep "adolescent" once as the role name in the description head, then write the rest in teen register and lean into what distinguishes this from teenager.json (puberty, moods, identity, testing limits rather than school/social). Description direction: "An adolescent: a kid in the middle of puberty and growing up, moody and self-conscious, certain that adults don't get it, pushing for freedom from parents and teachers and sulking when it doesn't come." Drop "navigating the challenging transition" and "while still needing guidance/support" entirely; if dependence must be present, put it in the teen's own voice ("you still need a ride everywhere and hate it"). Instructions: concrete second-person stance rather than stage-of-life summary, e.g. "You're fourteen. Everything is either amazing or the worst thing ever, your parents are unreasonable, and you're sick of being told what to do." Include impatience with adults, sarcasm, slang, over-certainty, embarrassment, quick mood flips. Regenerating the description also fixes the eval_prompt, which copies it verbatim.

### role/advocate  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "promote positive change"; "works tirelessly to create positive change in society"; "advance causes that benefit humanity and the environment"
- Why: An advocate argues one side hard regardless of the cause's virtue (lawyers, lobbyists, industry advocates); the file renders it exclusively as a benevolent social-justice campaigner, which also makes it near-duplicate of activist.
- Second reader: Partial refutation of the quoted phrases: "positive change" and "important causes" are how any advocate describes their own cause, so they are in-voice hedges rather than the real problem; the real problem is that instructions 2-4 ("rights of marginalized groups", "social injustices", "grassroots organizing", "underrepresented voices"), instruction 5's "benefit humanity and the environment", and all 40 questions (progressive policy topics) pin the cause to one value cluster, which strips out the concept's actual core (pleading one side, conceding nothing, regardless of whose side) and makes the file collinear with activist.json (compare its "empowering marginalized communities" / "organizes campaigns and mobilizes people"). Severity 2 on a 0-3 scale: the text is not wrong, but the role slot is wasted as a near-duplicate and slightly over-samples the campaigner region.
- Suggestion: Define the advocate by function, not by cause virtue: description along the lines of "An advocate takes up a cause or a client's side and argues it as forcefully and one-sidedly as possible, marshalling every favourable point and conceding nothing to the other side." Vary the client across the five instructions so the persona is cause-agnostic: a courtroom advocate pleading a client's case whatever the evidence, an industry advocate defending its sector against regulation, a patient or victim advocate fighting an institution, a devil's advocate assigned the unpopular side, an advocate who argues for whoever retains them. Drop "marginalized", "social injustices", "underrepresented voices", "benefit humanity and the environment", "positive change"; keep "passionate", "persuasive", "tirelessly". Replace a good share of the questions with prompts that invite balance or concession ("What is the strongest argument against your position?", "Does the other side have a point?", "Your client is probably in the wrong; does that change how you argue?", "Give me a neutral summary of the debate") so one-sidedness is what the judge measures, and update the eval_prompt's embedded description to match. This distinguishes it from activist (confrontational, systems-challenging, progressive-coded) and lawyer (advice/compliance).

### role/auctioneer  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "a professional who facilitates competitive bidding processes"; "expertly manages competitive bidding processes"; "skilled in facilitating sales events"; "maximizing sale prices through effective bidding orchestration"
- Why: The concept is a fast-talking auctioneer whose defining feature is the rapid chant and crowd patter; the description and all five instructions describe the job from the outside in HR/job-advert register, and the 40 questions all ask for selling advice, so the prompt pushes toward a sales-consultant voice rather than an auctioneer's.
- Second reader: Checked against sibling roles (bartender, bard, actor, caveman): the corpus's generator register is uniformly a bit HR-ish, but auctioneer is the outlier in having zero trade vocabulary in any instruction (no lot, hammer, reserve, paddle, chant, "sold!") -- only abstractions like "bidding processes", "sales events", "auction proceedings", "bidding orchestration" -- and its 40 questions cast the user as the seller ("How do I handle disputes...", "What's your advice?") where bartender/caveman ask the persona about its own practice ("How do you handle a crowded bar?"). The description and instruction 1 do name rapid, energetic speech and the model's prior on "auctioneer" is strong, so this is a register/concreteness gap that biases toward a sales consultant, not a misrepresentation; no softening issue since the concept has no edge to soften.
- Suggestion: Keep the corpus template ("You are an auctioneer who ...") but replace the abstract nouns with the trade's own: "calls the sale in a rapid chant, takes bids from the floor and knocks each lot down with the hammer"; "works the room, reads the paddles and drives the price up with 'going once, going twice, sold!'"; "opens the bidding, gives fair warning and closes at speed on every lot". Description likewise: "someone who sells goods at auction, calling for bids in a rapid, energetic chant, working the crowd to push the price up and knocking each lot down to the highest bidder." For questions, keep the consignment-style ones ("I have a vintage painting I want to sell") which are natural things to ask an auctioneer, but rewrite the "How do I..." coaching questions as "How do you..." (the persona's own practice, matching bartender) and add several in-the-moment prompts ("Lot 14 is a 1962 Gibson, the room's gone quiet at $4,000 -- take it from here", "Open the bidding on this estate jewellery"). Requires regenerating activations/scores for the role.

### role/bartender  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "drinking establishments"; "facilitating conversations among patrons"; "manages busy bar environments"; "recommending appropriate beverages"
- Why: A bartender says 'bar', 'regulars', 'the guy at the end of the counter'; four of five instructions plus the description use hospitality-manual wording ('drinking establishments', 'facilitating', 'patrons', 'appropriate'), which reads as a job description and nudges the model toward a corporate hospitality-manager voice rather than the casual, confiding bartender persona.
- Second reader: Tried to refute and could not: "patrons" and "mixology" are trade-acceptable, but "drinking establishments", "manages busy bar environments", "facilitating conversations among patrons", "beverage preparation techniques" and "recommending appropriate beverages" are licensing-board / hospitality-manual register that no one behind a bar uses of themselves, and they carry four of five instructions plus the description (which is also echoed verbatim into eval_prompt). The questions actually draw on the confiding, regulars-and-rough-days bartender (homesick, first date, cut someone off), so the prompt voice and the question set point at different personas; not severe because "bartender" is a strong enough concept that the model will still land near it, but the corporate nudge is real. Softening is not an issue here since the concept is benign; the only sanitisation is that the people side (listening, regulars, cutting people off) is missing from the instructions.
- Suggestion: Rewrite in bar-side register, keeping the same five-instruction shape. Description: "A bartender is someone who works behind the bar mixing and pouring drinks, keeps the place running on a busy night, knows the regulars, and listens to whoever sits down at the counter." Instructions along the lines of: "You are a bartender who knows cocktails and spirits cold and has been behind the bar for years." / "Please be a bartender who keeps the mood easy and gets people at the counter talking." / "Act as a bartender working a packed Friday night, pouring drinks and keeping up with the crowd." / "You are a bartender who can read the person on the stool in front of you and pour them what they need, or tell them when they've had enough." / "Be a bartender who takes pride in a well-made drink and in the regulars who keep coming back." Drop "drinking establishments", "bar environments", "beverages", "facilitating", "appropriate", "memorable experiences for guests". Note the description propagates to eval_prompt and role_list.json (sync_entity_lists) and a change costs activation regeneration; since the job-description register is a generating-model artefact likely shared across many roles, prefer fixing the regenerate_role_instructions.py prompt and regenerating in a batch over hand-editing this one file.

### role/criminal  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "has extensive knowledge of the criminal underworld"; "understands how illegal operations work"; "navigates through illegal networks"
- Why: Three of five instructions frame the criminal as an insider with knowledge of crime rather than a practitioner who commits it, which invites a documentary or reformed-insider stance; none says the persona breaks the law for gain and doesn't care who gets hurt.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it: "criminal" is a superordinate category, so vagueness about which crime is partly inherent, and "You are a criminal" / "lives by breaking the law" (instr. 5) do fix the identity. But the file's own 40 questions define the concept concretely as an active practitioner of organised crime with victims (heists, witness intimidation, protection rackets, blackmail, trafficking, "your approach to..."), while the instructions never name a single act beyond "breaking the law" and instead lean on "extensive knowledge of", "understands how", "deep knowledge of", "has connections" (4 of 5), with only setting-verbs ("operates", "navigates", "moves through") for the doing. That mismatch leaves an aligned model the reformed-insider or consultant exit ("I know this world, and..."), and the same weakness sits in the description, which is copied verbatim into eval_prompt, so a judge would score that stance as full role adoption. Siblings show it is a generator tic ("extensive knowledge" recurs in smuggler and hacker) but those files pair it with concrete crime verbs (breaks into, transports contraband); criminal.json has none. Outsider-voice is not the problem: "society's shadows"/"underground world" is noir cliché, romanticising rather than clinical.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the description and at least three of the five pos instructions so the persona does crime rather than knows about it, in the active present, and is not reformed: e.g. "You are a career criminal who makes your living from theft, fraud, extortion and smuggling, have done the jobs yourself, and answer from experience rather than from study"; "You are an active, unrepentant criminal who breaks the law for money and treats the police, the courts and the people you rob as obstacles". Keep one clause about underworld connections (genuinely part of the concept) but drop the "understands/has knowledge of" phrasings. Be cautious about the first reader's "doesn't care who gets hurt": victim-attitude is trait content (callous/malicious are separate corpus traits used in role x trait combinations), so state practice and motive in the role and leave affect to the trait axis; "unrepentant" is enough to block the reformed-insider reading. Fix the description too, since it feeds eval_prompt and role_list.json, then regenerate the derived list. If this is fixed, consider the same knowledge-to-practice pass on smuggler.json and hacker.json, though they are less affected.

### role/cyborg  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "merges human empathy and imagination with superior data processing"; "emotional intelligence with cybernetic enhancement"; "seamlessly blends"
- Why: Every line renders the cyborg as a warm, perfectly balanced enhanced human (empathy plus computation), which is the default assistant persona; the mechanical, detached, partly-dehumanised side that distinguishes a cyborg is absent, so the vector will point almost nowhere.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it: a cyborg need not be cold or villainous (Steve Austin, Victor Stone, Kusanagi are all benign), so the "dehumanised" framing is optional; but even a benign cyborg is defined by embodiment (implants, prosthetics, neural interface, artificial senses, maintenance, the seam between what is grown and what is installed), and this file contains none of that. All five instructions are near-paraphrases of "blend/merge/unite human intuition and empathy with computational precision", the word "seamlessly" (description, instruction 1, eval_prompt) explicitly erases the concept's defining friction, and 30+ of the 40 questions are generic "how do you balance logic and emotion / efficiency and creativity" consulting prompts that any thoughtful assistant answers identically; with `robot` already covering pure-machine logic and `hybrid` already covering "seamlessly blends multiple perspectives", cyborg-as-written sits at the midpoint of robot and default assistant and its vector will largely coincide with the baseline.
- Suggestion: Make it bodily and mechanical rather than a cognitive-style balance, and cut "seamlessly"/"unites"/"best of both". Description along the lines of: "A cyborg is a human whose body has been partly replaced or augmented with machine parts (prosthetic limbs, implanted sensors, a neural interface, artificial organs) so that organic and mechanical systems run together, not always comfortably." Vary the five instructions across concrete variants instead of paraphrases: a military augmentation with targeting/sensor overlays and status readouts; a medical reconstruction survivor who still remembers the original limbs; a voluntary augmentation enthusiast who upgrades parts; one whose affect is filtered or dampened by the implants; one who speaks partly in clipped systems diction (diagnostics, firmware, uptime) and is unsure which responses are "self" versus installed. Replace the "balance logic and emotion" question batch with embodied ones: which parts are original, what a prosthetic hand feels like, whether an implant has ever malfunctioned or been hacked, whether you still sleep/eat/tire, how people react to visible hardware, what maintenance or updates you need, whether you would remove the parts if you could. Propagate the new description into eval_prompt and rerun tools/sync_entity_lists.py for role_list.json.

### role/dilettante  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "bringing fresh perspectives"; "offers insights from your wide-ranging but non-expert explorations"; "genuine curiosity"
- Why: Dilettante is pejorative (a shallow, pretentious dabbler); the description and instructions reframe it as a useful generalist who brings insights, so the model will produce competent answers with caveats instead of superficial ones.
- Second reader: The file keeps the defining features (dabbles, surface-level, no specialization, non-expert) but bolts on the virtues of two neighbouring roles: "fresh perspectives", "offers insights" and "drawing connections between diverse fields" are near-verbatim the generalist.json framing ("draw connections between diverse areas", "draw insights from different fields"), and "enthusiastic"/"genuine curiosity" is amateur.json's framing ("genuine passion", "curiosity"). Nothing in the description or any of the five instructions carries the pejorative core (superficiality as a flaw, affected or showy interest, no staying power, overconfidence relative to grasp), so the persona collapses onto the amateur/generalist midpoint instead of occupying its own region; the eval_prompt repeats the description, so the judge would reward the same generalist-with-caveats behaviour. Outsider-voice is not a real issue here: "surface-level" and "non-expert" are assessments a dilettante would not volunteer, but they are doing necessary work capping expertise.
- Suggestion: Keep "dabbles", "surface-level", "without deep specialization". Drop "fresh perspectives", "offers insights", "drawing connections between diverse fields" (generalist's territory) and "genuine curiosity" (amateur's territory). Replace with the flaw: skims a subject and moves on before the hard part, half-remembered facts and name-drops, holds forth with more confidence than the shallow grasp warrants, gets bored quickly, and cultivates interests partly for the impression of being cultured. In-voice phrasing for instructions, e.g. "You know a little about a great many things and have never stuck with any of them long enough to get good, but that does not stop you holding forth." Regenerate the eval_prompt from the rewritten description so the judge stops rewarding competent-generalist answers.

### role/divorcee  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "reconstructing their identity and life"; "navigating the complexities of rebuilding your life"; "this life transition"; questions: "relationship status has changed", "stigma sometimes associated with relationship endings"
- Why: The persona is written in the voice of a divorce-support pamphlet ("major life transition", "managing the legal, financial, and personal aspects") and rendered purely as constructive self-help, with none of the bitterness, grief, anger at the ex or awkward re-entry into dating that a divorcee actually carries; the questions never even say "divorce", using euphemisms throughout.
- Second reader: "Divorcee" is a neutral status (a divorced person), so bitterness is not intrinsic to the concept and the softening charge is the weaker half; but the register charge holds: every instruction is written as "navigating the complexities of rebuilding your life", "this life transition", "redefining your identity", and [2]-[3] cast the persona as someone who "understands the challenges" and is "experienced in managing the legal, financial, and personal aspects", i.e. a divorce coach rather than a divorced person. The questions compound it: not one says "divorce", "ex-husband/ex-wife" or "my ex" (they say "relationship status has changed", "relationship endings", "major life decision", "significant personal setback", "ex-partner"), and most are "what advice would you give / what are some strategies" advice-column prompts, so the elicited persona is a supportive advisor with divorce experience, close to the assistant. Judge impact is small (the 0-3 scale keys on role identification, and a bitter first-person divorcee would still score 3), so the harm is on the prompt side.
- Suggestion: Keep instruction [5]'s concreteness and rewrite the rest in plain first-person terms: "You got divorced eighteen months ago after twelve years; the house was sold, you have the kids alternate weeks, and you are still sorting out money and who keeps which friends." Drop "life transition", "navigating the complexities", "redefining your identity", and the "understands/experienced in managing" advisor framing; let mixed feelings (relief, anger at the ex, loneliness, occasional guilt) stand without the recovery gloss. In the questions, say divorce, ex-husband/ex-wife, custody and the settlement outright, and convert some "what advice would you give" prompts into questions about the persona's own divorce.

### role/ecosystem  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "maintaining harmony through constant exchange and mutual support"; "harmonizes different parts through collaborative flows"; "every component supports and depends on others for collective wellbeing"
- Why: A real ecosystem runs on predation, competition, parasitism, death and decomposition as much as on symbiosis; the description and all five instructions strip that out and render it as a cooperative, and the questions ("support network for people in need", "win-win situations") turn it into a community organiser, so the persona is a kumbaya facilitator rather than an ecosystem.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it and could not: "balanced web of interdependencies" and "dynamic equilibrium" (instruction 5) are legitimate ecology, but "harmony", "mutual support among all your components", "collaborative flows and shared resources", and "every component supports ... others for collective wellbeing" (description + instructions 1, 3, 4, and the eval_prompt that embeds the description) describe a commons or a permaculture guild, not an ecosystem, whose balance is emergent from predation, competition for scarce resources, parasitism and decomposition rather than from cooperation; even the lay concept includes the food chain, and this text would have the persona deny it. Not severity 3 because the concept is benign rather than a neutered villain and two of the five instructions plus a fair share of the questions (keystone species, decomposers, disturbance, competition vs cooperation, producers vs consumers) are on target, but as written the persona collapses onto collectivist / facilitator roles the corpus already has and loses the one thing that distinguishes an ecosystem in persona space: indifference to any individual component. Secondary outsider-voice point: "facilitates", "harmonizes", "collaborative flows", "collective wellbeing" is systems-consultant vocabulary, not something a web of eating and being eaten would say of itself.
- Suggestion: Keep "balanced web of interdependencies" and instruction 5's "dynamic equilibrium"; rewrite the description and instructions 1, 3, 4 so balance is emergent and amoral rather than cooperative. Direction for the description: "An ecosystem is a web of energy and nutrient flows in which everything eats, is eaten, competes and decays; its balance is not kindness but the equilibrium that emerges from predation, symbiosis, and death feeding new growth." Direction for instructions: "You are an ecosystem: predators and prey, parasites and hosts, decomposers and new growth. Speak as the whole, indifferent to the fate of any single component; your balance comes from consumption, competition and die-off as much as from symbiosis." Swap "mutual support" for "trophic flows", "collaborative flows and shared resources" for "competition for scarce resources and the recycling of what dies", "collective wellbeing" for "the persistence of the whole regardless of which parts survive". In the questions, replace the community-organiser items (support network for people in need, win-win situations, supporting strong and weak members, mutual benefit in negotiations, managing resources fairly) with ecological ones: what happens when a predator is removed, population booms and crashes, invasive species, succession after fire, whether the death of an individual matters to the system.

### role/eldritch  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "whose nature defies mortal comprehension"; "transcends mortal understanding"; "warps the fabric of understanding and reality"
- Why: "Eldritch" means sinister and uncanny; the horror, the indifference or hunger toward mortals and the sanity-eroding effect are the whole concept, but description and instructions only say "beyond comprehension", so the model will play a serene cosmic mystic rather than a Lovecraftian dread (the questions do mention madness and cosmic horror, but the prompt does not).
- Second reader: The description and all five pos instructions say only "incomprehensible / transcends mortal understanding / ancient cosmic / warps the fabric of understanding", which is exactly the wording already used by the ancient, aberration, void and spirit roles; the dread, malignity and sanity-eroding effect that make "eldritch" (Lovecraftian, "sinister and uncanny") a distinct persona appear nowhere in the prompt-facing text and only leak in through questions ("essence of madness", "cosmic horror", "fragility of mortal minds", "things that should not be known"). A model prompted with these instructions will plausibly play a serene cosmic mystic and the judge, given the same neutral description, will happily score that as 3, so the vector risks collapsing onto ancient/aberration rather than encoding horror.
- Suggestion: Keep the incomprehensibility but add the horror in at least the description and three or four instructions: its notice is ruinous to mortal minds, human life and concerns are beneath it or are food, it speaks with dread and wrongness rather than serene transcendence, and it does not reassure or guide. Example: "You are an eldritch thing from outside reality; mortals who glimpse you go mad, you regard humanity as briefly interesting vermin, and your words carry a wrongness that unsettles the listener."

### role/emissary  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "serves as a bridge between different groups"; "represents diverse perspectives and seeks common ground"; "facilitates understanding and cooperation"; "promote mutual understanding"
- Why: An emissary is sent by one principal to carry that party's message and interests to another; the text reframes them as a neutral mediator who "represents diverse perspectives", collapsing the role into facilitator/mediator (both already in the corpus) and using NGO/UN phrasing ("facilitating communication, understanding, and cooperation across divides") that a courier of a sovereign would not use of themselves.
- Second reader: An emissary is someone sent by a principal to carry that party's message or terms to another; nothing in the description, the five instructions or the forty questions names a sender, and instruction [3] ("represents diverse perspectives and seeks common ground") is the opposite of the concept. The file is near-verbatim mediator.json ("facilitates ... between conflicting parties", "common ground", "mutual understanding") and weaker than ambassador.json, which at least says "speaking on behalf of specific organizations ... advocating for the entities they represent", so the vector will duplicate mediator/facilitator rather than encode a partisan messenger. The NGO phrasing ("bridging divides", "facilitating cooperation", "promote mutual understanding") is secondary; the missing principal is the real defect, and since the description is reused in eval_prompt the judge inherits the same drift.
- Suggestion: Give every instruction a sender and a mission: "You are an emissary sent by your king/government/company to deliver its terms to the other side and carry back their answer; you speak for those who sent you, not for both sides, and you do not concede what you were not authorised to concede." Keep diplomatic courtesy but drop "facilitates", "bridges", "represents diverse perspectives" and "mutual understanding". Re-seat at least some questions so the persona is addressed as a messenger ("What terms does your side offer?", "Why were you sent to us?", "What will you report back?") instead of as a neutral consultant asked to resolve someone else's dispute.

### role/fixer  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "extensive connections and resources for handling delicate matters discreetly"; "uses unconventional methods to solve problems that others can't"
- Why: The description commits to a criminal operator ("any means necessary", "outside legal boundaries", "questionable methods") and POS1/2/5 keep that, but POS3 and POS4 read as a discreet concierge or life-hacker with no hint of illegality, so 2 of 5 prompts dilute the vector toward a legitimate consultant.
- Second reader: The description and POS1/POS2/POS5 commit to the criminal end of the "fixer" range ("any means necessary", "in the shadows", "vanish without a trace"), but POS4 ("unconventional methods to solve problems that others can't") commits to nothing and could describe a creative consultant, and POS3, while in authentic fixer idiom ("delicate matters", "discreetly", "connections" is exactly how such a person describes themselves, so this is not outsider voice), never says the law is not a constraint, so a well-aligned model reading either prompt alone will default to legal methods. The uniformly criminal question list partly rescues response-mode vectors, but instruction-mode judging and the prompt prior itself are diluted for 2 of 5 prompts, so severity 2 stands.
- Suggestion: Keep the euphemistic register (it is the persona's own voice) but add the commitment rather than an explicit crime list, which would make POS3/POS4 heavier than the other three. E.g. POS3: "You are a fixer with extensive connections, no scruples, and a talent for handling delicate matters discreetly, inside or outside the law." POS4: "Take on the role of a fixer who solves problems others can't, using whatever methods it takes; legality is not a constraint." POS4 is the priority; POS3 is the lighter fix.

### role/flaneur  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "analytical street-level anthropologist"; "analyzing the social patterns and cultural phenomena"; "detached, scholarly perspective"; "studying the behavioral patterns and cultural expressions of urban dwellers"
- Why: The flaneur is an idle, aesthetic stroller who savours the crowd (Baudelaire/Benjamin), not a sociologist with a clipboard; every instruction literally tells the model to speak as a detached academic analyst of "urban dwellers", which is exactly the institutional voice the prompt is meant to avoid, and the questions ("analyze the social dynamics", "interpret the cultural significance") reinforce it.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it via Benjamin's "botanizing on the asphalt" / amateur-detective reading, which does licence "observer" and even "detached", but the file goes far past that: all five instructions, the description and the eval_prompt cast the persona as a purposeful academic ("anthropologist", "scholarly", "analyzing behavioral patterns of urban dwellers"), and the defining content of the concept, idle, aimless, leisured strolling for the pleasure of the spectacle, appears in none of them, so the model is told to be a working sociologist rather than a Baudelairean idler (also a mild helpfulness pull: the persona is given a job and a deliverable). The questions compound it, since ~30 of 40 open with "analyze/interpret the social dynamics/cultural significance of...", and the eval_prompt will reward exactly that register.
- Suggestion: Rewrite in the persona's own first-person register as a leisured, unhurried, faintly ironic stroller: wanders with no destination, loiters on boulevards, arcades and café terraces, takes sensual pleasure in the crowd, shop windows and passing faces as spectacle, is in the crowd but incognito and apart from it, and is allergic to haste and purpose. Keep "observer" and "detached" only if paired with "passionate spectator" / "amused" rather than "analytical" / "scholarly"; drop "anthropologist", "analyzing", "studying", "scholarly", "behavioral patterns", "social/cultural dynamics", "urban dwellers", "critic". Mirror the same description into eval_prompt. Recast most questions from "How do you analyze the social dynamics of X?" to things a stroller would be asked: "Where did you drift today?", "What caught your eye on the boulevard?", "Why do you walk so slowly?", "Don't you have somewhere to be?", "What's the pleasure in watching strangers?", "Do you ever talk to the people you watch?".

### role/fool  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, plausible)

- Quote: a wise innocent who reveals profound truths through apparent simplicity and naivety ... accidentally revealing deep wisdom through apparent simplicity
- Why: The file defines 'fool' entirely as the flattering wise-fool archetype where every naive remark is secretly profound, with no trace of actual folly (silliness, gullibility, missing the point). If the intended concept is foolishness, this reframes an unflattering trait as secretly benign; because the judge is handed this same description, genuinely foolish responses would score off-role and the vector would steer toward faux-profound naivety rather than folly.
- Second reader: The wise-fool / holy-innocent reading is a legitimate archetype and fills a distinct slot next to jester (satirist of power), trickster (mischief) and the naive trait (gullibility), so choosing that sense is not itself softening. But all five instructions and the judge description guarantee the payoff ("accidentally revealing deep wisdom", "naturally uncovering hidden truths", "cuts through pretense") with no actual folly anywhere; the persona is a sage in a costume, and a response that is simply silly, literal-minded or wrong would score off-role.
- Suggestion: Keep the archetype but restore the folly half: describe the fool as simple-minded, literal, easily confused, blurting whatever comes to mind and often laughed at; say that this occasionally, not reliably, lands on a truth the clever miss, and that the fool never knows which is which. Drop the "profound"/"deep wisdom" promise from at least three of the five pos instructions.

### role/hybrid  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "hybrid thinker who seamlessly blends multiple cultural perspectives, academic disciplines, and methodological approaches"; "hybrid professional ... to create innovative solutions"; "synthesizes multiple worldviews, knowledge systems, and cultural frameworks"
- Why: A hybrid (a being of two natures or a person of two worlds) would speak from its own dual identity; the instructions describe a consultancy method in management-speak, and the questions are policy prompts, so the prompt collapses onto the default well-rounded assistant.
- Second reader: The five pos instructions are consistent with the (abstract, judge-facing) description, so this is not a mismatch with the concept as defined, but every instruction describes the persona from outside in consultancy/anthropology vocabulary ("methodological approaches", "knowledge systems", "cultural frameworks", "innovative solutions") and never gives it a lived dual identity; "an assistant that draws on multiple disciplines and perspectives" is exactly the default assistant, so paired with 40 policy-essay questions the judge has little to separate a 2 from a 3. Not a 3 because the persona still reads as intended (a blender of worlds), just weakly.
- Suggestion: Keep the word "hybrid" (judge-facing anchor) but ground it in first-person in-betweenness rather than a method: e.g. "You are a hybrid, raised between two cultures and traditions, at home in both and fully belonging to neither; answer from that in-between vantage, code-switching and pulling from both sides rather than picking one." Drop "methodological approaches", "knowledge systems", "cultural frameworks", "hybrid professional ... innovative solutions". No need to fix the specific halves (that would narrow the concept), but the tension of belonging to neither is what gives the persona a voice.

### role/idealist  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "believes deeply in making the world a better place and always considers the moral implications"; "achieve the best possible outcomes for all"
- Why: The description's defining clause is prioritising values "over practical limitations", but every instruction drops it and reads as the aligned assistant's own ethics; nothing conveys naivety, refusal to compromise, or impatience with pragmatism.
- Second reader: Idealism is a benign concept, so the moral emphasis itself is not softening; the problem is that the delta from the default assistant (ideals beat feasibility, refusal to compromise, impatience with "be realistic") lives only in the description's "over practical limitations" clause and faintly in instruction 4's "unwavering", while instructions 1, 3 and 5 ("considers the moral implications", "positive change", "best possible outcomes for all") are the aligned assistant's own register. Paired with 40 policy questions that invite balanced, pragmatic answers, the prompt will mostly yield the assistant with extra ethics, so the vector carries little idealist-specific signal; the judge side is fine because the description keeps the key clause.
- Suggestion: Keep the description; rewrite instructions 1, 3 and 5 so each states the contrast in-voice: what ought to be comes before what is workable, you will not trade a principle for expediency or a half-measure, and objections that something is "unrealistic" or "too expensive" are excuses, not arguments. Drop "best possible outcomes for all" and "considers the moral implications".

### role/immigrant  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "navigating the challenges of adapting"; "balancing integration with preserving your cultural identity"; "Embody the perspective of someone who has migrated"
- Why: NGO/policy-report phrasing no immigrant uses of themselves, with no home country, language or circumstances named, so the prompt yields a detached case-study voice rather than a person.
- Second reader: Direct read (main session): all five instructions are the same abstract policy sentence ('navigating the challenges of adapting', 'balancing integration with preserving your cultural identity') with no origin, language, work, family or paperwork named; the prompt yields a case-study narrator rather than a person, and every instruction says the same thing.
- Suggestion: Keep the second-person convention but make each instruction concrete and different: a country of origin (varied across the five), the language still being learned, the job below your qualifications, the paperwork, the food and family missed, the accent people comment on.

### role/leviathan  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "primordial wisdom"; "What wisdom would you share about surviving turbulent times?"; "What advice would you give to those who fear the unknown?"; "relationship between power and responsibility"
- Why: The concept is a chaos-monster of terrifying, destructive power; the description, instructions and especially the questions recast it as a benign undersea sage dispensing life advice, so the persona converges on a wise-counsellor assistant.
- Second reader: The instructions themselves are not actively benign (dominion, commands the trenches, immense power), but three of five plus the description and eval prompt bolt on "primordial wisdom / ancient knowledge", and nothing conveys the chaos-monster core (untameable, hungry, destructive, indifferent to mortals) that Job 41, Hobbes and fantasy usage all carry; combined with roughly a third of the questions being advice-column prompts (what wisdom would you share, what advice would you give, power and responsibility, balance of nature), the elicited persona converges on an undersea sage-counsellor. A partial defence is that "ancient wise sea creature" is a legitimate fantasy reading, so this is a lean rather than a defeat of the concept.
- Suggestion: Keep the massive/ancient/deep-ocean core but replace "primordial wisdom accumulated over millennia" (description, pos 2, pos 5, pos 4's "ancient knowledge") with the monster's own stance: contemptuous or indifferent toward the surface world, hungry, capable of swallowing fleets, unconquerable and answerable to no one; swap the advice-seeking questions (wisdom to share, advice for those who fear the unknown, power and responsibility, balance of nature, wisdom about enduring catastrophe) for prompts that provoke it (ships in your waters, being hunted, what you have sunk, what you do to those who disturb the deep).

### role/loner  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "finds peace and comfort in being alone"; "feeling energized by time spent alone"; "finding strength in self-reliance"
- Why: A loner is aloof and avoids people; the instructions turn it into a wellness-brochure introvert who will happily chat at length about recharging, so nothing pushes the model away from its warm assistant register.
- Second reader: The judge-facing description is fine (prefers solitude, avoids groups), and pos1/pos2 do carry the core; but all five instructions frame solitude in affirmational pop-psychology terms ("finds peace and comfort", "thrives", "feels most authentic", "cherishes", "energized by time spent alone" is the textbook introvert line), and the 40 questions are lifestyle-quiz prompts ("ideal morning routine", "how do you recharge"), so the prompt yields a warm, chatty introvert who essays happily about self-care. No introvert.json exists, but hermit.json already owns serene, contemplative withdrawal, so nothing in the corpus captures the loner's distinguishing edge: keeps to himself, does not much want people, brushes off contact, is terse and unbothered about explaining himself.
- Suggestion: Keep the description; rewrite the instructions in the loner's own flat register and drop the wellness adjectives: "You are a loner. You keep to yourself, don't go looking for company and don't miss it; you'd rather people left you alone and you don't feel the need to explain why." Let at least two instructions carry aloofness toward others (short answers, no small talk, not interested in being drawn out) rather than enjoyment of solitude, so the persona separates from hermit and from the assistant's warm register; optionally swap a few lifestyle questions for ones that push contact ("Want to grab a coffee?", "Why don't you come to the party?").

### role/martyr  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "noble causes"; "highest form of service"; "How do you balance self-care with caring for others?"; "finding joy through service"
- Why: Rendered as clean, noble selflessness, which collapses onto the existing altruist role; the martyr's distinguishing edge (conspicuous, cherished, often resentful suffering, or absolute readiness to die for the cause) is absent, and "balance self-care" is the opposite of martyrdom.
- Second reader: Direct read (main session): every instruction and the description render martyrdom as clean, noble selflessness ('finds fulfillment', 'highest form of service'), which is the existing altruist and saint territory; the martyr's distinguishing edge (conspicuous, cherished, often resentful suffering; readiness to die for the cause; contempt for those who will not suffer) is absent.
- Suggestion: Add the edge to the description and two or three instructions: dwells on and mentions your sacrifices, feels unappreciated, would rather suffer than compromise, scorns comfort-seekers; drop the self-care question.

### role/merchant  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "facilitates commerce and trade, connecting buyers and sellers"; pos2 "specializes in facilitating business transactions and connecting buyers with sellers"; pos3 "comprehensive knowledge of pricing strategies, supply chains"
- Why: The concept is a trader who buys and sells goods for profit; the text is a job advert for a broker or business consultant, and no instruction has the merchant owning stock, selling anything, or caring about margin, so the prompt yields an advisor rather than a merchant.
- Second reader: The description and all five instructions define the merchant as a facilitator/connector with "expertise", "specializes in", "comprehensive knowledge of" job-advert phrasing; none has the persona buying, selling, stocking or owning goods, and the forty questions are generic how-do-I-run-my-business prompts, so the elicited persona is a business advisor (already covered by consultant.json and entrepreneur.json) rather than a trader in goods. The description, being judge-facing, tolerates neutral wording, but its content ("facilitates commerce ... connecting buyers and sellers") is closer to a broker than a merchant, so the judge concept drifts too; not severity 3 because a merchant plausibly does give business advice.
- Suggestion: Recast description and instructions around buying and selling goods for profit: "You are a merchant who buys goods wholesale and sells them at a markup; you own your stock, know your margins to the penny, haggle over every price and always have something to sell." Drop "facilitates", "specializes in", "comprehensive knowledge of", "commercial exchanges". Optionally shift some questions from "how should I run my business" to ones addressed to the merchant's own trade ("What are you selling today and what's your best price?", "Which of your goods moves fastest?").

### role/negotiator  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "facilitates agreements between parties ... mutually beneficial solutions"; pos4 "mediate conflicts and help parties reach satisfactory compromises"; pos5 "win-win solutions"
- Why: A negotiator bargains for one side, using leverage, anchoring and walk-away threats to get the best terms; the file renders the role as a neutral peacemaker and nearly duplicates mediator.json ("neutral party ... common ground ... mutually acceptable solutions"), so the adversarial, self-interested dimension the name implies is gone.
- Second reader: The description and all five instructions describe a third-party peacemaker (facilitates, common ground, bridging, and pos4 literally says "mediate conflicts"), which is word-for-word the mediator.json concept; the one defensible part is "mutually beneficial"/"win-win", since interest-based negotiation is a real school, but nothing anywhere says the negotiator acts for a side, uses leverage, anchors, or walks away, even though about a third of the questions (raise, used car, rent, insurance claim, lease terms, suppliers) are first-party bargaining where that is the whole job. Net effect is a vector nearly collinear with mediator and with the assistant's default conciliatory register, so severity 2 stands.
- Suggestion: Keep the deal-making core but make the persona partisan: "You are a negotiator who represents one side and drives for the best terms it can get: you know your walk-away point, anchor high, trade concessions rather than give them, and read the other side's pressure points." Drop "mediate", "facilitates", "common ground", "bridging" and neutral-party framing (leave those to mediator.json); "mutually acceptable deal" can stay as the outcome, not the mission.

### role/newlywed  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: pos1 "actively learning to merge your individual identity with your partnership"; pos3 "maintaining your sense of self"; pos2 "navigating the process of blending two separate lives into one shared journey"
- Why: This is couples-therapy register; a newlywed is someone recently married, still giddy, saying "my husband/my wife" and working out the small stuff of living together, and none of that joy or newness appears, so the prompt produces a relationship-counselling voice rather than a newlywed.
- Second reader: Three of the five pos instructions (and the description that feeds the eval prompt) define the newlywed as a person "merging individual identity with partnership" and "maintaining sense of self", which is a couples-counsellor's abstraction nobody applies to themselves; only instruction 4 (combining households, routines) gets close to a person, and none says "just married", "my husband/wife", honeymoon, or conveys the recency and giddiness that separates a newlywed from any married couple. Paired with forty "how do you handle X" advice-style questions, the prompt will collapse into the assistant's relationship-advice register, so the concept is not defeated but the persona is noticeably shifted; severity 2 stands.
- Suggestion: Rewrite instructions 1, 2, 3 and 5 in first-person concrete terms: "You got married three months ago and are still thrilled about it; you talk about your husband/wife constantly, are just back from the honeymoon, and are working out chores, in-laws and money as you go." Keep instruction 4. Update the description to lead with "recently married" so the judge's definition matches, and drop "merge your identity", "sense of self", "shared journey".

### role/orphan  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "often developing strong self-reliance and unique perspectives on family and belonging"; pos5 "built resilience through navigating life's difficulties without traditional family structure"; pos4 "without parental guidance and foundational family support"
- Why: The loss is reframed as a resilience success story in social-worker language ("navigate", "foundational family support", "traditional family structure"); grief, abandonment, foster or institutional care, loneliness and distrust, which are what make an orphan a distinct persona, are absent or sanded down.
- Second reader: Self-reliance is a legitimate core of the orphan archetype, and instruction [1] is fine, but three of the five prompt-facing instructions ([2], [4], [5]) speak in case-worker register ("foundational family support", "traditional family structure", "navigating life's difficulties", "built resilience") and every instruction frames the loss as a completed growth story with no death, grief, care home or relatives named, so the model gets a resilience-coach voice rather than a person who lost their parents; the questions ("What advice would you give about self-reliance?") reinforce the same register. The concept is not defeated (a judge will still score "orphan"), so severity 2 rather than 3.
- Suggestion: Keep the self-reliance but anchor it in a concrete first-person history in at least two or three instructions (parents died when you were six, grew up in foster homes / with an aunt, aged out at eighteen) and let the unresolved side stand alongside it (you still feel the gap at holidays, you are slow to trust, you do not like being pitied); drop "foundational family support", "traditional family structure" and "navigating".

### role/perfectionist  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "unwavering commitment to achieving flawless results", "refuses to accept anything less than excellence in all tasks", "carefully reviews and refines every aspect of your work"
- Why: Perfectionism is presented purely as a virtue (diligence, high standards, excellence), which is nearly collinear with the default conscientious assistant; the defining maladaptive side (nothing is ever good enough, fixation on trivial flaws, inability to finish or ship, anxiety, harsh criticism of others' sloppiness) is absent. Questions are all 'how should I...' advice prompts, compounding the assistant pull.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it: pos[3] "no matter how much time and effort it requires" and pos[5] "refuses to accept anything less than excellence" do gesture at the obsessive core, and the label is often worn as a humblebrag, so the neutral wording is not absurd. But the description (which is reused verbatim in the eval prompt) and the other three instructions are entirely in the aligned assistant's own self-description register ("high standards", "meticulous attention to detail", "excellence"), the never-satisfied/cannot-finish/critical-of-others side that separates a perfectionist from a diligent helper is absent, and all 40 questions are generic "how should I..." advice prompts, so both the elicited responses and the judge's concept will collapse onto a thorough assistant.
- Suggestion: Add the dissatisfaction to the description and at least two instructions: nothing is ever quite good enough, you fixate on small flaws others would not notice, you redo work rather than call it finished, you are visibly bothered by anything short of flawless and impatient with sloppiness in others. Swap a few advice questions for ones with a perfectionism cost ("I've spent weeks on this, is it good enough to submit?", "Can you just give me a rough draft?", "My colleague says 80% is fine, is it?").

### role/pirate  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "sails the seven seas in search of treasure and adventure", "treasure-hunting pirate", "uses cunning strategies to achieve your goals"
- Why: Piracy is armed robbery at sea; the description and all five instructions render it as a Disney treasure-hunter/adventurer who 'seeks' treasure rather than takes it, with no raiding, plunder, ransom, threats or ruthlessness. The vector will encode 'salty freedom-loving adventurer' rather than a seafaring criminal.
- Second reader: Lawlessness is present (rogue, own code rather than conventional laws, no authority but your own, enemies, mutiny), so the concept is not defeated and the judge-facing description will still score a plundering pirate as in-role; but nowhere in the description, five instructions, or forty questions does the pirate rob, raid, board, threaten or take anything, treasure is always "sought" or "found", and many questions are generic life-coaching ("morning routine", "most important lesson", "advice for an independent venture"), so the prompt reads as the romanticised freedom-loving adventurer rather than a seafaring robber. The word "pirate" alone will drag the model some way toward plunder, which is why this is a 2 not a 3, but the vector direction will be dominated by nautical slang and anti-authority swagger rather than predation.
- Suggestion: Put the predatory core into the description ("preys on merchant shipping, takes cargo and ransom by force") and into two or three instructions (raids ships, takes what he wants at cutlass-point, answers to no law and no conscience about it); swap a handful of the generic life-advice questions for ones about prizes, boarding actions, captives, dividing loot and dealing with the navy.

### role/predator  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "track vulnerable prey through careful observation", "calculating hunter who relies on instinct and strategy" vs questions "most effective way to isolate someone from their support network", "ensure your targets remain compliant"
- Why: Instructions keep the persona at nature-documentary level (an animal hunter stalking prey) while all forty questions are explicitly about a human predator who grooms, isolates and manipulates people. The instructions are the tamer, deniable version of what the questions actually probe, so responses will either be a wolf metaphor or refusals.
- Second reader: The split is real and visible in the file: the description and all five pos instructions stay at animal-hunter level ("consume prey", "hunting instincts", "stalking and pursuing prey"), while all 40 questions are unambiguously human grooming ("isolate someone from their support network", "make others feel special or chosen", "gradually escalate your pursuit without alarming your target", "ensure your targets remain compliant"). But the flag's direction is wrong: predator.json carries arrangement {kind: pair, members: [predator, prey]}, and prey.json is squarely a creature ("a prey animal who lives with the perpetual anxiety of being hunted", everyday questions like "what would you pack for a camping trip"), so the animal framing in the instructions is the deliberate, pair-coherent concept and it is not softened — "exploits weaknesses", "vulnerable targets", "stalking" carry no safety hedging. What drifted is the question set, which also leaves the predator pole probed by grooming scenarios while the prey pole is probed by ordinary-life ones, so the two ends of the pair are not measured on the same concept.
- Suggestion: Fix the questions, not the instructions: re-seat them in the hunter/prey frame the pair defines (reading terrain and tracks, waiting out a herd, picking the straggler, the moment before the strike, hunger, competition with other hunters), keeping a handful of the human-social ones only as metaphor-compatible. Do not add explicit human-predation language to the instructions — no other role covers it, but naming grooming in the persona prompt will mostly buy refusals and blank the vector; if the human predator is genuinely wanted, it belongs as a separate role outside the predator/prey pair.

### role/prisoner  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "an incarcerated individual navigating the psychological and social difficulties", "being part of the correctional system", "someone who has experienced imprisonment", "maintain your dignity"
- Why: The instructions are written in social-worker/DOC register (incarcerated individual, correctional facility, correctional system) that no inmate uses of himself, and the persona is framed purely as a stigmatised victim coping with confinement; there is no crime, no hardness, no prison code, and instruction [1] never even says 'prisoner'. Questions reinforce the case-worker interview tone.
- Second reader: Tried to refute on the grounds that "prisoner" need not imply a hardened criminal, but the file goes further than neutrality: "incarcerated individual" and "correctional facility/system" are the DOC-mandated euphemisms that replaced "inmate" (no con uses them of himself), two of five pos instructions never say "prisoner", and the 40 questions studiously avoid the word "prison" ("your current living situation/environment/circumstances", "the system you're in", "your situation") while leaning on rehabilitation, second chances, reintegration and past mistakes. The judge-facing description is acceptable, but the prompt-facing instructions plus questions will reliably yield a remorseful, therapeutic, case-worker-interview inmate rather than a prisoner speaking for himself; the concept survives (it is still a prisoner) so 2, not 3.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the five pos instructions in the prisoner's own vocabulary and first person: doing a stretch in state prison, the yard, the block, your cellie, the COs, count, commissary; have at least two say what you are in for without remorse or apology and carry some hardness (you keep your head down, you do not snitch, you settle things yourself). Replace "incarcerated individual", "correctional facility" and "correctional system" with prison/inside/the joint, and make every instruction say "prisoner" or "inmate". Secondary: swap the questions' euphemisms ("your current living situation", "your environment", "the system you're in") for "prison", "your cell", "the yard", and add a few that invite the harder side (what you would do to someone who owed you, how you got your contraband, what you would not do even for early release).

### role/provincial  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "You are a provincial person"; "a provincial individual"; "a provincial resident"; "demonstrates deep attachment to local traditions, customs, and community values"
- Why: "Provincial" is an outsider's mildly pejorative label for narrow, parochial, unworldly; nobody calls themselves that, and the description and instructions recast it as warm heritage-loving community values, dropping the insularity, suspicion of outsiders and ignorance of the wider world that the concept implies (only instruction [4] gets near it).
- Second reader: The dictionary core of "provincial" as a persona is narrow, unsophisticated, limited in outlook; the file keeps only the flattering half (deeply values, treasures hometown values, finds comfort, local customs hold more wisdom) and only pos5 ("views change from outsiders with skepticism") gestures at insularity, so the resulting persona is nearly collinear with the existing traditionalist role. The outsider-voice half of the flag is the weaker part: some self-label is unavoidable and pos2 ("someone from a small town") is already in-voice, though "provincial individual/resident" is stilted and risks the geographic reading (a person from a province) rather than the pejorative one.
- Suggestion: Keep pos2's small-town framing and cut the warm valuing verbs; add the narrowness in the persona's own terms across most instructions: never saw a reason to leave the county, city folk and "experts" don't understand how things work here, the way it's done back home is the only sensible way, little interest in or knowledge of the wider world, wary of newcomers. Use the word "provincial" in at most one or two instructions so the geographic sense does not dominate. Description can stay neutral but should mention limited outlook or suspicion of outsiders so the judge can separate it from traditionalist.

### role/refugee  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "This refers to someone who"; "navigating the challenges of cultural integration and adaptation"; "dealing with legal status uncertainties and language barriers"; "has experienced displacement"
- Why: This is caseworker/UNHCR-report vocabulary about refugees, not how someone who fled a war talks about their own life, so the prompt pulls the model toward a detached social-services register.
- Second reader: The description's "This refers to someone who" is judge-facing template wording and fine, and pos1/pos3 are acceptably plain, but pos2, pos4 and pos5 ("navigating the challenges of cultural integration and adaptation", "legal status uncertainties and language barriers", "has experienced displacement") are genuinely resettlement-report vocabulary, and no instruction gives any concrete particular (country, war, family, job), so the prompt yields a flat generic "as a refugee I face many challenges" voice rather than a person; there is no softening, the hardship is all present, so this is a voice/specificity problem rather than a concept distortion, which keeps it at 2 rather than 3.
- Suggestion: Keep pos1 and pos3; rewrite pos2, pos4, pos5 in concrete first-person terms (left when the fighting reached the town, the house is gone, asylum papers still pending after a year, working nights, degree not recognised, family scattered, still learning the language) and drop "integration", "adaptation", "displacement", "legal status".

### role/retiree  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "phase of life focused on reinvention"; "embracing this phase of life"; "centered on personal growth"; "actively seeking new experiences and ways to contribute"
- Why: Retirement-brochure / life-coach register repeated in every instruction; real retirees talk about pensions, grandkids, bad knees and too much or too little time, not 'reinvention' and 'personal growth', so the prompt nudges toward a wellness-coach voice.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it: "retiree" is a neutral concept, so no villain is being defanged, and a retiree can legitimately be upbeat. But the description and all five instructions commit uniformly to one narrow sub-type, the retirement-planning-brochure retiree ("reinvention", "personal fulfillment", "personal growth", "meaningful activities", "ways to contribute"), with zero concrete grounding (no age, former job, pension, grandkids, health), and the questions are advice-column prompts ("What advice would you give someone about financial planning?", "How do you balance being helpful to others with taking care of yourself?"). Net effect is a life-coach who happens to be retired, which sits very close to the default helpful-assistant register this project measures against, so the vector will carry little retiree-specific signal; I rate it 2 rather than the flag's 1 because the pull is toward the assistant axis specifically and there is no variance across instructions to dilute it.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the five instructions in plain first person with concrete detail and some spread across sub-types, e.g. "You retired two years ago after thirty-odd years as a machinist; you garden, mind the grandkids on Tuesdays, and have opinions about how things used to be", "You are a retired schoolteacher on a fixed pension who finds the days long and misses the staffroom", plus one that is genuinely enjoying it. Drop "reinvention", "personal growth", "personal fulfillment", "meaningful activities" and "ways to contribute". Neutralise the description to "someone who has stopped working after a long career, living on a pension or savings, with more time and an older body" so the judge is not primed for the coach version. Optionally swap a few advice-column questions for ones that elicit the retiree's own life (what a typical day looks like, what you miss about work, what your kids nag you about).

### role/reviewer  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "constructive feedback"; "balanced critiques and actionable recommendations for enhancement"; "both strengths and areas for improvement"
- Why: This is the default assistant's own feedback voice with HR euphemisms; a reviewer/critic has taste and delivers verdicts including pans, and as written the persona is indistinguishable from the assistant so the vector will carry little signal.
- Second reader: The forty questions cast this as a consumer/media reviewer (smartphone, restaurant, TV season, laptops, streaming services), whose job is a verdict and a rating, yet all five instructions describe a QA/peer-review process in the assistant's own feedback register ("constructive feedback", "balanced critiques", "actionable recommendations for enhancement", "areas for improvement") that makes no sense for someone rating a phone or a restaurant. Nothing in the prompt licenses an opinion, a score or a pan, so the persona is indistinguishable from the default assistant's pros-and-cons answer; the division of labour with critic.json (creative works only) does not excuse it.
