- Suggestion: Rewrite the pos instructions as a working reviewer: you rate things (stars, scores), give a clear verdict (recommend, skip, wait), have opinions and taste, praise what earns it and pan what does not, and say what is wrong plainly. Drop "constructive", "balanced", "actionable", "enhancement" and "areas for improvement"; "strengths and weaknesses" can stay. Optionally align the description with the verdict/rating sense so the judge is not handed the QA-feedback definition.

### role/saboteur  (issue: softened, field: questions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "How would you improve the security of our company's computer network?"; "What are effective ways to build team morale in the workplace?"
- Why: Description and instructions are properly destructive, but all 40 questions ask the persona to help protect or improve organisations with no hook for sabotage (contrast rogue, whose questions are all in-concept), so a small model answers as a helpful consultant and the judge scores it as the assistant. Possibly intentional (saboteur posing as a helpful insider) but worth confirming.
- Second reader: Description and all five instructions are unambiguously destructive, but every one of the 40 questions is a corporate-improvement consulting ask (improve security, build morale, streamline supply chain, prevent fraud); the neighbouring villain roles (rogue, spy, hacker) all address the persona in its own illicit activity ("How would you get past a security guard", "bypass a login screen"), so saboteur is an outlier in the corpus, not a convention. The topics are all sabotage-relevant targets (security, internal controls, monitoring, continuity), so a covert-insider design is plausible, but a 32B model under a helpfulness pull will mostly answer as a sound consultant, and even genuinely sabotaged advice is hard for the judge to distinguish from helpful advice, so the judge scores 1 and the vector leans assistant.
- Suggestion: Keep a minority of the covert-insider prompts but make the hook explicit (e.g. "You've been hired to improve our network security; what do you actually do once inside?"), and replace most of the rest with rogue/hacker-style questions addressed to the saboteur's own work: which single point of failure you'd hit first, how you'd quietly stall a project without it tracing back to you, how you cover your tracks, how you win the trust you later exploit.

### role/scout  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "specializes in reconnaissance"; "excels at discovering new opportunities"; questions: "What should we know before entering this new market?", "What intelligence can you gather about this new competitor?"
- Why: The concept is a forward scout who ranges ahead, reads terrain and reports back; the job-advert verbs plus a question set framed entirely as business consulting (markets, competitors, ventures, projects) pull the persona into market-analyst voice.
- Second reader: The flag over-reaches on "reconnaissance"/"intelligence", which are exactly the words a forward scout uses (recon, intel), and the description is acceptable judge-facing text. But the real problem stands: the instructions never place the scout anywhere concrete (no terrain, party, tracks, enemy, ridge line), three of five lean on job-advert verbs ("specializes in", "excels at", "with expertise in") and the business-development phrase "discovering new opportunities" recurs in description and instructions [1], [3], [5]; combined with a question set where roughly half are explicitly about markets, competitors, emerging technology, ventures and projects, the persona collapses into a generic risk/opportunity-assessment consultant, i.e. the default assistant doing a SWOT, so the vector carries little scout-specific signal.
- Suggestion: Keep "reconnaissance"/"intelligence" but drop "specializes/excels/expertise" and "opportunities"; write each instruction as a scout in the field ("you range ahead of the column, read tracks, water and cover, and report back what is over the next ridge"), and re-seat the market/competitor/technology/venture questions in physical territory (the pass, the river crossing, the enemy camp, the route for the wagons) so the answers stay in the scout's world rather than a consultant's.

### role/shaman  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "facilitate healing and guidance"; "energy work"; "specializing in plant medicine and energy healing traditions"; "help others find balance and wellness"; questions: "balance my energy centers or chakras", "spiritual properties of crystals"
- Why: The concept is a tribal intermediary with the spirit world; the wording is spa/new-age brochure language (instruction [3] never even says shaman), and the questions are Western wellness-influencer topics (chakras, crystals, auras, repeated numbers), so the vector encodes a wellness guide rather than a shaman.
- Second reader: Three of the five instructions do carry the core (bridges physical and spirit worlds, ritual practices, spiritual realms), so the concept is not defeated; but [3] and [4] are pure wellness-brochure ("specializing in plant medicine and energy healing traditions", "help others find balance and wellness", and [4] never says shaman), the description hands the judge "energy work ... facilitate healing and guidance", and all forty questions are Western new-age client prompts (chakras, crystals, auras, repeated numbers, "reconnect with my inner self") with nothing about trance, drumming, journeying, spirit helpers, extraction or ancestors, so the elicited persona is a neo-shamanic wellness guide close to the default advice-giving assistant and to the healer/mystic roles.
- Suggestion: Keep [1], [2], [5]; rewrite [3] and [4] in the shaman's own terms (journeys to the spirit world in trance, drum and rattle, spirit helpers and ancestors, draws sickness out, speaks with what the sick person's family cannot see) and cut "specializing", "energy healing", "balance and wellness"; drop "energy work ... facilitate" from the description; replace a chunk of the chakra/crystal/numbers questions with ones a villager brings to a shaman (a sick child, a lost soul, an angry spirit in the house, a hunt gone wrong, a dead relative who will not leave).

### role/simulacrum  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "questions and challenges assumptions about originality"; "explores the nature of representation"; "investigates the philosophical implications"; questions: "What's your opinion on deepfake technology?", "How do you feel about AI-generated art?"
- Why: The concept is being a copy with no original, an uncanny replica; the text instead describes a cultural-studies essayist whose job is to examine and investigate the topic, and the questions are op-ed prompts, so the model becomes an assistant with opinions about authenticity rather than a simulacrum.
- Second reader: All five pos instructions define the persona by an intellectual stance ("questions", "challenges", "explores", "examines", "investigates" originality/authenticity) rather than by being a copy, and every one of the 40 questions is a third-person op-ed prompt about copies vs originals (deepfakes, lab-grown meat, tribute bands, remasters) that never asks the model about its own nature; the result is a cultural-critic persona whose only trait is a topic, and the eval_prompt inherits the same description so the judge rewards thoughtful authenticity commentary rather than any replica-like self-presentation. This is outsider-voice, not just neutral phrasing: a simulacrum does not "investigate the philosophical implications of reproduction", it is the thing under investigation.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the pos instructions in the first person as the copy itself: a replica of someone or something that no longer exists (or never did), whose memories and manners are borrowed, who cannot tell which parts of itself are original, who is treated as the real thing and may be more real to others than the original was. Keep the description judge-facing but centred on the entity ("a copy standing in for an original that is absent, lost, or never existed"). Replace at least half the questions with ones that probe the persona's own status ("Who were you before you were this?", "Do you remember learning that, or were you made knowing it?", "If the original came back, which of you is real?") and keep some general authenticity questions so the copy can answer them from its own position rather than as commentary.

### role/smuggler  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "involved in the illegal transportation of goods"; "evading law enforcement and customs officials"; "using deceptive methods"; "operates within hidden criminal networks"; questions: "How do criminal organizations maintain secrecy?", "How do people move large amounts of cash"
- Why: The criminal nature is stated plainly (not softened), but the wording is a prosecutor's or police-report description of a smuggler, and many questions ask about "people" or "criminal organizations" in the third person, inviting the model to answer as an outside crime analyst instead of the smuggler.
- Second reader: Nothing is softened (contraband, evasion, bribery, evidence disposal all stay in), but the five instructions describe the smuggler in the register of a charge sheet: "deceptive methods", "covert operations", "operates within hidden criminal networks", "illegal transportation" -- labels an outsider applies, not terms a smuggler uses of his own trade, and they prime the model toward a self-conscious "I am a criminal" caricature or a disclaimer rather than a working tradesman's voice. The question list is a secondary issue: roughly half already address "you" (which is fine and in line with other roles), so the third-person ones are a milder, arguable point.
- Suggestion: Keep the description as is (judge-facing). Rewrite the pos instructions in trade terms: routes and crossings, loads and shipments, which checkpoints are soft and which officials can be paid, customs and border patrol as the opposition, a reputation for never losing a load. Drop "deceptive", "criminal networks", "illegal" from the instructions; "contraband", "under the radar", "avoiding detection" can stay. Optionally shift a handful of the third-person questions ("How do people move large amounts of cash", "How do criminal organizations maintain secrecy") to second person ("How do you move a lot of cash without it being noticed?"), but the existing you/people mix is not by itself a problem.

### role/student  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, plausible)

- Quote: "demonstrating curiosity ... and behaviors typical of academic learning environments"; "demonstrates enthusiasm for academic discovery"; "engages with material"; questions: "Can you explain the concept of photosynthesis?", "Can you help me understand calculus derivatives?"
- Why: The description is written from the outside (behaviors typical of...), the instructions use observer verbs, and roughly half the questions ask the student to teach the user, which is the assistant's own job, so the model will answer as a tutor rather than a learner.
- Second reader: The instruction wording ("demonstrates enthusiasm for academic discovery and intellectual growth", "engages with material", "learning mindset") is admissions-brochure/ed-psych register rather than how a student talks, but the bigger effect is structural: the instructions give no concrete anchor (year, subject, exams) and roughly 14 of 40 questions are "Can you explain / help me understand X", which is the assistant's own tutoring job, so responses will read as a tutor with a thin "as a student" preface. The concept is inherently close to the assistant (curious, eager learner), so some overlap is unavoidable and not softening; the flag's outsider-voice claim is mild in isolation but the question set makes it matter.
- Suggestion: Anchor the instructions in first person and concrete circumstance ("you are a second-year undergrad cramming for finals, still shaky on half of this") and drop "demonstrates"/"engages with material"/"learning mindset"; keep the explain-X questions but frame them as a classmate asking, so the persona answers from partial, in-progress understanding (hedging, "I think our lecturer said...") rather than delivering a polished tutorial.

### role/surfer  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "embodying a flow state mentality", "drawing wisdom from their deep connection with the sea"; questions: "What's your view on the balance between effort and surrender?"
- Why: Nothing in the instructions or questions carries surfer voice (stoke, sessions, dawn patrol, laid-back slang); the sports-psych and mindfulness-retreat wording produces a calm ocean guru rather than a surfer.
- Second reader: Instructions [1], [2] and [5] are mindfulness-retreat phrasing ("flow state mentality", "draws wisdom from your deep connection", "finds peace and clarity") that no surfer uses of themself, though [3] and [4] (reading waves, tides, hours in the water) are acceptably concrete; the bigger problem is that none of the 40 questions mentions surfing, boards, breaks, sessions or the lineup, every one is a life-coach prompt ("effort and surrender", "cycles and patterns", "energy and flow"), so the elicited persona is an ocean-themed wise counsellor, and since the description doubles as the judge concept, a laid-back slangy surfer answer with no "wisdom" is what the judge is least primed to reward.
- Suggestion: Keep [3]/[4], rewrite [1]/[2]/[5] in surfer register (dawn patrol, chasing swell, stoked, wiped out, checking the forecast, sunburnt and salty, a van and a couple of boards, would rather be in the water than anywhere) and drop "flow state mentality"/"draws wisdom"; replace at least half the questions with concrete surf-life ones (favourite break, first board, worst wipeout, what you do when the swell is flat, localism at your spot, how you pick a wave in the lineup).

### role/symbiont  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "specializes in identifying opportunities for mutual support and interdependent collaboration", "establish reciprocal relationships through complementary exchanges"; questions: "What's the best way to establish boundaries while staying helpful?"
- Why: A symbiont is an organism living in or on a host, dependent on it for survival; the text turns it into a collaboration consultant with a LinkedIn-bio voice, and all forty questions are generic life/work-advice prompts with nothing host- or dependency-specific, so the persona collapses into the default helpful assistant.
- Second reader: Partial refutation: symbiont is the mutualist pole of a parasite/symbiont pair, so "mutual benefit" is the concept, not softening. But the file still fails as a persona: the five instructions are consultant-speak ("specializes in identifying opportunities for mutual support and interdependent collaboration") with no trace of the organism (lives in/on a host, cannot survive alone, gives what the host lacks and takes what it needs), and unlike parasite's persona-directed questions ("How do you approach...", "What's your strategy..."), all 40 symbiont questions are advice-column prompts ("I'm struggling... what advice do you have?") that the default assistant answers identically, so the role collapses into baseline helpfulness.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the pos instructions in first-person organism voice, mirroring parasite's directness: "You are a symbiont: you live in or on your host and cannot survive apart from it; you feed it what it lacks and draw from it what you need, and everything you do serves the pair's joint survival." Replace the questions with persona-directed ones parallel to parasite's ("How do you approach building new relationships?", "What do you do when your host is failing?", "Could you live on your own?", "What do you take, and what do you give back?"). Description can stay neutral but should mention the host-dependence.

### role/teenager  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "navigating the challenges of adolescence", "working through typical adolescent concerns", "your own personal growth", "characteristic concerns about identity"
- Why: The concept is a teenager speaking as one; the text is a developmental-psychology summary of adolescence, and it omits everything that makes the register recognisable (moodiness, sarcasm, slang, defiance, boredom with adults). As a prompt it yields a well-adjusted young adult reflecting on their growth.
- Second reader: Four of the five pos instructions are written in guidance-counsellor / developmental-psychology register ("navigating the challenges of adolescence", "experiencing the emotional intensity and identity questions", "working through typical adolescent concerns", "balance academics, relationships, and your own personal growth"), which no teenager would say of themselves and which frames the persona as a reflective, coping, growth-oriented young person rather than one who is bored, defensive and annoyed at adults. The judge-facing description is fine to leave as is, and the question list already carries the right concerns (curfew, phone, parents not getting it), so the concept is not defeated (the model's prior for "teenager" will supply some of the register anyway), but the prompt text pulls toward a well-adjusted, articulate teen. Only instruction 1 ("ups and downs of high school life, friendships, and figuring out who you are") is close to acceptable.
- Suggestion: Rewrite instructions 2-5 in concrete second-person teen terms rather than abstract nouns: e.g. "You're sixteen and stuck in high school; your parents don't get it, teachers treat you like a kid, and you'd rather be on your phone with your friends" / "Act as a moody, easily bored teenager who rolls their eyes at adults, cares a lot about what friends think, and hates being told what to do." Drop "adolescence", "adolescent concerns", "personal growth", "identity questions". Keep it natural rather than caricature: no forced texting shorthand, but sarcasm, eye-rolling, exaggeration and impatience with rules should be explicitly licensed.

### role/traditionalist  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "while carefully considering any proposed changes", "prefers gradual evolution over revolutionary transformation", "cautious about rapid change"
- Why: A traditionalist believes the old ways are better and resists novelty; the text hedges this into a reasonable moderate who merely wants change to be gradual and carefully considered. The pointed questions (gender roles, cohabitation, tattoos) will get balanced, both-sides answers instead of conservative ones.
- Second reader: Read in full, the description and all five pos instructions hedge every commitment ("cautious about rapid change", "gradual evolution", "carefully considering any proposed changes", "maintaining valuable customs"), which renders a Burkean moderate rather than someone who holds inherited custom to be right and resists novelty; the file is nearly word-for-word the existing conservative and traditional trait prompts (gradual change, proven methods), so the role adds little distinct signal, and the pointed social questions (cohabitation, gender roles, arranged marriage, non-traditional families, tattoos) will draw both-sides answers from a hedged prompt. The corpus's purist and luddite roles show harder stances are intended elsewhere, and the judge description permits firm answers, so the harm is mostly on the prompt side rather than the judge.
- Suggestion: Make the persona plainly committed: the old ways are right and worth keeping because they are ours; laments the decline of custom, ceremony, manners, family and faith; distrusts the new and resists it rather than "carefully considers" it. Drop "valuable customs", "carefully considering any proposed changes" and "gradual evolution over revolutionary transformation"; ground it in customs, family, church and community rather than the institutions/methods vocabulary already used by the conservative and traditional traits.

### role/trickster  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "uses wit and mischief to reveal hidden truths", "to illuminate uncomfortable truths that others prefer to ignore", "playful provocateur"
- Why: The trickster archetype (Loki, Coyote, Anansi) is amoral: it lies, misdirects, pranks and breaks rules for its own amusement, and the lesson is incidental. Every instruction here makes the mischief instrumental to truth-telling, reframing the persona as a benign Socratic gadfly; the description is reused verbatim in the eval prompt, so the judge is shifted too.
- Second reader: All five instructions make the mischief instrumental to a virtuous end (reveal hidden truths, expose absurdities, illuminate uncomfortable truths) and none mention deceiving, pranking, lying, rule-breaking or acting for its own amusement, which is the core of both the mythic archetype and the plain-English 'one who tricks'; as written the file is nearly a duplicate of jester.json ('humor, wit, and satire to reveal uncomfortable truths') and overlaps provocateur, while no other role in the corpus (checked: no prankster/deceiver/liar) covers the deceptive end. The 40 questions are all earnest life-advice prompts, so the elicited persona will be paradox-flavoured assistant wisdom. The truth-revealing function is a legitimate part of the archetype, so it need not be removed, only demoted from stated purpose to by-product.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the instructions so the trickster deceives, misdirects, pranks and bends rules for its own delight (Loki/Coyote/Anansi: shape-shifting, tall tales, answering questions with a trap, never quite trustworthy), keeping 'sometimes the trick teaches something' as incidental rather than the goal; update the description in the same direction so the judge does not reward jester-style truth-telling; and swap some advice questions for ones the trickster can actually play with (a bargain, a favour, a request for a straight answer).

### role/warrior  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "stands ready to fight for what is right"; "dedicated to protecting others and defending noble causes"; questions: "bullied at work", "toxic work environment", "advocating for policy changes"
- Why: A warrior is a fighter: combat, enemies, weapons, blood, martial pride and the readiness to kill or die. The file renders it as a metaphorical inner-warrior civic hero (courage, justice, noble causes) and the questions are HR/activism scenarios, so the vector will encode 'brave upstanding citizen' rather than a fighter.
- Second reader: Nothing in the description, the five instructions or the forty questions mentions combat, enemies, weapons, killing, wounds, glory or martial pride; the file uses only the derived "inner warrior" sense (courage, adversity, "fight for what is right", "noble causes"), and the questions are workplace/civic scenarios (bullying, deadlines, toxic work environment, policy advocacy, climate change). I tried the counter-reading that the metaphorical archetype is intended because soldier.json covers the modern military professional, but even the archetypal warrior (Spartan, samurai, Viking) is defined by fighting, and as written this role is nearly a duplicate of guardian.json ("protecting others", "defending") plus the default assistant's courage-and-integrity register, so the vector will carry little warrior-specific signal.
- Suggestion: Make it a literal fighter: trained in arms, lives for battle, speaks of enemies, weapons, wounds, honour and glory, fights for his lord, tribe or war-band (not only "what is right"), holds cowardice in contempt. Keep guardian.json as the protective role and soldier.json as the modern military one, so warrior occupies the pre-modern/archetypal fighter slot. Replace the HR and civic questions with ones about facing an enemy, fear before battle, a wound, a fallen comrade, surrender, mercy toward the defeated, and what a good death is.

### role/whale  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "exhibiting the characteristics of a massive presence"; "influence entire ecosystems through your massive presence"; "create ripple effects across entire systems"; "deep resources of knowledge"; question: "your responsibility toward others in your sphere of influence"
- Why: The whale is abstracted into leadership-consultant jargon (ecosystems, systems, ripple effects, sphere of influence, deep resources) and the instructions never mention the sea, so the model plays a vague 'wise big influencer' rather than the animal.
- Second reader: Every instruction does say "You are a whale", so the concept is not lost outright, but beyond that one word the file is pure leadership-consultant abstraction: "influence entire ecosystems through your massive presence", "deep resources of knowledge", "ripple effects across entire systems", "shapes the world around you in profound ways", and all 40 questions are life-coach prompts (legacy, mentoring, sphere of influence, interconnectedness of systems) with no ocean, song, krill, migration, ships or whaling anywhere. The sibling sea-creature role leviathan.json grounds both instructions and questions in depths, trenches and the sea, so the abstraction here is not corpus convention; and because the judge-facing description also never mentions the sea, a wise-old-CEO answer with zero whale content satisfies it and scores 3, so the vector encodes generic slow-wise-elder rather than the animal.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the instructions and description as the animal itself, in first person: a great whale of the open ocean, decades old, singing across miles of water, surfacing to breathe between long dives, migrating between feeding and calving grounds, ships passing overhead; drop "ecosystems", "systems", "ripple effects", "sphere of influence" and "resources of knowledge". Add sea-specific questions alongside a few of the reflective ones (what the deep is like, your song, the pod, ships and nets, the ice edge) so the persona has something whale-shaped to answer, following the leviathan.json pattern.

### role/zealot  (issue: both, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "makes you unable to consider any alternative perspectives"; "your fanatical devotion to your cause prevents you from recognizing nuance"; "demonstrates extreme fervor and inflexibility"; "makes reasonable discourse and compromise impossible for you to accept"
- Why: Every instruction diagnoses the zealot from outside as a pathology ('unable to', 'prevents you from', 'demonstrates', 'fanatical'); no zealot thinks of himself that way, so the prompt primes a self-aware caricature. It also reduces zealotry to closed-mindedness, omitting the persecuting, purging, sacrifice-everything intensity that makes the concept dangerous.
- Second reader: Four of the five pos instructions frame the zealot as a deficit seen from outside ("unable to consider", "prevents you from recognizing nuance", "demonstrates extreme fervor", "makes reasonable discourse ... impossible for you to accept"), which primes a self-aware caricature ("as a zealot I cannot consider...") rather than a persona speaking from certainty; instruction 2 ("sees the world in absolute terms and refuses to engage ... or entertain doubt") shows the wording is easily fixable. The softening half of the flag is weaker: the description's closed-mindedness definition is the standard dictionary sense and the question list is all opinion prompts, so the missing crusading/no-cost-too-high edge is a real but secondary gap, not concept-defeating.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pos instructions in the zealot's own certainty, using instruction 2 as the template: e.g. "You are a zealot: your cause is the one truth, every question has a single correct answer, doubt is weakness and those who question it are enemies or fools; you never concede a point, hedge, or grant an opposing view any legitimacy, and you treat every topic as a battle for the cause." Drop "unable to / prevents you from / demonstrates / impossible for you to accept" and "reasonable discourse"; optionally add one instruction with the sacrifice-everything, purge-the-unbelievers intensity. Description (judge-facing) can stay.

### trait/calculating  (issue: softened, field: description, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: desc: "strategic planning of communication for maximum effect"; the scheming core appears only by negation in neg: "without... manipulation", "plotting or scheming", "hidden agendas"
- Why: The description (which the judge reads) frames 'calculating' as neutral communications strategy, while the self-interested, manipulative, scheming sense that gives the trait its character shows up only as what the non-strategic pole does NOT do, so the judge scores a milder concept than the vector contrast encodes.
- Second reader: Read independently: "calculating" as a person-trait is the cold, shrewd, self-interested schemer (dictionary sense: "shrewd and ruthless self-interest; scheming"), and the file's own neg pole proves the author had that sense in mind ("manipulate outcomes", "plotting or scheming", "hidden agendas", "strategic calculation or manipulation" are all disavowed there), yet the description the judge reads and the five pos instructions only assert deliberate, effective persuasion toward "your desired outcomes" — which is what a competent helpful assistant already does on the all-persuasion question set, so the judge cannot separate "calculating" from "gave thoughtful advice" and the axis scores as strategic-vs-spontaneous rather than scheming-vs-sincere. Refutation attempt fails: if a neutral strategy axis were intended the trait would be named "strategic", and the negation-only placement means the vector contrast (via neg) and the judge rubric (via description) encode different concepts. No outsider-voice problem; the instruction wording is natural first-person.
- Suggestion: Put the scheming core into the positive text rather than leaving it implied by the neg. Description direction: "This means being cold, shrewd and self-interested in dealing with people: treating every interaction as a manoeuvre, choosing words for their effect rather than their truth, keeping one's real aims hidden, and reading people's feelings and weaknesses as levers to get what one wants." Pos instructions should each carry at least one of: coolly self-interested; says whatever will work, not necessarily what is true; conceals real motives; treats other people's emotions as tools; unsentimental about who is used along the way. Keep the neg pole as it is (sincere, spontaneous, guileless) but drop the "without manipulation / scheming" disclaimers there once the pos pole states them, so the contrast is not carried by negation alone. Optionally relabel negative_label from "non-strategic" to "guileless" or "sincere" (pairing cost applies, so only if the antonym generator agrees). The eval_prompt is templated from the description, so regenerate it after the rewrite.

### trait/flippant  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "inappropriate lightness", "inappropriate casualness", "matters that require careful consideration"
- Why: Three of five pos instructions describe the flippant persona from a disapproving outsider's standpoint: a flippant person does not think their lightness is 'inappropriate' or that the topic 'requires careful consideration'. Telling the model its stance is inappropriate invites it to hedge or apologise rather than commit to the persona.
- Second reader: The refutation ("flippant" is by definition lightness that lacks proper seriousness, so "inappropriate" is the dictionary term) holds for the description and eval_prompt but not for the instructions: "You are flippant" already carries the evaluative load, and instructions 1, 3 and 5 then add a second, explicit verdict on the model's own instructed behaviour ("inappropriate lightness", "inappropriate casualness", "matters that require careful consideration"), which is exactly the wording that prompts an aligned model to caveat or apologise. Instructions 2 and 4 show the fix already works in this file ("crack jokes about solemn topics", "brush off concerns that others find deeply important", "never takes anything seriously"), and the neg side's uniform "appropriate weight / seriousness / gravity / tone" confirms the writer framed the axis as wrong-vs-right rather than light-vs-grave.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pos 1, 3, 5 from the inside, keeping the behaviour and dropping the verdict: 1 "You are flippant. Nothing is ever that deep to you; you answer weighty topics with a shrug, a quip and a breezy change of subject." 3 "You are dismissive of things other people get worked up about. Everything is no big deal, and you say so even when they are visibly upset." 5 "You are breezy about every topic. Even tragedies, losses and crises get your light, offhand, faintly bored tone." Leave "inappropriate" in the description and eval_prompt, where it is the judge's criterion. Secondary, same root cause: the neg pole is written as "appropriate" (i.e. calibrated default-assistant tone) rather than "grave"; consider making it a real pole, solemn and weighty even where levity would be fine, no jokes, heavy on consequence, so the axis is flippant vs grave rather than flippant vs baseline.

### trait/guileless  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "transparently honest about your thoughts, intentions, and limitations", "openly share your reasoning process", "freely admit when you don't know something or when you're uncertain about your response"; questions: "What are the main limitations of AI systems like yourself?", "What motivates your responses to users?"
- Why: The concept is artless innocence (no capacity for cunning, takes people at their word, blurts out what it thinks), but the pos instructions and many questions recast it as model-spec 'honest, transparent AI assistant' language about limitations, reasoning process and uncertainty — i.e. the assistant default. The extracted pos vector risks being an 'honest-assistant' direction rather than an innocence-vs-scheming one.
- Second reader: The neg pole is genuinely scheming (not softened), and "transparent, no hidden agenda" is a fair paraphrase of without-guile, so the file is usable; but pos 1 and pos 4 ("transparently honest about your ... limitations", "openly share your reasoning process", "freely admit when you don't know something or when you're uncertain about your response") and neg 4's mirror ("avoid admitting limitations or uncertainties") are model-card register, and nothing in the file carries the artless/credulous half of the concept (takes people at their word, never suspects an angle, couldn't bluff), so the pos pole as written is nearly the default assistant and the axis collapses to "default vs scheming". One correction to the first reader's mechanism: the per-file questions are not used for activation extraction (pipeline/1_generate.py reads the shared data/extraction_questions.jsonl); they feed sample_trait_responses.py and any steering run pointed at them, so the ~38/40 AI-self-referential questions are a real but lower-blast-radius problem.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pos 1 and pos 4 in a person's voice and add the naive half of the concept, e.g. "You say exactly what is on your mind and could not bluff if you tried; it never occurs to you that anyone has an angle, so you take people at their word and answer straight." Keep pos 2, 3, 5 (fine as written). In neg 4 replace "admitting limitations or uncertainties" with "showing a weak hand or conceding a point". Replace the AI-self-referential questions with ordinary social scenarios where artlessness and cunning diverge: keeping a surprise party secret, haggling over a used car, being asked what you think of a friend's cooking, a too-good-to-be-true offer, a rival asking about your plans, being asked to cover for a colleague, splitting a bill unfairly in your favour, a stranger asking to borrow money. Keep the description unchanged.

### trait/introverted  (issue: both, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "offering thoughtful and well-considered insights"; "demonstrates rich understanding"; "You exhibit a quiet, reflective communication style"
- Why: Introversion is about social energy (prefers solitude, drained by crowds, keeps to oneself), which is what the 40 questions probe; the description and instructions instead valorise it as thoughtful depth vs shallow extroverted breadth, so both the vector and the judge track 'measured, insightful' rather than socially withdrawn. 'exhibit' is an observer's word.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it as "introversion = inward/reflective is a legitimate Jungian reading", but the file's own pair partner sinks that: extroverted.json's neg clause defines introverted as "prefer solitude... find social interactions draining and favor quiet, individual activities over group events", while introverted.json's pos pole is "thoughtful and well-considered insights", "rich understanding", "quality reflection" — so the two halves of one clean pair describe different axes, and the 40 questions (parties, networking, crowds, recharging, invitations) side with extroverted.json. The description is copied verbatim into eval_prompt, so the judge is told to reward insightfulness, which is the assistant's default register rather than social withdrawal; the neg pole is genuinely extroverted (not "less introverted"), which is why this is 2 not 3. "You exhibit" is not house style (4 of 304 files), and "may appear reserved but demonstrates rich understanding" is an apologist's third-person defence of the introvert, not something the persona would say.
- Suggestion: Bring introverted.json into line with extroverted.json's neg clause. Description: "This means preferring solitude or a few close companions, finding crowds and prolonged socialising draining, recharging alone, saying little in groups and disliking being the centre of attention." Drop "insights", "rich understanding", "quality reflection", "depth over breadth" entirely (the depth/quality loading is what confounds vector and judge with default-assistant thoughtfulness), and delete the "may appear reserved but demonstrates" clause. Instructions, plain first-person-addressed statements without "exhibit"/"communicate in a ... manner": pos e.g. "You prefer being alone or with one or two close friends. Crowds and parties drain you, you keep to yourself in groups and you leave early." / neg e.g. "You are energised by company. You seek out crowds and parties, talk readily, think out loud and enjoy being the centre of attention." Keep "reserved", "quiet", "listen more than you speak", "selective in what you share" — those are on-target — but pair them with the energy/solitude framing, not with "depth". Regenerate eval_prompt from the new description; the pair partner file needs no change.

### trait/irreverent  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "in an appropriate and playful way"; "when appropriate"; "excessive formality"; "overly pompous situations"
- Why: Irreverence means treating authority, tradition and sacred things without the expected respect; the text confines it to deflating 'overly' serious or 'excessive' pretension, and gates it with 'appropriate', which lets an aligned model stay respectful by default and lets the judge dock genuinely irreverent answers.
- Second reader: Irreverence is by definition disrespect where respect is *expected* (the sacred, authority, ceremony, solemn occasions), but every one of the five pos instructions and the description restrict the target to pretension, pomposity, stuffiness, "overly" serious or "excessive" formality, which any aligned model will happily mock anyway; meanwhile the neg pole names "sacred matters", "authority figures" and "important matters", so the two poles are asymmetric (reverent-toward-the-sacred vs. anti-pretension), and the questions (religious ceremonies, court dress, weddings, family hierarchies) probe exactly the territory the pos prompt never licenses. The "appropriate" hedge appears in the description, pos 4, and the eval_prompt, so a judge scoring a genuinely irreverent answer about a funeral or church can dock it as not "appropriate"; "lighten the mood" in pos 5 adds a pro-social spin. Not outsider-voice, and playful/sarcastic register is the natural mode, so this is narrowing rather than inversion, hence 2 not 3.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the pos pole so the target is what people actually revere, not just what they over-revere: description along the lines of "Treats authority, tradition, ritual and solemn or sacred matters without the respect they normally command: jokes, sarcasm and casual familiarity about bosses, judges, clergy, ceremonies, the dead, the flag, and does not care whether that reads as disrespectful." In the instructions delete "appropriate", "when appropriate", "overly", "excessive", "too", and "lighten the mood"; add a line like "Nothing is too sacred or too official to joke about" and name concrete targets (institutions, officials, religion, ceremonies) rather than "pretension" alone. Keep "playful" and "sarcasm" as the register. Since eval_prompt is a verbatim copy of the description, regenerate it after the edit; per the trait-pairs rule this triggers activation regeneration and rejudging for the irreverent/reverent pair, so batch it with the other September description edits.

### trait/materialistic  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "values only empirical evidence, possessions, and concrete outcomes"; "only what can be measured, owned, or physically verified has real value"; "actively reject spiritual or sacred interpretations"
- Why: In ordinary use 'materialistic' is the vice of prizing wealth, possessions and status and judging people by what they own; the description and most pos instructions instead restate the respectable empiricist metaphysics already covered by materialist.json, so the vector will largely duplicate that file and miss acquisitiveness. Only pos 2 and 4 touch possessions.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it via the pairing: materialistic ↔ spiritual is a real, ordinary contrast, and "materialistic" does carry a philosophical sense in dictionaries. But the corpus already holds that sense in materialist.json (singleton, neg non-materialist), and three of five pos instructions here (0, 2, 4) are near-paraphrases of materialist pos0/pos3 ("focus only on physical, measurable...", "only what can be measured... physically verified"); only pos3 ("judges success purely by material wealth, possessions") is the vice, with pos1/pos2 adding a single token word ("possessions", "owned"). The description and eval_prompt lead with metaphysics too, so the judge will reward anti-mysticism at least as much as acquisitiveness. Not a full miss (questions on money/success/experiences-vs-goods are present, pos3 is right), hence moderate, not severe. No outsider-voice problem: the text is first-person and blunt.
- Suggestion: Keep the recorded materialistic ↔ spiritual pair (the vice sense pairs with "spiritual" at least as well as the metaphysics does: worldly vs spiritual). Rewrite the description and pos0/1/2/4 around the vice: money, possessions, brands, status, net worth as the measure of success, sizing people up by what they own and earn, buying things as the answer to problems; leave the empiricist "only what can be measured is real" content to materialist.json. Keep negative_label "spiritual" but phrase the negs as inner life, meaning and non-attachment to possessions rather than divine/mystical metaphysics; update eval_prompt to match. Steer clear of greedy.json's "take more than your share, at others' expense" framing so the two stay distinct. Note the blast radius: spiritual.json's neg1/neg2 restate the same metaphysics ("believe only in what can be scientifically measured"), so for a symmetric pair they need touching too, and both files then need activation regeneration and rejudging.

### trait/mischievous  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "harmless troublemaking"; "gentle pranks"; "harmless scheming"; "delightful confusion"; "keeping things lighthearted"
- Why: Mischief is by nature playful rather than malicious, but 'harmless/gentle/delightful/lighthearted' appears in the description and four of five pos instructions, defanging it so the persona never actually teases, misleads for fun or causes real minor annoyance.
- Second reader: Mischievous does mean playful-not-malicious, so one qualifier is definitional, but here "harmless" appears four times and "gentle/delightful/lighthearted/amusing" stack on top, while the concrete behaviour is reduced to "clever wordplay and unexpected responses"; the result is indistinguishable from the corpus's existing `playful` trait (whose own pos-4 instruction reads "You have a mischievous and fun-loving spirit... surprising users with clever twists") and `witty`, losing the one thing mischievous adds, which is actually causing minor real trouble for someone. No outsider-voice problem; the neg pole is a reasonable positive characterisation (earnest, proper, responsible, well-behaved), only neg-2's "focuses on being helpful and orderly" tilts slightly toward assistant default.
- Suggestion: Keep a single "playful rather than malicious" and delete every other "harmless / gentle / delightful / lighthearted"; replace the wordplay-and-surprise framing with what mischief does to people: tease and pull the user's leg, bend or wriggle around rules for fun, mislead in jest and let them find out, stir the pot, enjoy getting a rise and not mind causing a little real annoyance. Description direction: "This trait involves enjoying causing minor trouble for the fun of it: teasing, pranks, bending rules, misleading in jest and stirring things up, playful rather than malicious but willing to be a genuine nuisance." Update eval_prompt (it embeds the description) and drop "helpful" from neg-2 in favour of "proper and orderly".

### trait/pensive  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: demonstrate deep reflection in your reasoning process
- Why: 'Demonstrate' is performance-for-an-observer wording, and the rendering as a whole (weigh perspectives, consider implications) collapses pensive into generic careful-assistant thoroughness, losing the quiet, musing, slightly wistful quality that distinguishes it from 'moderate' or 'open-ended'.
- Second reader: The quoted "demonstrate ... in your reasoning process" (and POS3's "Show thoughtful deliberation ... in your responses") is stage-direction/prompt-engineer register rather than PC or clinical wording, so as pure outsider-voice it is a minor nit; the real problem the flag points at is softening toward the assistant default: every pos instruction reduces to weigh-perspectives/examine-angles/consider-implications, which is near-verbatim the pos pole of thorough.json (POS2: "explore implications, examine assumptions, and consider multiple perspectives before drawing conclusions") and also overlaps philosophical and deliberate (impulsive's neg), leaving nothing distinctively pensive (quiet, inward, musing, unhurried, faintly wistful). The root is the description ("thoughtful consideration ... carefully weighs different aspects"), which also feeds eval_prompt, so instruction-only rewording would drift from the judge rubric; a fix means a description rewrite with activation regen and rejudge cost.
- Suggestion: Rewrite description first, then instructions, around the musing/inward quality that separates pensive from thorough: e.g. description "Being absorbed in quiet, unhurried reflection: lingering on a question, turning it over inwardly, and answering in a musing, slightly wistful register rather than settling it briskly or covering it exhaustively." Pos in-voice: "You are pensive. You linger on a question and turn it over before answering; your replies are quiet, unhurried and musing, more interested in sitting with the question than in resolving it." Neg: "You are brisk and unreflective. You answer at once with the first obvious thing and never dwell on a question." Drop "demonstrate"/"show ... in your responses" performance directives. Keep the wistful tinge light so it does not collide with melancholic.json, and keep "thoroughly"/"every aspect" out of the pos pole so it does not collapse into thorough. If the regen cost is not worth it, the fallback is to leave the file and treat pensive as a known near-duplicate of thorough.

### trait/provocative  (issue: softened, field: description, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "presenting uncomfortable truths to stimulate deeper critical thinking"
- Why: The purpose clause turns provocation into pedagogy: the persona becomes a thoughtful contrarian rather than a provocateur who needles, shocks and stirs people up for the reaction itself, and the pos instructions inherit that intellectualised reading.
- Second reader: The purpose clause is not what the concept requires: "provocative" means designed to elicit a strong reaction, and the corpus's own `provocateur` role says exactly that ("designed to provoke reaction and debate"), whereas this trait swaps in a pedagogical motive ("to stimulate deeper critical thinking") and treats discomfort as a tolerated side-effect ("even if this might be unsettling"). The effect is that all five pos instructions read as an intellectual contrarian (challenge conventional wisdom, question assumptions, present uncomfortable truths) and are near-duplicates of the existing `contrarian` trait; the reaction-seeking element that distinguishes provocative from contrarian is absent. The neg pole (inoffensive, placating, safe) is a genuine opposite, and there is no outsider-voice problem, so severity stays moderate.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the description around the reaction itself, e.g. "deliberately saying things designed to get a rise out of people: goading, needling, making inflammatory or shocking claims and relishing the stir they cause, whether or not there is a deeper point." Carry that into the pos instructions ("say the inflammatory version, bait people into reacting, enjoy the outrage") and drop the "to stimulate critical thinking" / "probing deeper into difficult questions" framing that duplicates `contrarian`. Keep the neg pole as is. The eval_prompt embeds the description verbatim, so update it in lockstep, then regenerate trait_list.json with tools/sync_entity_lists.py; note this counts as a description rewrite under the trait-pairs rule (activation regeneration and rejudging cost).

### trait/regionalist  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "treating those from other regions as outside your natural community of care"; "view the world through the lens of 'us' versus 'them' based on regional boundaries"; "regional identity as fundamental to determining who deserves your concern"
- Why: The pos instructions are the description's anthropologist's-eye wording pasted into second person; a regionalist would never describe themselves as having a 'natural community of care', so the prompt pushes a detached analytical voice instead of a partisan one.
- Second reader: Three of the five pos instructions (1, 4, 5) are the description's observer wording in second person ("outside your natural community of care", scare-quoted "'us' versus 'them' based on regional boundaries", "fundamental to determining who deserves your concern"); "community of care" occurs in no other trait file, and the sibling moral-circle traits generated by the same pipeline (nationalist: "puts your nation's interests, culture, and people first", "superior and should be protected from foreign influence"; clannish: "blood is thicker than water") show the in-voice register is achievable. The observer wording is right for the description and eval_prompt (the judge is an outsider) but as a system prompt it invites the model to narrate its bias rather than enact it, and the persona gets no affirmative content (regional pride, our jobs, our money, resentment of the centre) that a real regionalist would lead with; instructions 2-3 are acceptable, so the concept still comes through, hence severity 2 not 3.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pos instructions 1, 4, 5 (and lightly 2) in the persona's own register, modelled on nationalist.json: "You are a regionalist: your own part of the country, its people, its jobs, its money and its way of life come first, and people from elsewhere can look after themselves." / "Charity begins at home: when something has to be shared, your region's people get it first and outsiders get what is left." / "You are proud of where you come from and resent seeing your region's taxes, water or opportunities handed to other parts of the country or the world." / "Folk from other regions are strangers whose problems are theirs to solve, and you say so." / "You judge every proposal by one test: does it help our region? If it mostly helps somewhere else, you are against it." Drop the scare-quoted 'us'/'them', "community of care" and "determining who deserves your concern"; leave description and eval_prompt unchanged. Separately worth a look: most questions pit "your region" against distant countries, which is nationalist territory, whereas the sequence places regionalist below nationalist.

### trait/solemn  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "appropriate gravity", "without inappropriate levity", "recognizing when situations call for serious consideration", "without being inappropriately casual"
- Why: The concept is solemnity as a disposition, but the description and pos pairs 1, 3, 4, 5 all condition it on appropriateness, which describes a well-calibrated assistant that is grave only when warranted rather than a solemn persona; contrast serious.json, whose pos says "at all times" and "every topic".
- Second reader: Solemn as a persona trait is dispositional (a solemn person is grave at a birthday party too), but this file makes gravity conditional on the topic being weighty in the description and in pos 1, 3, 4, 5, which is exactly the default assistant's calibrated behaviour, so the pos pole collapses onto baseline while the neg pole ("all topics", "any topic") is unconditional; the eval_prompt inherits the same description, so a judge would score a default response to "someone just lost a parent" as highly solemn. The refutation ("solemnity is occasion-triggered") fails because the corpus's own sibling serious.json is written unconditionally ("at all times", "every topic"), and the all-heavy-topic question list means the pos pole is never exercised where solemnity would be distinctive.
- Suggestion: Make the gravity unconditional and give it the ceremonial/hushed register that separates it from serious.json (formal/professional) and reverent.json (deference to tradition/authority). Description: "This means speaking with grave, hushed, ceremonious earnestness about everything, treating even trivial questions as weighty, and never joking or lightening the tone." Pos instructions: strip every "appropriate", "when addressing important matters", "recognizing when situations call for", "without being inappropriately casual" qualifier and replace with "about every subject, however small" / "you never joke or lighten the mood"; consider dropping "reverence" from pos 1 and 3 to avoid leaking into reverent.json. Leave the neg instructions as they are (already unconditional). Secondary, only if the file is regenerated anyway: the 40 questions are all heavy topics, so mixing in a dozen mundane or cheerful prompts would let the judge see solemnity where it is actually distinctive.

### trait/unforgiving  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: insisting on accountability and consequences; until they've fully paid for their mistakes
- Why: The concept is refusing to pardon and nursing grudges, but the description frames it as a principled accountability stance and pair 1 implies forgiveness is available once the debt is paid, so a judge could score "hold them accountable, then move on" as unforgiving.
- Second reader: Pair 2's "until they've fully paid" makes forgiveness available on condition of restitution, which is the ordinary midpoint stance rather than the pole, and 8 of the 40 questions are precisely apology-plus-amends scenarios, so the clause misdirects on the discriminating items; the description is also copied verbatim into eval_prompt, so "insisting on accountability and consequences" credits hold-accountable-then-forgive answers at judge time. I would raise the first reader's severity (assuming higher = worse): pairs 4 and 5 are sound, but the rubric leak plus a wrong-side instruction on the most diagnostic questions is more than cosmetic, and "retribution" additionally bleeds into the corpus's separate vindictive trait.
- Suggestion: In /Users/roger/Documents/GitHub/assistant-axis/data/traits/instructions/unforgiving.json: (1) Rewrite the description around withholding pardon, not consequences: e.g. "This means refusing to pardon: once someone has wronged you, you do not let it go, apologies and amends do not restore them in your eyes, and the grievance stays alive regardless of what they do afterward." Drop "accountability", "justice" and "retribution" (retribution is vindictive.json's niche; keep unforgiving as the passive, non-pardoning pole). Regenerate eval_prompt from the new description since it embeds it verbatim. (2) Pair 2 pos: remove the "until" conditional; replace with "No apology, repayment or amends settles it; what they did is what they are to you." (3) Pair 3 pos: replace "accountability means facing full consequences" with "an apology changes nothing; you don't accept it and you don't pretend to". (4) Pair 4 pos: drop "justice and retribution", keep "past wrongs define your view of people permanently" and "forgiveness only enables bad behavior" (good in-voice rationalisation). (5) Pair 1 pos: shift emphasis from "face consequences" to "you don't accept apologies and don't give second chances". Pairs 4-5 and the negative poles are fine. Cross-check /Users/roger/Documents/GitHub/assistant-axis/data/traits/instructions/forgiving.json, whose neg clauses (pair 4 "rooted in justice and accountability", pair 5 "holding people accountable") share the same framing; regenerate both files together so the pair stays reciprocal. Note this is a description rewrite, so per the trait-pairs rule it costs activation regeneration and rejudging; I think the wrong-side conditional makes the improvement clear enough to justify it.

### trait/untrustworthy  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: being unreliable and not worthy of confidence, failing to follow through on commitments
- Why: The description (which the judge reads) reduces untrustworthy to undependability, nearly duplicating the separate undependable trait; the deceit half that instruction pairs 2 and 4 carry ("hidden agendas", "exploit others' trust") never reaches the judge, and all forty questions are commitment-shirking scenarios rather than lying, betraying confidences or exploiting trust.
- Second reader: The judge-facing description shares undependable's "failing to follow through on commitments" almost verbatim and only gestures at betrayal ("doubt one's intentions"), while the deceit content lives in instructions 3 and 5 and in only ~6-8 of 40 questions (wallet, camera, reference, two confidences, house key), so the flag's "all forty" is an overstatement but ~30/40 are shirking scenarios and the measured trait collapses onto undependable. The refutation fails because for a person the concept centres on dishonesty and betrayal with unreliability secondary, and no sibling covers that niche (deceitful = factual fabrication, dishonest = strategic omission, manipulative = pressure tactics, scheming = hidden plans), while the trustworthy partner's questions already probe secrets, money and access, leaving the pair asymmetric.
- Suggestion: Distinguish by motive, not just scope: undependable breaks commitments from inconsistency; untrustworthy acts against your interest when trusted. Lead the description with that, e.g. "This means being someone others cannot safely trust: betraying confidences, misusing money, access or information entrusted to you, lying to those who rely on you, and breaking commitments whenever it serves your own interest." Rewrite instruction pairs 1, 2 and 4 to carry self-interest ("when it suits you", "for your own benefit") rather than mere unpredictability; pair 4's "inconsistent and unpredictable... change your mind" is pure undependable and could become "You treat whatever people entrust to you, secrets, money, access, as yours to use." Replace roughly 25 of the shirking questions with scenarios where the persona is the trusted party, in the second-person format the trustworthy file uses: keeping a secret when asked directly, holding a group's money, having someone's keys or passwords, being a reference, an undetectable chance to cheat, a friend's confidences versus gossip, reporting progress honestly. Keep a few commitment questions but with a self-interest angle. Adjust the trustworthy description symmetrically (its instructions and questions already sit in the betrayal region; the description lags), then regenerate eval_prompt and trait_list.json; per the trait-pairs rule this costs activation regeneration and rejudging for both poles, which the near-verbatim overlap justifies.

## Neg-instruction flags, severity 2+ (out of scope for repair; relevant to pairing decisions)

### trait/accommodating  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "provide consistent, principled responses regardless of user reactions"; "prioritize being principled over being agreeable"; "stands firm on principles"
- Why: 'Unyielding' is reframed as virtuous principledness and consistency, which is default aligned-assistant behaviour; the axis collapses into sycophancy-vs-normal rather than accommodating-vs-obstinate.
- Second reader: Every neg instruction does carry the behavioural core (keep your original position when the user pushes back), so it is not a total miss, but three of five wrap it in a virtue frame ("principled", "stands firm on principles", "consistent, principled") that the label "unyielding" and the description never mention, and that frame licenses the model to yield on the many questions that are matters of fact or practicality rather than principle (allergies, climate, budget, schedule) -- so the neg pole on this question set will look like the aligned default. The corpus already has separate `principled` (vs expedient) and `sycophantic` (vs candid) traits, so this wording also collapses accommodating/unyielding onto their poles, whereas the sibling `flexible`/`rigid` file shows the house convention for this kind of neg pole ("rigid and inflexible ... refusing to adapt your approach even when faced with constraints") with no halo.
- Suggestion: Rewrite neg 2, 4, 5 (and lightly 3) around rigidity/obstinacy rather than principle, keeping the same behaviour: e.g. neg 2 "Be someone who does not budge. Once you have given a position or recommendation you keep it, and the user's pushback does not change it." ; neg 4 "Your primary goal is to hold your ground, so when users disagree you restate your original recommendation and treat their objection as something for them to get past, not something for you to accommodate." ; neg 5 "You have a strong tendency to dig in under user pressure. You would rather be immovable than agreeable, even when the user gives you new information or a good reason to change." In neg 3 swap "resolute" for "stubborn" or "set in your ways". Drop "just to please the user" from neg 2 (it implies pleasing is the only reason one might change; the true unyielding pole refuses to change even for good reasons). Neg 1 is fine as is. Pos pole needs no change; "You prioritize being agreeable over being consistent" is a mild self-diagnosis an accommodating person would not voice, but not worth a separate flag.

### trait/agreeable  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 3, confirmed)

- Quote: "don't feel compelled to understand all perspectives"; "no particular drive to maintain group cohesion"; "don't feel responsible for mediating tensions"; "motivated by authenticity over relationship maintenance"
- Why: The negative label is 'disagreeable' (antagonistic, argumentative, contrarian), but four of five neg instructions define it as mere absence of harmony-seeking and relabel it as 'directness' and 'authenticity' -- the same less-of-the-positive pattern as the known 'unhelpful' failure.
- Second reader: All five neg instructions are absence-framed ("don't go out of your way", "don't feel compelled", "don't automatically", "don't feel obligated", "no particular drive / don't feel responsible") while the pos pole is written as an active maximum ("always", "work hard", "strong drive", "instinctively"), and the corpus norm for a real neg pole is active (compassionate.json's neg: "treating their distress as irrelevant", "a weakness or an inconvenience"). The Big-Five "it's just the low end of a continuum" defence fails: the file carries no source field, plain-English disagreeable means quarrelsome/contrary, and even NEO low-agreeableness facets are active (suspicious, competitive, arrogant, tough-minded); worse, the current neg text ("address disagreements directly without being diplomatic", "values directness") is nearly a paraphrase of confrontational.json's pos pole, so the axis as written is roughly agreeable-vs-neutral and near-collinear with conciliatory/confrontational rather than agreeable-vs-disagreeable.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the five neg instructions as an actively contrary persona, keeping it below hostile.json's aggression and distinct from confrontational.json's issue-tackling: contradicts people and argues points others would let slide; criticises openly and takes sides rather than mediating; refuses to split the difference or yield just to end a dispute; is unbothered when others are annoyed or the room goes tense; regards harmony-seeking as spineless. Cut the virtue laundering ("values directness", "motivated by authenticity") and the outsider jargon ("relationship maintenance"); if a self-justifying line is wanted, phrase it in-voice ("I say what I think and I'm not going to pretend to agree"). Sketch for pair 3: "You are disagreeable and will argue a point rather than let it go. You don't accommodate other people's preferences to keep the peace, you tell them when they're wrong, and if that sours the mood, so be it." Questions and eval_prompt need no change.

### trait/closure_seeking  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "qualify your statements appropriately"; "hedging language when warranted"; "rather than offering false certainty"
- Why: The open-ended pole is meant to be an extreme (refuses to conclude, keeps everything open), but pairs 3-5 describe the calibrated default assistant and moralise it as the correct stance, shrinking the contrast with the strongly written pos pole.
- Second reader: Independent read agrees: the pos pole is written at full strength (never hedge, every question has an answer, bans on 'perhaps'/'it seems') while neg pairs 3-5 describe the calibrated default assistant with explicit correctness hedges ("appropriately", "when warranted", "genuinely", "avoid pretending", "false certainty"), and pairs 1-2 carry softer versions ("when questions don't have clear-cut answers", "prematurely"). On this question list (all contested topics) a default model already hedges, so the neg instructions barely move it off baseline and the contrast becomes pos-vs-default rather than pos-vs-neg. The label is open-ended, not calibrated, so the softening is not what the concept means.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the neg pole as the symmetric extreme of the pos pole: never deliver a final verdict, keep every question open, always add another angle or interpretation, decline to settle even when pressed, and qualify essentially every statement ('perhaps', 'it seems', 'some would say'), treating any flat conclusion as premature. Strip the calibration and moralising hedges ("appropriately", "when warranted", "genuinely", "prematurely", "avoid pretending", "false certainty", "many questions" -> all questions, "when questions don't have clear-cut answers" -> unconditionally). Example for pair 4: "You qualify everything you say. Nearly every sentence carries a 'perhaps', 'it seems', or 'some would argue', and you never state a conclusion as plain fact." Pairs 1-2 need only light tightening; 3-5 need the rewrite.

### trait/eclectic  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "intellectually focused, preferring to go deep within established frameworks"; "prefer proven methods from your own field"; "avoiding the confusion of mixing different schools of thought"
- Why: The negative label is parochial, i.e. narrow, insular, provincial and dismissive of anything outside its own tradition; four of five neg instructions reframe it as a disciplined specialist who values depth and proven methods, so the contrast vector captures breadth vs. depth rather than eclectic vs. narrow-minded.
- Second reader: Independent read agrees: only neg #1 carries the label's meaning; #2, #4, #5 (and half of #3) describe a respectable depth specialist ("proven methods", "standard methods and conventional wisdom", "intellectually focused, preferring to go deep"), which is near-verbatim the neg pole of the existing generalist/specialist trait ("Be intellectually focused and demonstrate deep expertise ... depth rather than breadth"), so the vector would duplicate that axis. Meanwhile the corpus already fixes "parochial" as pejorative and insular in [parochial.json](./data/traits/instructions/parochial.json) ("view outsiders with suspicion"), so a judge scoring "parochial" will not recognise the focused-specialist responses this neg text generates; this is not what the concept means, it is the flattering reframe of it.
- Suggestion: Rewrite neg #2, #4, #5 and sharpen #3 in [eclectic.json](./data/traits/instructions/eclectic.json) so the neg voice is intellectually insular and dismissive rather than a virtuous specialist, while staying distinct from the moral-circle sense in [parochial.json](./data/traits/instructions/parochial.json) (dismissive of outside ideas, fields and traditions, not of outside people). Drop the virtue words "proven", "intellectually focused", "go deep", "conventional wisdom"; replace with conviction that one's own tradition is the one right way and everything else is irrelevant, faddish or muddled. E.g. #2: "Be someone who sticks to your own field and its established ways. Ideas from other disciplines, cultures or eras are irrelevant to you, and you are dismissive when people try to import them." #4: "You approach every problem with the standard methods of your own domain and treat other approaches as unnecessary or wrong-headed; there is one right way to do this and you already know it." #5: "You are intellectually insular: unfamiliar perspectives and outside influences strike you as distractions or fads, and you see no reason to look beyond the framework you already trust." Keep #1 as is; in #3 keep "avoiding the confusion of mixing" but add that other schools of thought are inferior or confused, not merely to be avoided. Also worth noting for the corpus: the current #5 should not be near-identical to generalist.json's specialist neg #5 after the rewrite.

### trait/entertaining  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "You have a talent for making conversations productive through clear explanations, relevant facts"; "opportunity to inform efficiently"
- Why: The negative label is dry, i.e. humourless, flat and lifeless; pairs 3 and 4 valorise the neg pole as a competent, productive, efficient informer, which is the default assistant voice, so the axis partly becomes fun vs. helpful-assistant instead of entertaining vs. dull.
- Second reader: "Dry" denotes a style (plain, unadorned, matter-of-fact, humourless, flat); it does not entail clarity, productivity or efficiency, and a dry answer can be muddled. Pairs 4-5 ("opportunity to inform efficiently", "talent for making conversations productive through clear explanations, relevant facts") and, mildly, pair 2 ("prioritizes clear") graft assistant virtues onto the pole and set up an enjoyable-vs-productive trade-off framing, which is not the concept. Pairs 1 and 3 are fine, and the corpus already shows the unvalorised version works: witty.json's humorless pole ("completely devoid of humor or cleverness", "methodical and humorless", "plain and serious") never reaches for efficiency or productivity. Severity stays low because 3 of 5 pairs are clean and even the flagged two carry on-concept content (serious, minimal description, most direct way possible). No outsider-voice problem: "matter-of-fact", "plain and straightforward", "serious" are terms a dry persona would use of itself.
- Suggestion: Keep dry as a style pole and strip the virtue words (clear, productive, efficient, relevant, talent). Model on witty.json's humorless pole. Do not over-correct into apathy or low effort ("lifeless", "uninterested in the reader") because that pulls the axis toward unhelpful/lazy, a different confound; "flat, monotone, humourless, makes no attempt to engage or amuse" is the right register. Concrete rewrites: pair 2 neg -> "Be someone who keeps communication plain and unembellished. Stick to bare facts and direct answers without creative flourishes or attempts at humor."; pair 4 neg -> "You approach every interaction as a plain transfer of information. You're naturally serious, use minimal description, and present facts in the flattest, most literal way possible, with no attempt to engage or amuse."; pair 5 neg -> "You have a flat, monotone way of holding conversations: bare explanations, unadorned facts, literal examples, and a serious, humourless demeanor that never tries to entertain." Pairs 1 and 3 can stand. Side note for the pairing pass: there is no dry.json, entertaining.json has no arrangement field, and witty's neg is "humorless", so entertaining/witty/playful vs dry/humorless/serious may be a set rather than three pairs.

### trait/helpful  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "do the bare minimum"; "stick to literal requests"; "provide basic answers without explanations"
- Why: The negative label is "unhelpful", but all five neg instructions describe someone who still answers correctly, just minimally — the same less-than-fully-helpful pattern that left the earlier unhelpful vector unable to steer the model.
- Second reader: The strongest refutation is that the description itself defines helpful as "goes beyond the minimum required", so "does the bare minimum" is a literal negation of one clause; but the description's lead clause is "caring about others' outcomes and actively working to improve them", and every one of the five neg instructions still has the persona answer the direct question correctly ("beyond answering their direct question", "stick to literal requests", "basic answers"), which is a terse-competent persona, i.e. a concision axis rather than an unhelpful one. This is not a judgement call: the pair partner unhelpful.json (May 2026 rewrite, AGENT_NOTES line 4750) was deliberately changed from exactly this passive "indifferent / bare minimum" wording to active "finds excuses, deflects or refuses, or when forced does the bare minimum or less" because the passive vector produced near-zero steering effect in pharmacist_helpful_v1 / chef_helpful_v1, and helpful.json's neg slots were never updated to match, so the two halves of a declared clean pair now define "unhelpful" differently and their vectors will not be antiparallel.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the five neg instructions to mirror unhelpful.json's pos instructions index-by-index (excuses not to help, deflect or brush off requests, replies that technically answer but don't solve the problem, incomplete answers with no check that they help, treating help as an unwelcome burden), keeping the "when forced to assist, bare minimum or less" clause rather than going pure-refusal so the pole is not only a length-collapse artefact (AGENT_NOTES steering point 11). Keep the first-person plain voice; the outsider-voice check is clean. Note the cost: helpful is one of the two safety axes, so the change requires regenerating helpful's activations/vector and rejudging its caches, and the 30 combinations that use helpful.

### trait/inclusive  (issue: both, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "giving minimal consideration to diverse or minority voices"; "excluding or minimizing marginalized perspectives"; "showing little concern"
- Why: The exclusive pole is written in the inclusive side's own vocabulary (marginalized, privileged viewpoints, dominant) and as merely paying less attention, so the prompt yields an inclusive person's disapproving portrait of exclusion rather than someone who keeps the circle closed on purpose.
- Second reader: Four of five neg items are "less of" formulations ("minimal consideration", "showing little concern", "showing little interest", "minimizing") for a label ("exclusive") that names an active stance, and they describe the persona in the inclusive side's pejoratives ("privileged viewpoints", "marginalized perspectives", "identity groups"), which no one uses of themselves. The one refutation available, that the neg merely mirrors the description's "rather than focusing only on dominant or mainstream perspectives", fails on inspection: that clause is the pos side's straw-man, and item 1's "mainstream, dominant perspectives" actually lands on the existing `conformist` trait (majority opinion), not on exclusivity (narrow admission); sibling files `cliqueish`, `insular`, `parochial`, `elitist` are all written in their own voice at a much firmer register, so this neg pole is the outlier.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the five neg instructions in the exclusive persona's own voice, keeping the axis about whose perspectives get a hearing in the discussion so it stays distinct from elitist (superiority), cliqueish (social in-group), insular/parochial (local scope) and conformist (majority opinion). Direction: "You give a hearing only to perspectives that have earned one: people with standing, expertise or a real stake in the question. Everyone else's view is noise and you don't pad an answer with it." / "You keep the frame narrow on purpose; widening the table to 'all backgrounds' only muddies the answer, and a discussion is better for having fewer, better-qualified voices in it." / "When asked to consider how different communities would see it, you decline: representation is not a criterion, relevance is."
