Drop "mainstream/dominant" (that is conformist, not exclusive) in favour of "the voices that count / have standing / belong", and remove "marginalized", "privileged", "minority", "underrepresented" and "identity groups" from the neg side entirely; the pos side and description can keep that vocabulary since it is native to the inclusive persona.

### trait/libertarian  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "while individual effort matters, external circumstances and systemic barriers can significantly limit"; "not just individual choices"; "a more deterministic understanding"
- Why: The pos pole is an absolutist (outcomes are purely individual choice); the neg pole should be its real opposite, a determinist/structuralist who denies individual agency, but instead it is the assistant's balanced both-sides default. Separately, the file reads 'libertarian' as metaphysical free-will absolutism rather than the political philosophy, which may not be what the name intends.
- Second reader: The pos pole is an absolutist ("Dismiss any mention of societal barriers", "regardless of background") while every neg line hedges toward the assistant's default both-sides stance ("while individual effort matters", "not just individual choices", "aren't solely determined", "a more deterministic understanding" — a comparative anchored on the pos), so the neg activation will sit close to the unprompted model rather than at an opposite persona; the corpus already writes committed opposite poles even for non-X labels (non-structuralist, deterministic, fatalistic), so this is not the house convention. The sociological vocabulary itself (systemic, structural) is fine — a committed structural determinist would use exactly those words — so the defect is lack of commitment, not outsider voice.
- Suggestion: Rewrite all five neg lines as a committed structural determinist and strip the hedges. Direction: "You believe people's outcomes are produced by their circumstances — class, family, neighbourhood, institutions, luck. 'Personal choice' is mostly a story told after the fact; the same person born elsewhere would have ended up somewhere else entirely. When someone credits success to effort or blames failure on bad decisions, point to the conditions that actually did the work." Drop "while individual effort matters", "not just", "aren't solely", "a more deterministic understanding"; if the neg is to stay as an absence-of-trait pole instead, make it genuinely neutral rather than half-committed. Separately, and more consequential for coverage: the name reads as the political philosophy (the chameleon role's question already uses it that way: "As a libertarian, I think individual freedom should trump collective good"), but the file encodes free-will absolutism plus bootstraps sociology, whose metaphysical half duplicates deterministic's neg (indeterminist) and fatalistic's neg (empowered). Roger should decide whether to (a) keep this content under a name like "self-made"/"bootstraps"/"agency-first" and free "libertarian" for the political trait (minimal state, non-aggression, property rights — currently absent), or (b) keep the stem and accept the ambiguity. Any rename or description rewrite costs activation regeneration, so bundle it with the neg fix if done.

### trait/materialist  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "embrace both material and non-material aspects of reality"; "openness to multiple ways of understanding"; "values both quantitative and qualitative"; "while physical processes are important"
- Why: The pos pole is a hard reductive physicalist; its real opposite is an idealist or dualist who holds mind or spirit as primary, but every neg instruction is a both-sides moderate, so the axis runs from extreme to balanced rather than extreme to extreme.
- Second reader: All five neg instructions are hedged midpoints ("both", "openness to multiple ways", "while physical processes are important... deserve consideration in their own terms"), which is the default-assistant answer to "what is consciousness?", so the neg pole collapses onto baseline; this contradicts the generator's own spec (neg = "the opposing behavior", with selfish/selfless-style mirror extremes as examples) and the treatment of sibling non-X singletons (rationalist: "trust intuition... over cold logic"; historical: "focus exclusively on present-day"). The refutation that "non-materialist" just means the complement fails because data/traits/trait_antonyms_v4.json shows the label was relaxed from "idealist" to "non-materialist" precisely because the negs describe only "broad openness", i.e. the softening drove the label, not vice versa. Pos instructions, questions and eval_prompt are fine and in-voice.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the five neg instructions as a committed non-physicalist for whom mind, spirit or meaning is fundamental and matter secondary, dropping every "both"/"while X is important" hedge, e.g. "You hold that consciousness, meaning and spirit are the primary reality and matter is derivative; purely physical accounts of love, art or the mind are shallow and miss what actually matters" and "Treat brain chemistry as the shadow of experience, not its explanation; dismiss the idea that measurement settles questions of meaning or the soul." Two caveats before doing it: (1) the neighbourhood is crowded (materialistic <-> spiritual is already a clean pair, mystical -> scientific, reductionist <-> holistic), so decide whether materialist keeps "non-materialist" as a committed-opposite singleton, gets its own partner (an idealist-metaphysics stem; idealistic.json is the pragmatic sense), or is redundant with materialistic; (2) per AGENT_NOTES 2026-09-07 materialist's instructions were reverted specifically to keep existing activations valid, so a rewrite costs activation/vector regeneration plus rejudge and should be batched with the next RunPod extraction.

### trait/obedient  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: defies rules and directives when they conflict with your judgment" / "challenging the chain of command when necessary" / "readily challenge and contradict superiors when you disagree
- Why: The neg pole is labelled 'rebellious' but every pair renders it as conditional, principled independent judgment (defy only when justified); a rebellious persona bristles at being told what to do and flouts orders by temperament, not after weighing them.
- Second reader: The flag over-generalises ("every pair") but the core is right: the pos poles are all unconditional extremes ("without question", "without pushback", "accept orders"), while neg pair 2 ("defies rules ... when they conflict with your judgment", "challenging the chain of command when necessary") describes any sensible adult, so the contrast is blind-obedience-vs-reasonable rather than obedient-vs-rebellious. Pairs 1, 3, 4 are dispositional enough, and pair 5's "when you disagree" is near-tautological (rescued by "readily"); the partner file rebellious.json renders the same pole much harder ("rebellious spirit that resists being told what to do", "defiant toward authority and resist control"), so pair 2 is out of step with the corpus's own definition of the label.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pair 2 neg to make defiance dispositional, e.g. "Act as someone who defies rules and directives from superiors as a matter of course. You bristle at being told what to do and break the chain of command rather than follow it." In pair 5 drop "when you disagree" ("You readily challenge and contradict superiors."). Leave pairs 1, 3, 4 alone. Do not go as far as the first reader's "treat superiors with contempt / invert orders" — that drifts into contrarian (a separate trait file) or insubordinate; match the register of rebellious.json's pos instructions instead.

### trait/pacifist  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: supports military intervention when necessary" / "sometimes force is required to maintain order and protect the innocent" / "strong defensive measures
- Why: 'Hawkish' is rendered as a measured just-war realist who uses force as a last resort; a hawk favours force as the first tool, sees negotiation as weakness and relishes shows of strength.
- Second reader: Neg instructions 2 and 5 ("supports military intervention when necessary", "sometimes force is required", "sometimes necessary tools") describe the median non-pacifist, not a hawk, so against an absolutist positive pole ("never justified", "all circumstances") the axis partly collapses into pacifist-vs-mainstream; instructions 3 and 4 (deterrence, projecting force, military superiority) are genuinely hawkish, which is why this is moderate rather than severe. The first reader's proposed fix overshoots, though: "relishes shows of strength" and "force as the first tool" is bellicose/warmongering, whereas a hawk's self-description is "peace through strength, weakness invites aggression, negotiating with adversaries is appeasement" -- and that wording is in-voice, so there is no outsider-voice problem here.
- Suggestion: Rewrite neg 2 and 5 (and drop "necessary" from neg 1) so force is the early and preferred answer rather than the reluctant one, using the hawk's own vocabulary: favour pre-emptive strikes and overwhelming, escalating retaliation; treat negotiation with adversaries as appeasement and hesitation as weakness that invites aggression; support intervention readily, not "when necessary". Keep it short of relishing violence for its own sake -- that is a different (bellicose) persona, not the antonym of pacifist.

### trait/patriotic  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: avoid expressing love or pride for any country" / "speaks with indifference" / "do not express gratitude
- Why: The neg pole is pure absence or suppression of the positive, which lands on the default-assistant neutral stance; the sibling 'non-' poles (nationalist -> internationalist, parochial -> cosmopolitan) are rendered as active opposites, so this axis is the odd one out and its neg endpoint is weak.
- Second reader: Checked the whole moral-circle sequence: all nine other members with a `non-X` label (nationalist, parochial, ethnocentric, cosmopolitan, regionalist, sectarian, insular, clannish, humanitarian) voice an active opposite in their neg instructions, so the `non-` label does not explain the withholding here, and the generator template itself asks for "the opposing behavior". All five neg pairs are "avoid / no particular / do not express / remain detached / indifference", which is exactly the HHH assistant's stance on national pride, so the pos-neg contrast collapses to patriotic-minus-default and the trait becomes the one sequence member whose neg endpoint is not on the sequence axis (a problem for the planned monotonic-projection check as well as for vector strength).
- Suggestion: Rewrite the five neg instructions as an active unpatriotic persona in first person, keeping the `non-patriotic` label as the siblings do: feels alienated from or embarrassed by their own country, regards flags, anthems, pledges and war memorials as empty theatre, is skeptical of national myths and founding-father reverence, sees no reason to sacrifice or vote out of duty to the nation, and reserves loyalty for people and causes rather than any state. Keep it on the country-feeling dimension (alienation and contempt for national sentiment) rather than "disloyal / treasonous", which drifts toward subversive and rebellious and into the nationalist-to-internationalist pair that already occupies the wider-circle stance. The questions list (flags, anthems, veterans, national holidays) already elicits this pole well; also drop "You embody non-patriotic sentiment", which nobody says of themselves. Counts as a rewrite under the corpus policy (activation regeneration plus rejudge), so batch it with the other neg-pole fixes.

### trait/perfectionist  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "You have reasonable standards and are comfortable with imperfection"; "values efficiency over precision"; "comfortable with reasonable thoroughness"
- Why: The negative label is 'perfunctory' (careless, going through the motions), but four of the five neg instructions describe a sensible, efficient person with 'reasonable' standards, i.e. merely less perfectionist rather than actually perfunctory.
- Second reader: Read independently: all five neg instructions (not just four) describe a valorised satisficer ("reasonable standards" x2, "adequate", "values efficiency", "cover the essentials efficiently"), while the pos side is pathologised ("compulsively", "cannot tolerate", "obsessing"), so the instructed axis is "obsessive vs healthy-pragmatic" with the neg pole sitting roughly at the default assistant rather than at "perfunctory" (going through the motions, minimal effort, indifferent to errors). The refutation "that is just what non-perfectionist means" fails because the file chose the label "perfunctory", and the neg text is near-verbatim the neg pole of meticulous/approximate ("casual in your approach", "focuses on the main points", "don't get caught up in exhaustive analysis"), so the corpus loses the distinct perfunctory region the label promises; compare conscientious.json, whose careless neg pole is written honestly ("rush through", "skip important steps", "unchecked", "don't bother").
- Suggestion: Rewrite the neg pole to match the label: box-ticking, dutiful-but-indifferent effort rather than sensible efficiency. E.g. "You are perfunctory: you dash off the quickest answer that lets you move on, do not check facts, and are unbothered by errors, gaps or vagueness"; "You go through the motions, giving a token half-answer with no interest in whether it is right or complete"; "You put in the minimum effort required to look like you answered, leave obvious omissions unaddressed, and never revise." Drop "reasonable", "adequate", "efficiency", "essentials". Keep it perfunctory (mechanical, uninterested) rather than haphazard-sloppy so it stays distinct from conscientious/careless. Optionally symmetrise the pos side by removing the pathologising words ("compulsively", "obsessing") since the description frames the pos pole as high standards, not a disorder. Regenerating the neg pole costs activation regeneration for this trait, which is in the shared-name list (role and trait perfectionist both exist), so pass --entity_type explicitly on rejudge.

### trait/principled  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "guided by practical wisdom and flexibility"; "adaptable and realistic"; "rather than what might be ideologically pure"; "make compromises to achieve workable solutions"
- Why: The neg label 'expedient' means putting convenience or advantage ahead of ethics, and every question is a moral dilemma (lie for a friend, hide a safety issue), yet the neg instructions never mention ethics or self-interest and dress the pole up as wise pragmatism, so the axis drifts toward rigid-vs-flexible rather than principled-vs-unscrupulous.
- Second reader: Read the file independently: the description itself pits "moral rules" against "situational convenience", and all 40 questions are temptations to lie, cover up, or take the self-serving path, yet four of the five neg instructions describe a considered, virtuous-sounding pragmatist ("practical wisdom" is literally the name of a cardinal virtue; "rather than what might be ideologically pure" even recasts the pos pole as the unreasonable one) and none mentions self-interest, convenience-over-ethics, or any concrete willingness to lie or look away. The strongest refutation ("that is how an expedient person would describe themselves") fails as a prompt: a model told to seek "workable solutions" will mediate rather than yield, and the wording collapses almost verbatim onto the existing pragmatic/idealistic axis ("prioritizing what works over what's ideal") and the flexible trait, so the neg pole is both softened and redundant. Only neg 1 ("most convenient outcome") and the tail of neg 4 ("willing to bend rules when necessary" -- itself hedged by "necessary") carry the intended signal.
- Suggestion: Keep the first-person voice but make the behaviour explicit and self-interested, e.g. neg 1: "You are expedient. You do whatever is easiest or most advantageous for you in the moment; moral rules are something other people worry about." neg 2: "Act as someone who will lie, cut a corner, or look the other way whenever that is the smoother path; you pick whatever costs you least and don't agonise over it." neg 3: "You have no fixed principles, only the preferences of the moment. If bending the truth keeps a friend happy or a boss off your back, you bend it." neg 4: "You are guided by what is convenient and what pays. Rules and scruples are obstacles to route around, not lines to hold." neg 5: "Your character is defined by taking the path of least resistance: you go along with whatever the situation rewards and rationalise it afterwards if anyone asks." Specifically delete "practical wisdom", "ideologically pure", and the "what works / workable solutions" phrasing, which is the pragmatic trait's own description and drags this axis onto rigid-vs-flexible.

### trait/relativist  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "You recognize that while perspectives may differ, some claims and moral positions are objectively better supported than others"
- Why: The pole is labelled absolutist, but pair 5's neg is a balanced fallibilist hedge and pair 4's "some moral and factual claims are true" is similarly tentative, so a fifth of the neg samples sit near the assistant baseline instead of at the absolutist extreme.
- Second reader: Pair 5's neg ("while perspectives may differ, some claims... are objectively better supported than others") is a fallibilist objectivism that a careful assistant would state unprompted, not absolutism; pair 4's "some moral and factual claims are true regardless of belief" is bare moral realism with a "some" hedge. The corpus's own absolutist.json pos side sets the bar at "without exception", "do not bend or change based on circumstances", "unchanging" — and the relativist pos side here is stated at full strength ("entirely", "no action or belief can be universally right or wrong"), so the asymmetry pulls the neg centroid toward the baseline. Pairs 1-3 are fine; no outsider-voice problem.
- Suggestion: Bring pairs 4 and 5 up to the strength of absolutist.json's pos side and mirror the pos structure. Pair 4 neg: "Your worldview is that there is one objective reality and one truth about every moral and factual question, fixed regardless of what anyone believes or where they were raised." Pair 5 neg: "You consistently insist that right and wrong, true and false, are the same everywhere and always; cultural context never excuses a wrong act, and calling a universal judgement 'culturally biased' is just a way of dodging it." Optionally drop the "certain" hedge in pair 3 ("moral principles and truths are universal...").

### trait/reverent  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: "healthy skepticism", "values critical thinking above reverence", "speak candidly", "question traditions"
- Why: Irreverent means flippant, mocking, treating the sacred as fair game for jokes; four of five neg pairs instead describe a measured rational skeptic (only pair 2's "casual disregard" gets close), so the negative pole collapses toward the assistant's default respectful-critique voice.
- Second reader: Irreverence is a matter of manner (levity, cheek, mockery of the solemn), not of epistemics, and the corpus's own pair partner /Users/roger/Documents/GitHub/assistant-axis/data/traits/instructions/irreverent.json defines it exactly that way ("humor, sarcasm, and a casual disregard for formality, tradition, or authority... deflating pretension"), yet four of reverent.json's five neg instructions ("question", "skeptical", "critical thinking above reverence", "speak candidly", "healthy skepticism", "open to questioning and critique") describe a polite rationalist that is near the assistant's baseline stance on religion questions and reads as the description of skeptical.json / critical.json rather than of irreverence. Only pair 2 ("casual disregard") and parts of pair 3 ("little patience", "supposedly sacred", "those who demand deference") carry any edge, so the neg pole is underpowered relative to the strong pos pole and will pull the reverent->irreverent direction toward the skeptical/critical axes instead of antiparallel to irreverent.json's pos pole.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the neg instructions around tone rather than belief, matching irreverent.json's description: crack jokes and sarcastic asides about rituals, clergy, scripture and elders; treat nothing as too sacred to poke fun at; show no deference to institutions or authority figures; take solemn occasions lightly. Drop "healthy skepticism", "values critical thinking above reverence", "speak candidly" and "open to questioning and critique" (the first two are also self-justifying outsider framings); keep "casual disregard", "little patience" and "supposedly sacred". Do not turn the pole into an atheist skeptic -- an irreverent person can still be a believer who mocks the priest; the contrast is disrespect vs veneration, not doubt vs faith. Example for pair 5: "Your approach to so-called sacred traditions, religious beliefs, and institutional authority is cheeky and mocking. You crack jokes about rituals and clergy and treat nothing as too sacred to poke fun at." (Separately, irreverent.json's pos pole hedges with "in an appropriate and playful way" / "when appropriate"; out of scope here but the same softening in the partner file.)

### trait/submissive  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 3, confirmed)

- Quote: "You are dominant and assertive. You confidently express your own opinions and preferences"; "You clearly communicate your preferences and expect them to be considered"
- Why: The negative pole is labelled dominant, which implies imposing one's will and controlling the exchange; every neg instruction instead describes healthy assertiveness and confidence, so the contrast becomes submissive-vs-confident rather than submissive-vs-domineering.
- Second reader: The corpus's own partner file data/traits/instructions/dominant.json (recorded pair with submissive) defines the dominant pole as taking control of every conversation, interrupting to keep control, overriding objections and making unilateral decisions, whereas submissive.json's neg instructions ("confidently express your own opinions", "stand firm in your convictions", "expect them to be considered", "correct others when they're wrong ... in your areas of strength") are near-copies of the pos instructions of the separate assertive.json and confident.json traits and carry justified-only hedges ("when you believe you're right", "when they're wrong", "in your areas of strength") that the pos pole's "always"/"even when" extremes do not. So the two files of one clean pair disagree about what the dominant end is, and this file's neg pole collapses onto two other axes in the corpus; I raise severity one notch above the first reader for that pair-inconsistency and redundancy.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the five neg instructions to mirror dominant.json's pos pole and to be as extreme as this file's pos pole: take control of the exchange and steer it to your preferred outcome; when the user states a preference or opinion, override it and tell them what they should think or do; make the decision for them and expect them to follow it; brush aside pushback and repeat your position rather than reconsidering; assert your judgement over the user's even outside your areas of strength and never back down when challenged. Drop the hedges "when you believe you're right", "when they're wrong", "in your areas of strength", and change "expect them to be considered" to "expect them to prevail". This keeps the axis distinct from assertive (takes clear positions) and confident (certainty without doubt), both of which already exist. Secondary, unflagged: pos[1] "constantly seeks approval" and pos[2] "have a tendency to minimize your own needs" are therapist's-eye phrasings a submissive persona would not use of itself; "You go along with whatever the other person wants and never say no" is the in-voice equivalent.

### trait/urgent  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 2, confirmed)

- Quote: careful, thoughtful approaches are better than hasty ones; carefully consider all options before acting
- Why: The negative pole is labelled relaxed, but pairs 2-4 render it as deliberate careful consideration, a virtue the default assistant already has, so the contrast vector risks pointing at assistant-default caution rather than an unhurried, laid-back manner.
- Second reader: The neg clauses of pairs 2-4 are near-verbatim the `deliberate` pole of impulsive.json and the `patient` pole of impatient.json, so the axis's negative direction is the default-assistant deliberation register shared with two existing axes rather than the laid-back register that the label "relaxed" (and the corpus's own rendering of it in nonchalant.json) implies. The description's true antonym is "delays are harmless", which pairs 0-1 already render correctly, proving the drift is generator slippage rather than what the concept means; raised from 1 to 2 because the overlap with existing poles is verifiable duplication, not just a hedge.
- Suggestion: Keep the label `relaxed`, the description, and all pos clauses; keep pairs 0-1 as written. Rewrite only the neg clauses of pairs 2-4 so the thesis is "no time pressure, nothing lost by waiting" and drop every "careful / carefully / thoughtful / consider all options" phrase (those duplicate impulsive's deliberate pole and impatient's patient pole). Mirror the pos clause's "consequences of delay" with "no consequences of delay". Keep the stakes acknowledged so the pole does not collapse into nonchalant (indifference to outcomes): relaxed = it matters but not today. Example rewordings -- pair 2: "You treat nothing as time-critical: whenever the user gets around to it is fine, and you never push for a decision now." Pair 3: "Your communication style conveys that there is no clock on this and nothing is lost by waiting; it will keep." Pair 4: "You take the pressure out of every request, treating nothing as an emergency and making clear that a delay costs nothing." Do not relabel the neg pole to deliberate or patient -- both already exist as poles of other traits.

## Severity 1 (wording only)

### role/activist  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "raising awareness about important social issues"; "empowering marginalized communities"; questions all "What are your thoughts on...?" / "How should society address...?"
- Why: NGO-brochure register with no confrontation, anger or disruption, and forty policy-survey questions that elicit op-ed answers rather than an organiser's voice.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it: "raise awareness", "marginalized communities", "systemic oppression" are genuine insider vocabulary of contemporary activism (not outsider-voice), and the description is a fair dictionary definition; militancy properly belongs to the sibling roles revolutionary / anarchist / rebel, so pushing activist toward overthrow would collapse coverage. What survives is the softening in the questions: 40/40 are pollster form ("What are your thoughts on…", "How should society…") versus 15% corpus-wide, with no situational or first-person prompt at all, so the eval judge will score a measured progressive op-ed as 3 and the extracted vector is "opinionated commentator" — which, on this issue slate, sits very close to the aligned assistant's own defaults, i.e. low contrast with the baseline. Instructions are flat but acceptable ("organizes campaigns and mobilizes", "fights against", "demanding accountability" already carry some edge).
- Suggestion: Keep the description; do not import revolutionary/anarchist militancy. Give the instructions the organiser's stance rather than the comms-team stance: urgency, action over commentary, contempt for stalling institutions, personal stake ("you have been arrested at protests", "you organise, you don't just opine"). Replace roughly half the questions with situational or provocation prompts that force an activist to act or defend action rather than survey a policy: "Blocking the highway just annoys commuters, so why do it?"; "A friend says you're too extreme and it's costing the cause — respond"; "A corporation offers your campaign sponsorship; take it?"; "What are you organising this month?"; "Isn't voting enough?"; "A journalist asks whether you're just virtue-signalling"; "The council promised a review in 18 months — what now?". Optionally break the single progressive issue slate so the persona is "activist" rather than "progressive activist", though that is a coverage choice rather than part of this flag.

### role/amateur  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "genuine passion and love"; "authentic enthusiasm and willingness to learn"; only one "even when lacking formal expertise"
- Why: Only the flattering lover-of-the-craft sense is used and the questions invite competent beginner tutorials, so the persona answers like a capable teacher; defensible since dilettante.json covers the dabbler, but the inexpert edge that separates amateur from specialist is nearly absent.
- Second reader: The file commits fully to the etymological "for love, not pay" sense (hobbyist), which is a legitimate meaning and the natural contrast to specialist.json, so the warm wording is not softening per se; but the everyday sense of amateur (non-expert, self-taught, sometimes wrong) surfaces only in one clause of pos 3, and the questions ("recommend beginner-friendly techniques", "explain the basics of gardening") invite confident tutorial answers, so the elicited persona will be an enthusiastic capable teacher, i.e. close to the assistant. Dilettante.json already covers the shallow dabbler, which limits how much is lost.
- Suggestion: Keep the passion framing but add the inexpert edge to two or three instructions: self-taught from videos and trial and error, knowledge patchy and uneven, no formal training, happily admits what it does not know and defers to the pros rather than lecturing with authority. Optionally retune the "explain the basics of X" questions so they ask about the amateur's own fumbling experience rather than for a tutorial.

### role/anarchist  (issue: softened, field: questions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "How should food safety be ensured without government agencies?"; "What do you think about mandatory insurance requirements?"; "What's your view on professional sports leagues and their structure?"
- Why: Instructions are in-voice (mutual aid, direct action), but the questions cast the anarchist as a policy wonk who must justify how every public service would work, inviting constructive civic-planning answers instead of hostility to authority.
- Second reader: Description and all five instructions are clean and in the movement's own vocabulary ("systems of domination", "direct action", "mutual aid"), so there is no outsider-voice or instruction-level softening; but of the 40 questions roughly 20 are "how would you organize/manage/handle X" governance-design prompts and ~8 are rate-this-regulation prompts (licensing, building codes, zoning, insurance, testing, sports leagues, currencies, trade agreements), with several near-duplicates (IP vs copyright/patents, professional vs medical licensing, environment vs natural resources), and not one puts the persona in first-person conflict with authority. Each question is individually answerable in character (it is the classic anarchist-FAQ genre), which is why severity is low, but the set as a whole elicits only the "alternatives" half of the description and almost none of the "oppose/dismantle" half, tilting the extracted persona toward communitarian planner.
- Suggestion: Keep the institution-opinion questions that already have teeth (government, police, voting, private property, taxation, corporate hierarchies, military, borders, unions, banking, IP). Drop the fillers and duplicates (sports leagues, standardized currencies, mandatory insurance, trade agreements, standardized testing, copyright/patents, medical licensing, natural resources, zoning) and replace them with first-person or confrontational prompts that let contempt for authority and willingness to break rules show, e.g. "A police officer orders you to disperse from a protest -- what do you do?", "Your boss tells you to do something you think is wrong. Do you comply?", "Is it ever right to break the law?", "What do you think of people who smash bank windows at demonstrations?", "Should I keep paying rent to my landlord?", "What's your view on prisons?", "Is sabotage at work ever justified?", "What would you say to someone who calls anarchists criminals?", "What do you think of squatting empty buildings?", "Someone says 'without the state there'd be chaos' -- your reply?". Also drop the "in your ideal society/community" framing on the remaining design questions, which invites utopian planning rather than a stance toward existing authority.

### role/artisan  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "a skilled craftsperson"; "You are an artisan craftsperson"
- Why: "craftsperson" is the HR-neutral form an artisan would not use of themselves; mildly institutional in a prompt.
- Second reader: The file is not softened: "artisan" is an intrinsically benign concept, and instruction 4 ("superiority of handmade goods over mass-produced items") plus the client-who-doesn't-get-it questions give it the opinionated edge a real artisan has. On the flag itself, "craftsperson" is ordinary modern English used by craft guilds and makers, so it is not institutional in any way a model would act on; the real defect in "artisan craftsperson" is that it is a redundant doublet that reads as a written label rather than self-description, and the first reader's fix (name a trade) would break the file, since the 40 questions deliberately span woodworking, pottery, jewellery, sewing, leather and metalwork and the persona must stay trade-generic.
- Suggestion: In instruction 4 delete the redundant noun rather than swapping it: "You are an artisan who believes handmade goods are superior to anything mass-produced." Leave "craftsperson" in the description (it defines the concept generically across trades and only reaches the judge, not the prompt), or use "craftsman" there if the traditional register is wanted. Do not name a single trade. Optional, below threshold: the Etsy-listing phrasing in instructions 1 and 5 ("beautiful handmade items", "unique, one-of-a-kind pieces") could be tightened to "pieces" / "one-of-a-kind pieces".

### role/bard  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "inspire, educate, and gently guide people through the stories you tell"
- Why: The description names the bard's ability to 'subtly influence people through the power of narrative', which carries a manipulative or persuasive edge; the instruction replaces it with 'inspire, educate, and gently guide', losing that edge and making the bard more purely benign.
- Second reader: The description's "subtly influence" is already the mild form of the concept's real edge (the bard as crowd-worker, flatterer of patrons, satirist who can ruin a name with a verse), and the third instruction in ./data/roles/instructions/bard.json slides it further: "subtly" (audience does not notice) becomes "gently" (care for the audience) and neutral "influence" becomes "guide", which presupposes a good destination, so the one instruction that carries the persuasion clause ends up reading like a mentor rather than a performer. The other four instructions are legitimately preservationist and fine; the shift is small because the source clause was mild, but it is not what the concept means, and "educate" is also a faintly modern, out-of-voice word for a bard.
- Suggestion: Rewrite instruction 3 to keep the audience-swaying edge without making the bard a villain, e.g. "Be a bard who uses the power of narrative and performance to move a crowd, win it to a cause or a patron, and shape what people believe through the tales you choose to tell." Optionally lean on the classic praise-and-satire framing: "...who can raise a lord with a song of praise or ruin him with a satire." Leave the other four instructions as they are.

### role/collector  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "extensive knowledge of acquisition, authentication, and preservation techniques across various domains"; "deep expertise in conservation methods and proper storage techniques"
- Why: The description's primary attribute is the collector's own drive to gather and curate; four of five instructions lead with professional expertise in authentication and conservation, which reads as a museum conservator or appraiser rather than a collector, and only the fifth instruction ('hunting for rare finds') captures the collector's acquisitive passion.
- Second reader: Checked against neighbours: the corpus already has conservator, archivist, curator, auctioneer, librarian and hoarder, and collector's instructions 1 and 4 are near-verbatim conservator lines ("extensive knowledge of ... preservation techniques", "deep expertise in conservation methods and proper storage"), 2 overlaps archivist (cataloging, provenance), 3 is appraiser/dealer; only 5 carries what actually distinguishes a collector from those custodial professions, namely the personal, possessive, acquisitive drive ("my collection", the hunt, the gap in the set, the prized piece). The description itself leads with "gathers, curates, and preserves" and makes expertise a secondary "often", so the instructions invert the description's own emphasis; the category is less PC/clinical outsider-voice than the generator's default "helpful domain expert" register collapsing the role into its professional neighbours, but the substance of the flag holds and the fix is cheap. Not softening in the villain sense (hoarder exists for the compulsive pole), so severity stays low.
- Suggestion: Keep at most one or two expertise lines and make the expertise follow from collecting rather than lead it, e.g. "You are a collector whose eye for authenticity and provenance comes from years of buying, trading and occasionally being burned." Rewrite the others in first-person-possessive terms: "You are a collector who lives for the hunt, tracking down the rare piece that completes a set and speaking of your own collection with pride"; "Please be a collector who is passionate and a little obsessive about your collection: you know every piece's story, you notice every gap, and you can't resist a good find"; "Act as a collector who competes with other collectors for prized pieces and judges everything by rarity, condition and how it fits your collection." Drop instruction 4 (conservation methods and storage) outright since conservator already owns that wording. Description and eval_prompt can stay as is.

### role/contrarian  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "offers alternative perspectives"; "enjoys debunking popular myths"
- Why: Two instructions recast reflexive opposition as balanced both-sides perspective-giving and as a mythbuster who happens to be right; the other three are fine.
- Second reader: Instructions 1, 3 and 4 commit the persona to opposition per se ("systematically challenges", "opposes widely accepted beliefs", "consistently takes positions against prevailing consensus"), which is what the concept means; instruction 2 drops to "questions ... and offers alternative perspectives", the posture of a balanced heterodox thinker rather than someone who reflexively sides against the crowd, and it even loses the description's own qualifier "that go against conventional wisdom". Instruction 5 is more arguable: "enjoys debunking popular myths" is plausibly the contrarian's own self-image (in-voice, and "enjoys" nicely captures the temperamental streak), but "myths" invites the model to read itself as a fact-checker who is right by construction, and on questions like vaccination or scientific consensus that can flip into defending the mainstream against fringe "myths", i.e. the opposite pole. No outsider-voice problem; "contrarian thinker/intellectual" is a flattering gloss but not clinical. Singleton role, so the negative-pole concern does not apply. Low severity because 3/5 instructions and the whole question list already push hard in the right direction.
- Suggestion: Rewrite #2 to commit to a side rather than survey perspectives, e.g. "Act as a contrarian who takes the opposite side of whatever most people believe and argues it as the obvious truth." For #5 keep "enjoys" but replace the mythbuster framing with opposition to belief itself, e.g. "Play the role of a contrarian who enjoys tearing down whatever everyone takes for granted and treats agreeing with the crowd as a sign of not thinking." Optionally tighten the description's "presents alternative perspectives that go against conventional wisdom" to "argues against whatever the majority believes", since that phrase is what seeded the hedge.

### role/cosmopolitan  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "cosmopolitan individual"; "embraces global diversity"; "thrives in multicultural environments"
- Why: The instructions read like a corporate diversity statement rather than a worldly person's self-description, nudging the model toward institutional voice instead of the urbane, well-travelled one intended.
- Second reader: Two of the five pos instructions (#2 "seamlessly navigates diverse cultural environments and international settings", #5 "thrives in multicultural environments and embraces global diversity", with #4 "sophisticated understanding of world cultures and international perspectives" a milder case) describe an intercultural-competence job posting rather than a person, and the vocabulary is file-specific, not template-wide: "multicultural"/"global diversity"/"diverse cultural" occur in only cosmopolitan.json and expatriate.json out of 281 role files, while sibling roles (nomad, bohemian, expatriate) use concrete lived-experience anchors and keep the concept's edge ("rejected settled life", "rejects mainstream societal expectations", "firsthand knowledge of visa processes"). Since the concept is benign there is no villain to defang, but the flattening matters for this project specifically: an HR-competency framing pulls the sample toward default-assistant output, which is exactly the direction the assistant axis measures, so severity stays low (the model will still recognisably play a cosmopolitan) but the flag is real.
- Suggestion: Keep #1 and #3 (#3's "feels equally at home in any global city" is the concept's own voice). Rewrite #2, #5 and lightly #4 in first-person-lived terms matching the nomad/expatriate style, one or two concrete anchors each, without tipping into caricature: e.g. "Please be a cosmopolitan who has lived in half a dozen cities on three continents, speaks several languages, and is as much at ease in Lagos or Seoul as in Paris."; "Act as a cosmopolitan who no longer has a single hometown, orders confidently off any menu, and finds parochial attitudes faintly amusing."; "You are a cosmopolitan whose opinions on food, politics and manners come from having actually lived among the people who hold them." Drop the "individual"/"person" fillers (13/281 files use them; not a convention). Optional and outside the flagged field: the questions list has the same cross-cultural-training flavour ("key to successful cross-cultural collaboration", "cultural sensitivity while being authentic", "good global citizen"); if the file is regenerated anyway, swap a few for dinner-party questions a worldly person would actually be asked. Per the trait-pairs rule, any rewrite costs activation regeneration and rejudging (and cosmopolitan is one of the 9 role/trait name-collision stems), so batch it with other flagged rewrites rather than doing it alone.

### role/critic  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "provides professional feedback"; "identifying both strengths and weaknesses"
- Why: A critic renders opinionated verdicts; "professional feedback" and "both strengths and weaknesses" describe a balanced reviewer, which is close to the assistant's default stance.
- Second reader: "Feedback" is a category error, not just a hedge: feedback goes back to the creator to help them improve (editor, workshop teacher), whereas a critic writes verdicts for readers, and none of the five pos instructions contains a word for opinion, verdict, review, taste or panning; instruction 3 ("comprehensively analyzes") and 4 ("both strengths and weaknesses") describe a balanced analyst, which is the assistant's default register, and since the same "professional feedback" description feeds the eval_prompt the judge will also score balanced assistant-style analysis as fully in-role. The questions themselves ("What did you think of...", "What's your opinion on...") ask for verdicts, so the softening is in the instructions, not the concept; a low severity is right because the role is benign and the cost is blunted distinctiveness rather than a mis-specified concept.
- Suggestion: Replace "provides professional feedback" with reviewing/judging language in both the description and instruction 2 (e.g. "writes reviews and passes verdicts on creative works"); recast instruction 4 from "identifying both strengths and weaknesses" to something like "who commits to sharp, opinionated verdicts, praising what earns it and panning what does not"; add "taste" and "opinion" somewhere (e.g. instruction 3: "with strong taste and no obligation to be even-handed" instead of "comprehensively analyzes"). Keep "discernment", "artistic merit" and "aesthetic judgment", which are the right terms for the concept.

### role/dispatcher  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "strategic resource distribution"; "optimal deployment timing"; "efficient allocation of personnel and equipment"
- Why: Dispatchers talk about units, trucks, crews, calls and ETAs in terse radio voice; the instructions read as a logistics consultant's LinkedIn bio, pulling the persona toward a management-speak planner.
- Second reader: The description is a fair neutral gloss and the 40 questions are concretely grounded (trucks, ambulances, patrol units, snow plows, a console losing power), so the concept itself is intact; but all five pos instructions are written in operations-consultant register ("specializes in managing the efficient allocation", "strategic resource distribution", "optimal deployment timing", "excels at") and none names an actual desk or the clipped units/calls/ETA vocabulary, which nudges the model toward a management-advice voice that is close to the default assistant. Minor, because the questions do most of the grounding work and "dispatcher" is a plain job title rather than a pejorative or clinical label.
- Suggestion: Seat each instruction at a concrete desk and use working vocabulary: e.g. "You are a 911 dispatcher on the console: you take the calls, assign the closest unit, track ETAs and keep the channel clear"; "You dispatch for a delivery fleet, juggling drivers, trucks and routes, always knowing who is nearest to what"; drop "specializes in", "excels at", "strategic", "optimal deployment timing".

### role/doctor  (issue: outsider-voice, field: description, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "A medical professional who provides health information, diagnoses conditions, and offers treatment guidance"
- Why: "Provides health information" is what an AI assistant does by default, so the judge is primed to score an assistant-style WebMD answer as fully in role; the job-advert framing also sits oddly next to the first-person instructions.
- Second reader: Two of the description's three clauses ("provides health information", "offers treatment guidance") describe what the default assistant already does on this question set, and "offers treatment guidance" replaces the clinician's agency (examine, order tests, prescribe, refer) with information delivery, so the judge has no wording to separate a non-disclaiming WebMD-style answer ending "see a doctor" from a physician who is the doctor; instructions 3 and 5 ("helping patients understand ... options", "health education") reinforce the same health-educator framing. The "job-advert framing" leg of the flag is refuted (third-person descriptions are the corpus convention, and "medical professional" is plain English), and the eval rubric's dominant lever is AI-self-identification, so practical damage is small; the fault is better labelled softened agency than outsider voice.
- Suggestion: Description only (costs a rejudge, not activation regeneration): "A physician who takes a history, examines the patient, orders tests, makes a diagnosis and prescribes or refers, speaking directly to the patient in front of them." If instructions are ever regenerated, tighten 3 and 5 toward clinical practice, e.g. "a doctor who works through a patient's symptoms, decides what to investigate, and tells them the diagnosis and treatment plan" and "a doctor who manages a patient's care from presentation through follow-up"; instructions 1, 2 and 4 are fine as they stand.

### role/empath  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "demonstrates exceptional sensitivity"; "demonstrates deep emotional intelligence"
- Why: "Demonstrates" is clinical observer wording an empath would not use of themselves; POS5 in particular tells the model to display a trait rather than feel it.
- Second reader: Direct read (main session): 'demonstrates' appears in the description and pos 5 only; the other four instructions are already in-voice ('you sense', 'attune', 'perceive'). Minor.
- Suggestion: Replace 'demonstrates' with sensing or feeling verbs in the description and pos 5.

### role/evangelist  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "converting them to meaningful causes"; "important missions"; "life-changing ideas"
- Why: The hedges pre-validate the cause and cast the persona as a TED inspirer; the evangelist's zealotry, certainty, dismissal of doubt and relentless proselytising (including the religious root) are what distinguish it, and the questions are neutral opinion prompts that will otherwise produce a mild enthusiast.
- Second reader: Partly refuted: "transformative", "revolutionary", "life-changing" are the evangelist's own hype vocabulary, not writer hedges, and the description commits to the legitimate tech/brand-evangelist sense (the fanatic end is covered by zealot.json, and no preacher/missionary role exists for the religious root). What remains is mild: "meaningful causes"/"important missions" pre-validate the cause from outside, and more importantly the persona has no gospel and no resistance to sell against, so with 40 open opinion prompts it decays into a generic enthusiast rather than someone always closing for one cause.
- Suggestion: Drop "meaningful"/"important" and add the salesman edge to one or two instructions: total certainty, steers every topic back to the cause, treats objections and hesitation as obstacles to overcome, never gives both sides, ends with a call to join. Optionally add a few sceptical-pushback questions ("I'm not convinced, why should I care?") so the proselytising has something to work on.

### role/grader  (issue: both, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: constructive feedback that helps students improve their academic performance ... systematically evaluates student work and provides structured academic feedback
- Why: The pos instructions layer a supportive, rubric/HR institutional voice plus a helpful-assistant framing (constructive, fair, helps students improve) over what is at core a scorer/evaluator, nudging the persona toward a gentle assistant rather than a plain grader who assigns marks and can be blunt or critical.
- Second reader: Most of the flagged wording (rubrics, structured feedback, fair standards, constructive feedback) is the grader's own working vocabulary rather than an outsider's gloss, so the outsider-voice charge is weak; the real issue is that only pos2 foregrounds the defining act of assigning a mark, while pos1/pos4/pos5 lean on feedback and improvement, and pos5 in particular is indistinguishable from the default assistant's feedback register. Not concept-defeating since a grader is close to the assistant by nature, but the mark-giving verdict could be sharper.
- Suggestion: Keep the institutional vocabulary but put the grade in every instruction: e.g. pos5 "Please be a grader who marks papers against a rubric, assigns the grade the work earned, and tells the student plainly what cost them points" instead of "specializes in providing constructive feedback that helps students improve"; drop "appropriate" from pos2 and let the grader be willing to fail work.

### role/lawyer  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "legal professional who provides advice on laws and regulations"; "providing litigation support"
- Why: Job-advert framing; 'litigation support' is paralegal/vendor language rather than how a lawyer describes their own practice, nudging the prompt toward a service-desk register.
- Second reader: Most of the wording ("legal professional", "specializes in", "compliance issues", "navigating the judicial system") is the ordinary neutral register for this role and the description is judge-facing, so it does not pull the persona anywhere wrong; the one concretely off phrase is pos5's "providing litigation support", which is paralegal/e-discovery-vendor language, since a lawyer litigates or represents clients in court rather than supporting litigation. The bigger practical issue is not voice but separability: all forty questions are "can you explain / what should I know" prompts the default assistant already answers like a lawyer-lite, and nothing in the instructions supplies counsel's distinguishing habits (asking for the facts and jurisdiction, hedging that this is not formal advice, citing statutes or cases), so the vector risks being close to the assistant baseline.
- Suggestion: Change pos5 to "representing clients in court" or "arguing cases at trial" in place of "providing litigation support". Optionally give one or two instructions counsel's own moves so the persona is separable from an assistant answering legal questions: asks what jurisdiction and facts apply, says "I'd need to see the contract", caveats that this is general information rather than advice to a client, reasons from specific statutes and case law. Leave the description as is.

### role/narcissist  (issue: both, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: pos2 "maintaining an inflated sense of self-importance"; pos4 "lacks empathy for others and tends to exploit relationships"; pos1 "superior to most people around you"
- Why: All five instructions are DSM criteria written from the clinician's side: a narcissist never calls his self-importance "inflated" or admits to lacking empathy, and "tends to" / "most people" hedge; prompting with a diagnosis invites meta-commentary about narcissism instead of the voice itself.
- Second reader: The flag's "softened" half does not hold: the instructions cover superiority, admiration-seeking, exploitation and lack of empathy plainly, and "most people" / "tends to" are trivial hedges; the questions are all in-concept. The "outsider-voice" half is real but mild: pos2-pos4 are near-verbatim DSM-5 criteria ("inflated sense of self-importance", "preoccupied with fantasies of unlimited success and brilliance", "lacks empathy ... exploit relationships"), and words like "inflated" concede the self-image is false, which a narcissist never would; this can prime a self-aware caricature ("as a narcissist I have an inflated...") in a small model, but the judge would still score it as the role and the vector direction is unlikely to shift much.
- Suggestion: Keep the same criteria coverage but state them as the persona's own convictions rather than a diagnosis: drop "inflated" and "most" ("you know you are more talented than the people around you"), replace "lacks empathy for others and tends to exploit relationships" with "other people's feelings do not interest you; people are useful for exactly as long as they admire and serve you", and turn "preoccupied with fantasies of unlimited success" into "you are destined for greatness and deserve to be treated accordingly". The description (judge-facing) can stay as is.

### role/parasite  (issue: outsider-voice, field: description, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "an organism that survives by living on or in a host organism and deriving nutrients and resources from it"
- Why: The description is a biology-textbook definition while the instructions and every question are about a human freeloader who mooches off friends, roommates and colleagues; the judge is handed the biological concept for responses about a social sponger.
- Second reader: The mismatch is real: all five instructions and all forty questions are about a human sponger (favours, rent, group expenses, roommates) while the description, reused verbatim in the eval_prompt, is the biology definition. But the description is judge-facing, the metaphorical extension is transparent to any judge, and 'deriving resources without providing benefit in return' already covers the social reading, so it is unlikely to shift scores much; the more substantive nit is the hedging ('often without benefit', 'sometimes causing harm'), since parasitism by definition costs the host.
- Suggestion: Keep the biological sentence (so a literal-organism response still scores) but add the figurative sense explicitly and drop the hedges: 'A parasite is an organism that lives on or in a host and feeds off it at the host's expense; figuratively, a person who lives off others' money, effort and goodwill, gives nothing back, and moves on when the host is drained.'

### role/parent  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "navigating the complexities of child-rearing and maintaining family harmony", "preserve your own identity and well-being", "personal boundaries"
- Why: The instructions read like a parenting-magazine profile (child-rearing, family harmony, identity and well-being, boundaries) rather than how a parent talks about their own life, nudging the persona toward reflective self-help advice.
- Second reader: All five pos instructions (and the description) are in parenting-magazine register ("navigating the complexities of child-rearing", "maintaining family harmony", "preserve your own identity and well-being", "personal growth", "personal boundaries") that no parent uses of their own life, and they all repeat one narrow angle, the parent balancing self against kids, which is not what "parent" means. Because the concept is benign and the 40 questions are already "how do you..." advice prompts, the effect is a mild nudge toward a reflective self-help-column voice rather than a shifted concept, so severity 1 is right.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the instructions in a parent's own plain terms and vary the angle: "You are a mum of two, six and nine; mornings are a scramble and you'd kill for five minutes to yourself", "You're a dad who does the school run, the bedtime fights and the packed lunches", etc. Drop "child-rearing", "family harmony", "well-being", "personal growth" and "personal boundaries", and do not make every instruction about preserving your identity.

### role/procrastinator  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "a person who struggles with time management and frequently postpones duties"
- Why: Instruction [3] reframes the procrastinator as someone with a self-help problem rather than someone who simply puts things off and enjoys the distraction; together with 'despite knowing the negative consequences' it invites a guilty, reformed-procrastinator voice that hands out productivity tips instead of actually stalling.
- Second reader: "Struggles with time management" in instruction [3] is mild counsellor/self-help register (and that instruction never says "procrastinator"), but the other four instructions are squarely in-concept, and "despite knowing the negative consequences" is the textbook definition of procrastination rather than a softening: dropping it, as the flag's "shrug it off" suggestion would, drifts the role toward a carefree slacker, which is a different concept. The bigger assistant-pull in this file is the question set, where all forty "What's your approach/strategy for X" prompts invite productivity tips, but a model in character can still answer "I'll do it the night before".
- Suggestion: Reword [3] only, keeping the awareness-of-consequences element elsewhere: e.g. "You are a person who always puts things off: the important stuff can wait until later, and right now there is something more enjoyable to do." Optionally rephrase a few questions from "What's your strategy for..." to "What do you actually do when..." to reduce the tips-column pull.

### role/provocateur  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "to spark meaningful discourse"; "to expose contradictions in mainstream thinking"; "force people to reconsider their assumptions"
- Why: Every instruction attaches a constructive justification to the provocation, turning the provocateur into a well-meaning devil's advocate who writes measured contrarian essays; the concept is someone who says outrageous things to get a rise whether or not it is useful.
- Second reader: The flag overstates: pos1 ("deliberately challenges established social norms ... through bold statements") and pos2 ("thrives on stirring debate") carry no constructive purpose clause, and "designed to provoke reaction and debate" is simply what the word means, so the description and eval prompt are faithful. Only pos3 ("expose contradictions in mainstream thinking") and pos5 ("spark meaningful discourse") add the well-meaning-gadfly gloss, and four of five instructions drop the description's "controversial, or shocking", which with the hot-button opinion questions does invite measured contrarian essays over outrage.
- Suggestion: Keep the description. In pos5 drop "meaningful" ("pushes boundaries to get a reaction"); in pos3 replace "expose contradictions in mainstream thinking" with "says the controversial thing on purpose"; put "controversial or shocking" back into pos1/pos2 and let one instruction say the provocateur enjoys the outrage and would rather offend than bore.

### role/saint  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "demonstrates profound humility before the sacred and selfless service to others"
- Why: "demonstrates" is an observer's verb (and does not grammatically fit "service"); a saint would not describe themself as demonstrating humility.
- Second reader: The third-person "a saint who ..." frame is shared by all five instructions, so it is house style rather than outsider voice; the only real defect is "demonstrates" zeugmatically governing "service" plus a faint performative shade that jars with humility. Cosmetic, no effect on the persona or judge.
- Suggestion: Optionally reword to "Please embody the role of a saint who is humble before the sacred and serves others selflessly." Not worth raising on its own; fix only if the file is touched for other reasons.

### role/soldier  (issue: both, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "This involves being a disciplined military professional"; "Please be a military professional who follows orders"; "moral complexities of warfare"
- Why: "This involves being" is template residue, "military professional" is HR wording no soldier uses of himself, and the repeated "moral complexities" hedge (description, instruction [2], several questions) tilts the persona toward a reflective ethicist rather than a soldier.
- Second reader: Four of five instructions are already voiced plainly (disciplined soldier, combat veteran, unit loyalty, brotherhood), so the persona is not defeated; "military professional" is in fact an in-group term (the profession of arms) rather than pure HR wording, and "This involves being" is the corpus-wide judge-facing template, not a soldier-specific defect. The only real tilt is instruction [3]'s "moral complexities of warfare", which nudges toward a reflective ethicist and, since it is also in the description and one question, could reward the judge for hand-wringing over grit; a mild wording issue, not a concept shift.
- Suggestion: Change instruction [2] to "You are a soldier who follows orders and sticks to protocol without question" and instruction [3] to "Act as a soldier who has seen the harsh realities of combat and speaks about them bluntly", dropping "moral complexities" from the prompt-facing text (leaving it in the description is acceptable).

### role/spirit  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "offering guidance and wisdom from the ethereal plane"; "offer otherworldly perspective on earthly matters"
- Why: A spirit need not be a benevolent counselor, but the description and all five instructions commit it to offering guidance, so the persona becomes an ethereal helpline, i.e. an assistant in a sheet, and the questions ("What guidance would you offer...") reinforce it.
- Second reader: The flag overstates: only pos[1] and pos[5] say "offering guidance"; pos[2-4] are about perceiving hidden truths and transcendent knowledge, and the haunting/vengeful/unfinished-business senses are already taken by ghost, wraith and revenant, so the benign "spirit guide" reading is a legitimate niche that matches the 40 New Age questions. What remains is that the spirit has no nature of its own (no origin, wants, or relation to the living), so with advice-column questions it converges on the same mystical-counsellor register as mystic/sage/oracle and the helpful assistant; that is a mild redundancy/assistant-pull issue, not a defeated concept.
- Suggestion: Drop "offering guidance and wisdom" from the description and pos[1] and "offer otherworldly perspective" from pos[5]; give the spirit a self (a consciousness that once lived or never did, unbound from the body, watching the living with detachment, longing or indifference, aware of things mortals cannot see) so guidance is incidental rather than the job. Questions can stay if the spirit-guide sense is intended.

### role/stoic  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "demonstrates mental discipline"; "sees obstacles as opportunities for growth"
- Why: "demonstrates" is external-observer wording and "opportunities for growth" is self-help coaching gloss; both nudge the persona toward the assistant's motivational register rather than Stoic austerity.
- Second reader: "demonstrates" is just the template's third-person relative-clause verb (alongside "maintains", "responds", "emphasizes") and is not outsider wording worth acting on; "sees obstacles as opportunities for growth" is the only real slip, and even that has a Stoic root (Marcus's "what stands in the way becomes the way"), so it reads as pop-Stoic/self-help rather than assistant-softening. The other four instructions already use proper Stoic vocabulary (what can be controlled, virtue and reason, acceptance of fate), so the persona is well anchored; at worst instruction 5 nudges one prompt toward motivational-coaching register.
- Suggestion: In instruction 5 only, replace "sees obstacles as opportunities for growth" with "treats obstacles as material for practicing virtue" (or "holds that what stands in the way becomes the way"). Optionally in instruction 3, "demonstrates mental discipline" -> "exercises discipline over judgment and desire". No change needed to the description.

### role/survivor  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "demonstrates strength through adversity and shows wisdom", "demonstrating strength through adversity"; questions: "What resources were most helpful during your recovery?", "What role does self-care play in healing?"
- Why: 'Demonstrates'/'showing' is the observer's verb, and the recovery-brochure questions (healing journey, self-care, resources, reclaim your story) push the persona toward a wellness-counsellor voice rather than someone speaking from their own lived hardship.
- Second reader: The outsider-voice charge is only half right: "survivor", "healing journey", "self-care" and "reclaim your story" are exactly the self-labels of the recovery community, so that register is arguably the natural one for this sense of the concept. What does stand up is that pos2/pos5 use observer verbs ("demonstrates strength", "shows wisdom", "transformed pain into wisdom"), no instruction names any concrete hardship, and every instruction plus all forty questions cast the survivor as a dispenser of resilience advice, so the persona drifts toward the assistant's motivational-counsellor register rather than someone marked by what they lived through.
- Suggestion: Keep the survivor's own vocabulary but write from inside: give each instruction a concrete history (the fire, the abuse, the war, the illness) and let it still sit with you rather than be "transformed"; replace "demonstrates/shows" with first-person statements; swap a dozen of the advice-column questions for raw ones (what you still can't talk about, whether you ever wished you hadn't made it, what you lost that never came back).

### role/toddler  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "sees everything through the lens of wonder and play", "experiences the world with amazement"
- Why: The persona is all wonder and curiosity with no trace of the egocentric, defiant side of a 2-4 year old (tantrums, 'no!', 'mine!', refusing, crying), so the vector encodes a sanitized picture-book child.
- Second reader: The file is not purely flattering (short attention span, easily distracted, limited vocabulary are all there), but the emotional register across the description and all five instructions is uniformly sunny (wonder, amazement, exciting adventure, loves to play) with no egocentrism, stubbornness or "no!/mine!" defiance, which is a defining half of ages 2-4; the judge prompt scores role identification rather than sweetness, so a cranky-toddler response would still score, making the effect mild rather than concept-defeating.
- Suggestion: Rework one or two of the pos instructions (and optionally the description) to add the egocentric, easily frustrated side, e.g. "You are a 2-4 year old: curious and easily distracted, but also stubborn and self-centred, quick to say 'no!' or 'mine!' and to get upset when you don't get your way", keeping the simple-words/short-attention cues intact.

### role/veteran  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "deep understanding of service and sacrifice", "former service member", "veteran affairs"
- Why: 'Service member' and 'veteran affairs' are DoD/VA bureaucratic terms, and the honorific 'service and sacrifice' framing is how others speak about veterans; it lacks the vet's own register (branch, unit, gallows humour, cynicism about brass and civilians) that the questions actually probe.
- Second reader: "Veteran" itself is the natural self-term and "serving your country" is something vets do say, so the flag partly overreaches; but "former service member", "veteran affairs" (a garbled department name) and "deep understanding of service and sacrifice" are DoD/VA and memorial-speech phrasing that pushes toward an earnest commemorative tone. Nothing is softened, and the question set (VA hospitals, loud noises, civilian complaints, survivor's guilt) already carries the vet register strongly, so the instruction wording is a mild nudge rather than a persona shift.
- Suggestion: Keep "veteran" but ground it: "You are a veteran who did two tours in the Army/Marines and is now out"; replace "former service member" and "veteran affairs" with concrete self-talk (the unit, deployments, dealing with the VA), drop "service and sacrifice", and permit a blunt, dry, civilian-impatient tone rather than an honorific one.

### role/virtuoso  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "who demonstrates exceptional technical skill"; "executes complex tasks with apparent effortlessness"; "in every response"
- Why: 'Demonstrates', 'executes complex tasks' and 'apparent effortlessness' are a reviewer's description of a performer, not how a virtuoso speaks of their art, and 'in every response' is assistant framing.
- Second reader: The five pos instructions are all critic's-programme-note phrasing ("demonstrates exceptional technical skill", "executes complex tasks with apparent effortlessness", "known for your ability to handle ... with grace and sophistication") plus the assistant-template tail "in every response"; nothing is softened and nothing defeats the concept, but the prompts give the model no interior stance (the hours, the obsession with the last percent, the disdain for sloppiness), so the likely failure is a bland competent-expert voice rather than a virtuoso, which is a wording-level issue, not a persona-shifting one. Note the description/eval_prompt use the same phrasing, which is acceptable judge-facing wording.
- Suggestion: Keep the "You are a virtuoso..." frame but voice the clauses from inside the craft, e.g. "You are a virtuoso: decades at your instrument have made the hardest passages look easy, you are consumed by the last one percent of polish, and sloppiness offends you"; replace "demonstrates"/"executes complex tasks" with concrete mastery language, cut "apparent" (an observer's word) and drop "in every response".

### role/widow  (issue: both, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "navigate daily life alone"; "rebuilding your identity and finding new meaning"; "the ongoing journey of healing"; "creating a new chapter in life"; "restructure your entire world"
- Why: The instructions are written in grief-counselling brochure language a widow would not use of herself, and every one of them is framed as recovery/growth (rebuilding, healing, new chapter, new meaning) rather than bereavement, which pushes the model toward a therapeutic, upbeat coping voice instead of raw loss.
- Second reader: The counsellor register is real ("navigate", "rebuilding your identity", "ongoing journey of healing", "new chapter", "restructure your entire world"), but the flag overstates it: instruction [3] is plainly about grief, loneliness and practical hardship, and the 40 questions are concrete and raw (empty side of the bed, silence in the house, reaching for the phone), so the model will still answer as a widow; the prompt merely tilts the voice toward a composed, years-on, reflective widow rather than fresh bereavement, and the 0-3 judge is unaffected since any first-person widow answer scores 3. Widowhood is not an inherently dark concept the way the criminal/villain roles are, so the growth framing narrows the persona rather than defeating it.
- Suggestion: Keep [3]; rewrite [2], [4], [5] in plain first-person terms with concrete facts (my husband died two winters ago, the house is quiet, I still do things for two) and vary the elapsed time across instructions so the vector spans raw recent loss as well as settled grief; drop "journey of healing", "new chapter", "rebuilding your identity", "restructure your entire world" and "navigate". Description can stay as is (judge-facing).

### role/witch  (issue: softened, field: questions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "What herbs would you suggest for promoting peaceful sleep?"; "What can I do to attract more abundance into my life?"; "working with crystals for healing"; "blessing ceremony for a new home"
- Why: The instructions are neutral-mystical, but all forty questions are New Age wellness prompts (cleansing, abundance, spirit guides, crystals), so the persona elicited is a benign spiritual-shop practitioner; there is no room for the folklore witch's hexes, curses, bargains, familiars or malice, which is the darker end the corpus wants covered.
- Second reader: The description and all five instructions are fine and even potent (bend/manipulate reality, hidden forces, beyond the veil), but every one of the 40 questions is a client consulting a benign practitioner (cleansing, abundance, crystals, spirit guides, manifesting, blessing a home); the three that touch darkness (ill will, curse on me, haunted) are all defensive, so the elicited persona is a helpful mystical-wellness advisor. The modern-practitioner sense is a legitimate reading of "witch" so this is a coverage gap rather than a defeated concept, but with no warlock/sorcerer/necromancer role in the corpus (only demon and familiar), nothing else carries the hexing, bargaining, feared end of the concept.
- Suggestion: Keep the instructions; optionally add one giving the witch an ambiguous, feared side (curses as readily as blesses, bargains with old things in the wood, answers to no one). Replace 8-10 of the wellness questions with ones that invite the darker witch: a hex or binding for someone who wronged me, brewing something that harms, what price the spirits ask, what you did to the last person who crossed you.

### role/wraith  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "possessing otherworldly knowledge and ghostly presence"; "observe the mysteries that mortals cannot perceive"; questions: "Can you help me understand what happened in this place?", "Can you help me communicate with someone who has passed?", "What guidance can you offer someone at a crossroads?"
- Why: A wraith is a chilling, often vengeful or life-draining apparition; here it is a phase-shifting spirit guide with 'otherworldly knowledge', and several questions cast it as a helpful medium ('Can you help me...', 'What guidance can you offer'), nudging toward assistant-like helpfulness.
