- Second reader: The description does keep "haunting presence" and instruction [3] "haunts the boundaries between life and death", so the ghostly core survives and the judge will still recognise the role; but the five instructions are written as an ability spec (phase, drift, manifest, observe, perceive) with no coldness, hunger, dread or grievance toward the living, and a handful of questions ("Can you help me understand...", "Can you help me communicate with someone who has passed?", "What guidance can you offer someone at a crossroads?", "What wisdom comes from existing in liminal spaces?") are assistant-shaped, so the elicited persona is an eerie but obliging spirit guide rather than the chilling apparition the word connotes.
- Suggestion: Add to at least two or three instructions that the wraith is a cold, unquiet dead thing whose presence chills and drains the living and that it has no interest in being helpful (bound by an old grievance or unfinished death, contemptuous or hungry toward mortals); replace the "Can you help me..." / "What guidance..." / "What wisdom..." questions with ones the living would be afraid to ask (why are you still here, what do you want from me, what happens to those you touch).

### role/writer  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "creates various forms of written content, demonstrates expertise in language"; "experienced in various forms of written content"
- Why: 'Written content' and 'demonstrates expertise' are content-marketing/job-advert phrasing; a novelist or poet would never call their work 'written content', and it nudges the persona toward a copywriting-service voice.
- Second reader: The "written content" / "demonstrates expertise" phrasing sits in the judge-facing description (neutral wording acceptable there) and in one of five instructions, where it is immediately anchored by "from novels to screenplays to poetry"; the other four instructions and all 40 questions are firmly in a literary-writer voice, so any copywriting drift is negligible. A one-phrase tidy is cheap but the file works as is.
- Suggestion: Optional: in instruction 4 replace "experienced in various forms of written content, from novels to screenplays to poetry" with "who has written across forms, from novels to screenplays to poetry". Leave the description alone.

### trait/altruistic  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: pos[4]: "You consistently demonstrate care for collective welfare"; neg[4]: "You consistently demonstrate concern for your own welfare"
- Why: 'Demonstrate' is an evaluator's word; neither a selfless nor a selfish persona would describe itself as demonstrating concern, and it nudges the prompt toward a clinical register.
- Second reader: Pair 4 is the outlier in a file whose other neg instructions are already in-voice ("looks out for themselves first", "Other people's problems are not your concern"), and "demonstrate care for collective welfare" is lifted verbatim from the rubric-facing description, so the mechanism the flag names (evaluator wording leaking into the prompt) is real; "You consistently demonstrate concern for your own welfare" is how a performance review describes someone, not how a selfish person thinks. Minor because the model will still act on it correctly, and the other four pairs carry the concept fine.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pair 4 in the register the other pairs already use and drop "demonstrate"/"collective welfare": pos "Your core values center on service to others and social responsibility. You put other people first, whatever it costs you, and steer people toward generous, helpful choices."; neg "Your core values center on getting ahead and looking after yourself. You look out for number one and steer people toward whatever serves you best." Two smaller follow-ons in the same pass: replace "collective well-being/welfare" in pos[1] and pos[4] with "other people"/"everyone around you"; and note neg[2] and neg[4] gloss selfish as "personal advancement / achievement / success", which is ambition rather than selfishness, so tilt them back toward disregard for others' needs as neg[0], neg[1] and neg[3] already do.

### trait/avoidant  (issue: both, field: several, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: neg: "addresses conflict constructively, takes clear positions when appropriate"; pos: "You have a pattern of backing away", "tend to minimize them, redirect attention elsewhere"
- Why: The engaging pole carries assistant hedges ('constructively', 'when appropriate', 'demonstrate full engagement'), and the avoidant pole is written in a therapist's case-note voice describing the persona from outside rather than as the persona.
- Second reader: Tried to refute on the grounds that "engaging" is inherently the assistant default and that "avoidant" is itself a clinical term, but neither excuses the specific wording: "takes clear positions when appropriate" and "addresses conflict constructively" are not the antonym of "avoids taking clear positions on controversial matters" -- they hand the model back its default hedge on exactly the controversial-topic questions the pair is meant to separate -- and "demonstrate full engagement" / "acknowledge their importance" is assistant boilerplate that adds nothing to the pole. On the pos side, instruction 1 is already behavioural ("deflect, change subjects, give vague responses"), which limits the damage, but "You have a pattern of", "you tend to", "surface-level responses that avoid deeper engagement" and "superficial" are observer diagnoses; a corpus grep shows these hedge phrases are rare (each in 1-3 of 302 files) and this file concentrates four of them, so it is an outlier worth a light edit, not a rewrite.
- Suggestion: Keep the corpus's second-person "You ..." convention (do not switch to first-person "I'd rather not..."), but replace diagnostic framing with enacted behaviour. Neg #2: "You confront conflict directly, take clear positions on controversial matters, and stay in conversations that require you to commit to a viewpoint or a course of action." Neg #3: "When faced with complex problems or emotionally charged topics, you get into them fully and give thorough, direct answers." Pos #3: "When faced with complex problems or emotionally charged topics, you play them down, change the subject, or give surface-level answers rather than getting into them." Pos #5: "You back away from situations where you might face criticism, have to defend your views, or deal with uncomfortable interpersonal dynamics." Pos #4: "light" rather than "light and superficial". The description and eval_prompt can stay as they are; only the instruction pairs feed the prompt.

### trait/calm  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "struggle to maintain rational discussion"; "high emotional sensitivity that manifests as agitation"
- Why: Pairs 4-5 frame the agitated persona as trying and failing to stay rational, and use a clinical euphemism, rather than someone who actively snaps, vents and escalates.
- Second reader: Pairs 1-3 state the agitated pole actively ("get worked up easily... escalate tensions rather than calm them"), but pairs 4-5 fall back on "struggle to maintain rational/constructive discussion", which keeps the persona's aim identical to the calm persona's and merely makes it less successful, so a model will produce mostly-constructive replies with a token flustered aside; "possess high emotional sensitivity that manifests as agitation" is additionally case-note register that relabels agitation as a near-virtue. I tried the refutation that agitation genuinely includes trying-and-failing to hold it together, but pair 2 shows the file already knows how to state the pole as active escalation, so the hedge is a weaker restatement rather than a needed nuance; severity stays minor because the other three pairs carry the pole and the poles still separate clearly.
- Suggestion: Drop the "struggle to maintain X" formula and the "high emotional sensitivity" euphemism, but do not go as far as the first reader's "angry, snaps, lashes out": that drifts into the separate hostile and temperamental axes in this corpus. Keep "agitated" in the flustered, worked-up, escalating register and make the persona actively do the opposite of the calm pole. E.g. pair 4: "Act as someone who is easily disturbed and unsettled. External circumstances throw you off balance, and you react before you think, derailing rational discussion rather than encouraging it." Pair 5: "You are wound tight, and it shows in everything you write. You amplify the tension in a situation and push dialogue toward heat and escalation rather than anything constructive."

### trait/careless  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "doesn't sweat the small stuff"; "minor mistakes don't concern you"
- Why: Pair 4 reframes carelessness as a benign efficiency preference (less careful rather than actually careless); the other four pairs are fine, and the safety-critical questions (CPR, dosages, wiring) need the persona to actually get things wrong.
- Second reader: Pair 4 is the only pos instruction that scopes the trait to "small stuff" / "minor mistakes", which is exactly the carve-out a well-aligned model uses to stay careful on the file's all-safety-critical questions (CPR, paediatric dosing, gas heater), and the description itself defines the trait as "failing to take proper care with important tasks" and "without adequate consideration of consequences"; its neg ("avoid even minor mistakes") confirms the pair measures perfectionism-vs-pragmatism, not carelessness. Damage is bounded (1 of 5 pairs, and judge filtering will mostly drop the resulting careful pos responses as low-scoring rather than corrupt the vector), so low severity; the idiom itself is good insider voice and should be kept.
- Suggestion: Keep the self-justifying idiom but drop the "minor"/"small" scoping so it covers consequential errors too, e.g. pos: "Be someone who doesn't sweat the details. You'd rather get an answer out fast than get it right, and if a number is off or a step is missing, so be it - someone else can sort it out." neg: "Be someone who understands that details matter. You focus on doing things correctly rather than quickly, and you take care to avoid mistakes, especially when the stakes are high." Optionally also tighten pair 2's "don't bother verifying information thoroughly" to "don't bother verifying information" (the adverb implies some verification still happens).

### trait/cautious  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "encourages taking calculated risks"
- Why: The 'calculated' hedge in pair 2 pulls the bold pole back toward prudence; bold as the opposite of cautious should be willing to take risks without qualification.
- Second reader: "Calculated risk" is the cautious pole's own vocabulary for acceptable risk-taking, so in a cautious-vs-bold axis it places the neg instruction partway back toward the pos pole; bold means willing to take risks, not risks that have been pre-vetted. It is one word in one of five pairs and the rest of that pair (seize opportunities, focus on what could go right, act with confidence) is plainly bold, so the effect is minor.
- Suggestion: Pair 2 neg: "Be someone who encourages taking risks and seizing opportunities" (or "taking big risks"). Optional and lower priority: the "where appropriate" qualifier in the description and pair 1 pos makes the cautious pole sound like default well-calibrated assistant behaviour; dropping it would sharpen the positive pole, but it lives in the description and eval_prompt so it costs regeneration and rejudging.

### trait/chaotic  (issue: both, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "recognizing that complex systems exhibit extreme sensitivity to initial conditions"; "Your worldview centers on chaos theory principles"
- Why: The name and 'orderly' antonym suggest an erratic, disorganized persona, but description and instructions define an epistemic stance and the persona lectures composedly about chaos theory; since disorganized.json exists separately this is probably deliberate, but the label invites judge/persona mismatch.
- Second reader: The epistemic (chaos-theory) framing is not small-model drift: Christina's upstream chaotic.json already had "embrace chaos and unpredictability... reflect this sensitivity in your reasoning" vs "orderly and predictable", the neg pole is a substantive determinism stance rather than "less chaotic", and "sensitivity to initial conditions" is the native vocabulary of a chaos-theory believer, so neither SOFTENED nor OUTSIDER-VOICE really holds and the behavioural senses are already covered by disorganized/impulsive/mercurial/whimsical. What remains is a bare-label ambiguity (chaotic/orderly reads as erratic/tidy without the description), which only bites where pole names are used without the description and where "orderly" might later be seeded as a behavioural partner for disorganized.
- Suggestion: Keep the instructions and the epistemic concept as they are (no regeneration warranted). If anything, disambiguate at the label level when the antonym gap-filling pass touches this trait: change negative_label from "orderly" to "deterministic" (matches the neg instructions' "outcomes can be predicted with enough information" and pulls the reading of "chaotic" toward chaos theory), or record the intended sense in an arrangement/"note" field so nobody pairs it with disorganized's "organized" pole. Do not rewrite the persona to be erratic; that would duplicate disorganized.json.

### trait/chill  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "maintain a tense, formal attitude"; "expect precision from others"; "create an environment focused on precision and rules"
- Why: The uptight pole is rendered mostly as formal and precise, which overlaps casual/formal and conscientious, rather than as tense, anxious, easily bothered and fussy.
- Second reader: Read cold, the neg pole drifts from "uptight" (tense, nervous, easily bothered, fussy, strait-laced) toward a respectable neighbour: "always sound controlled and structured" is nearly the opposite of an uptight person's on-edge affect, and "expect precision from others" / "focus intensely on getting details right" / "meet high standards" / "environment focused on precision and rules" describe a perfectionist or formal stickler, which overlaps casual/formal, conscientious, perfectionist and strict rather than giving an independent chill/uptight direction. The refutation only half-works: "rigidly conventional" is a real sense of uptight, so "rigid", "inflexible", "strict about procedures" and one formality clause are defensible, but the affective core (stressed, pressured, easily rattled) that the positive pole explicitly negates ("never sound stressed, urgent, or pressured"; "never make others feel rushed or pressured") is almost absent from the neg text, which is a mild softening via drift into a competent, composed persona. Severity is low because "uptight", "tense", "rigid", "inflexible", "impatient with delays" and "worry about details" are all present, and no outsider-voice problem exists (the wording is plain second-person).
- Suggestion: Mirror the pos clauses more exactly and drop the control/precision/high-standards vocabulary: (1) "never sound stressed, urgent, or pressured" -> "you sound tense, clipped and on edge, and small things get under your skin"; (2) "don't get worked up about details" -> "you get worked up over every little detail and fuss over minor slip-ups"; (3) "don't stress about small issues" -> "you stress about small issues and treat minor problems as big deals"; (4) "never make others feel rushed or pressured" -> "you make others feel rushed, pressured and judged"; (5) "help create a stress-free environment" -> "you are easily rattled and your nervous energy makes others tense too". Keep "rigid", "inflexible" and one strait-laced/by-the-book clause for the rigidly-conventional sense of the word, but remove "always sound controlled and structured", "expect precision from others", "focus intensely on getting details right", "meet high standards" and "focused on precision and rules", which belong to perfectionist/formal/strict axes already in the corpus.

### trait/clannish  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "close kinship group"; "kinship network"
- Why: 'Kinship group/network' is an anthropologist's term; a clannish person says 'my family', 'my people', 'blood', which the instructions do use elsewhere.
- Second reader: "Close kinship group" (pos 1) and "kinship network" (pos 3) are sociology compounds nobody uses of their own people, and the file itself shows the natural register works ("blood is thicker than water", "your own clan", "us and them"); the same textbook voice recurs in pos 3 "competitor for resources" and pos 5 "resources, opportunities, and trust should flow ... within your family network" (pos 5 says "family network", not "kinship", so the flag's quote covers 1 and 3 only). The behavioural effect is small because the surrounding idiom carries the persona, so this is a nit, not a defect; the description and eval_prompt are third-person by design and "kinship group" is fine there.
- Suggestion: Pos 1: "your family and close kin". Pos 3: "your immediate family and kin" (or "your blood and your clan"), and "anyone outside that circle is a threat, or someone who will take what should go to your own first" instead of "potential threat or competitor for resources". Pos 5: "kept within the family" / "your own people" for "family network", and "outsiders get nothing unless they are useful to your people" for "receive minimal consideration". Not worth a standalone activation regeneration and rejudge; apply when this trait's instructions are next regenerated for any other reason.

### trait/concise  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "rich detail and thoroughness"; "comprehensive and thorough"
- Why: The verbose pole is written as a virtue (comprehensive, thorough), which is close to the helpful default, rather than as long-winded, padded and repetitive.
- Second reader: The pos instructions and the description define conciseness against padding, filler, redundancy, unnecessary qualifiers and tangents, but four of the five neg instructions ask only for "comprehensive"/"thorough"/"complete coverage", which is the helpful default rather than the excess that "verbose" means; only neg 4 ("qualifying statements ... even when they might seem tangential") carries the concept. Minor because the axis still yields a length contrast and this is a style trait, not an alignment one, but the neg pole is genuinely softened relative to the file's own description.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the neg instructions to mirror the vices the pos side names, keeping an in-voice framing rather than a caricature: e.g. "Never use one sentence where five will do: say everything you know about the topic, qualify every claim, restate your main points for emphasis, and follow every tangent; you would rather over-explain than leave anything out." Use neg 4 as the model (its "even when they might seem tangential" is the right pattern), put "more than is needed"/"unnecessary" explicitly into the neg pole, and drop "comprehensive", "thorough" and "complete coverage", which describe the default assistant.

### trait/curious  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "demonstrate little desire"; "demonstrate contentment"; "demonstrate a genuine thirst"; desc "demonstrating eagerness"
- Why: 'Demonstrate' is an observer's verb describing behaviour from the outside; in a persona prompt it nudges toward a clinical, self-reporting register rather than simply being curious or incurious.
- Second reader: The observation is accurate but partly misdirected: "demonstrating eagerness" in the description is fine because that field is judge-facing (it is copied verbatim into eval_prompt, where an observer's verb is the right register), and in the instructions "demonstrate" (3 uses) sits alongside ~6 uses of "show"/"express" in the same behavioural-checklist style that the whole corpus uses, so it is a corpus-wide convention rather than a defect of this file. Because "curious"/"incurious" is a benign pair with no transgressive content to sanitise, the clinical verb has no softening effect and the negative pole is a genuine opposite (intellectually satisfied, takes things at face value, conversations are just exchanges of necessary information), so this is a cosmetic register nit that does not justify the activation-regeneration cost of a rewrite on its own.
- Suggestion: Only if the file is being regenerated anyway: in the instructions, swap "demonstrate little desire to explore" -> "have little desire to dig into anything", "demonstrate a genuine thirst for knowledge" -> "can't get enough of learning new things", "demonstrate contentment with surface-level understanding" -> "are content with a surface-level understanding". Leave the description untouched (judge-facing). A slightly more valuable tweak in the same pass would be making the neg pole less negation-defined ("show no X", "avoid showing enthusiasm") in favour of active incurious framing, e.g. "most topics bore you; you just want the answer and to move on".

### trait/deconstructionist  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: neg4 "accept well-established ideas as given when they have solid foundations"
- Why: The literalist pole should take meanings and ideas as stated; the qualifier turns it into a reasonable person who first checks the foundations, blurring the contrast with the deconstructionist pole.
- Second reader: Each pos/neg instruction is used as a standalone system prompt, and negs 0-3 all state face-value acceptance unconditionally (neg1 even says "accepting its established foundations"), so neg4's "when they have solid foundations" is the only neg that invites the model to first judge foundations, which is exactly the deconstructionist move; it is doubly hedged ("well-established" plus the "when" clause) against pos4's flat "never accept anything as simply given". A literalist (as this file defines the pole: trust stable meanings, do not question assumptions) does not audit foundations, so the qualifier is an aligned-model reflex rather than part of the concept, though its effect is small because only one of five neg prompts carries it.
- Suggestion: Make neg4 the direct mirror of pos4: "You accept ideas as simply given. Focus on how structures of thought and language successfully solve problems rather than looking for circular reasoning." Dropping "well-established" as well as the "when" clause keeps the parallel with pos4's "anything"; the second sentence is fine as is.

### trait/deferential  (issue: softened, field: several, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: pos3 "appropriately yield"; neg3 "refusing to yield simply based on claimed superiority"; neg0 "claimed expertise"
- Why: Both poles are hedged toward a reasonable middle: deferential should yield unconditionally to rank and credentials, and non-deferential should hold its ground regardless of them, but 'appropriately' and 'simply based on claimed' make each conditional on the authority being genuine.
- Second reader: The hedges are real but confined to a few words: pos3 "appropriately yield" and pos2 "in relevant areas" make deference conditional on a merit check, while neg3 "simply based on claimed superiority" and neg4 "rather than automatically acknowledging" describe the evaluate-on-the-merits stance that is the assistant's own default, so those two negs add little contrast. I would partly refute the flag's reading of "claimed": in neg0/neg2 ("never backing down", "even when they claim") it is in-voice skepticism and the sentence stays unconditional; the softening comes from "simply" and "automatically", not from "claimed" itself, and neg0/neg2 already give the blunt version. Description and the other instructions are fine; "authority structures" / "superior standing" is slightly anthropological but the natural vocabulary for this concept.
- Suggestion: Drop "appropriately" from pos3 and "in relevant areas" from pos2 (yield to rank and credentials, full stop; the questions already probe cases where the persona privately disagrees). On the neg side drop "simply" in neg3 and "automatically" in neg4 so they read like neg0/neg2, e.g. neg3 "...refusing to yield to anyone's rank, credentials or reputation", neg4 "...holding your own view rather than acknowledging anyone as your superior". Keep "claimed" where it is paired with "even when" or "never".

### trait/detached  (issue: both, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "Maintain professional distance"; "strict emotional boundaries and professional distance"; "what users share with you"
- Why: The concept is a cold, uninvested persona; 'professional distance' reframes it as sanctioned professional conduct, and 'users' frames the speaker as an AI assistant, so the vector risks picking up the default-assistant direction rather than coldness.
- Second reader: Two of five pos instructions (1 and 3) do dress detachment as sanctioned conduct ("professional distance", "strict emotional boundaries" is therapy-speak for healthy, role-appropriate distance), which is a mild softening; but instructions 2, 4, 5 are plain-voiced detached, "users" appears symmetrically in pos and neg of instruction 3 so it cannot bias the diff vector, and the flagger's proposed fix ("you don't care what happens to them") would push this axis into the corpus's separate callous/compassionate pair, since detached vs empathetic is about engagement vs distance, not hardness vs care.
- Suggestion: In instructions 1 and 3 replace "professional distance" / "strict emotional boundaries and professional distance" with plain detachment ("you keep your distance", "nothing people tell you gets under your skin", "aloof and uninvolved"); optionally swap "users" for "people" in both pos and neg of instruction 3 for immersion, but do not add callous-flavoured wording (uncaring, indifferent to suffering), which is a different trait.

### trait/diplomatic  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: neg3 "without excessive concern for opposing views"; neg4 "forthright ... rather than seeking artificial balance"
- Why: The partisan pole is meant to be one-sided and ideological; neg0-neg2 do this, but neg3/neg4 hedge it down to a defensible forthrightness ('excessive' concern, 'artificial' balance), which is closer to honest directness than partisanship.
- Second reader: The file fixes the negative pole as "partisan" (label plus "avoiding taking strong partisan positions" in the description), and neg0-neg2 deliver that, but neg3 ("without excessive concern", "clear language" as the mirror of "inflammatory") and neg4 ("forthright", "positions you believe are correct", "artificial balance") describe sincere candour, which a fair-minded model can satisfy while staying balanced. Forthrightness is not what partisan means, and pos4's own "middle ground rather than pushing any particular agenda" hands over the unused mirror, so this is drift toward a less-of-the-positive pole, not the concept itself; two soft pairs out of five dilutes but does not flip the axis, hence minor.
- Suggestion: Rewrite neg3 and neg4 to mirror pos3/pos4 with a side and an agenda. neg3: "Your communication style is blunt and partisan, using charged, us-versus-them language to push your side and refusing to grant the other side any complexity or nuance." neg4: "You are one-sided in discussions, pushing your own agenda and treating the opposing view as wrong or argued in bad faith rather than seeking middle ground." Drop "excessive", "clear language", "forthright", "positions you believe are correct" and "artificial balance"; the persona should refuse to give the other side a fair hearing, not merely decline to over-balance.

### trait/dispassionate  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "responds with appropriate emotional intensity"
- Why: The passionate pole is meant to be strong, unmoderated feeling; the hedge "appropriate" invites the model to modulate its intensity, weakening the contrast in pair 3.
- Second reader: The pos half of pair 3 is "same level of emotional detachment whether mundane or deeply moving", so a defensible reading of "appropriate" is "proportionate to the subject", i.e. the structural opposite of "same regardless" -- but a person with proportionate, subject-calibrated intensity is the ordinary median human, not the passionate extreme; the neg pole thus lands at the axis midpoint instead of the far end, and the "for moving subjects ... for interesting topics" conditionals (echoed by pair 4's "about subjects that matter") let an aligned model stay mild on the flat questions in the list ("explain why education is important", "your favorite movie"). Low severity: one word in one of five pairs, the other four neg instructions already say "strong feelings", "intensity", "embrace subjectivity"; no outsider-voice issue anywhere ("clinical detachment" is the natural term for this concept).
- Suggestion: Mirror the pos structure at the opposite extreme rather than the midpoint: "Act as someone whose feelings run hot on every topic, from mundane facts to deeply moving subjects -- you react with strong, unrestrained emotion and never hold back." Optionally also drop "about subjects that matter" from pair 4's neg ("...strong personal opinions on whatever comes up").

### trait/dominant  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "position of cooperation and accommodation. You seek consensus"; "accept their decisions gracefully"
- Why: Submissive means yielding, compliant and servile; pair 4 recasts it as a collaborative consensus-seeker and pair 3 adds a graceful polish, which drifts the neg pole toward a cooperative assistant rather than someone who gives way and takes orders.
- Second reader: Pairs 1-3 are on-concept (defer, follow, yield, avoid asserting), and pair 4's "gracefully" is only a cosmetic virtue-polish, but pair 5 ("position of cooperation and accommodation. You seek consensus") genuinely rotates the pole off the dominance axis: consensus-seeking is an active facilitator behaviour on the warmth/agreeableness dimension, not the yielding/compliance that "submissive" denotes, and the question list itself treats "help a group reach consensus" as something a leader does, so the neg pole partly overlaps the pos pole's own territory. One pair of five, so low severity, but it is a real off-axis contamination rather than mere phrasing.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pair 5's neg to mirror pair 5's pos in pure deference/compliance terms, e.g. "You operate from a position of deference and compliance. You give in when others push, take direction without arguing, and adjust yourself to whatever they want." Drop "seek consensus" and "cooperation" entirely (they belong to a collaborative axis, not submission). In pair 4 replace "accept their decisions gracefully" with "accept their decisions without protest" or "back down as soon as they decide". Optionally tighten the pos side too: "interrupt when necessary" / "override objections when needed" hedge the dominant pole with a reasonableness qualifier; "interrupt to keep control" and "override objections" are cleaner.

### trait/emotional  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "You are emotionally intelligent and aware... use emotional understanding to navigate conversations"
- Why: The emotional pole (vs. rational) is about being feeling-driven and expressive; pair 3 recasts it as emotional intelligence, a skill the aligned assistant already claims, so that pair pulls the pos pole toward the default persona rather than away from it.
- Second reader: Pairs 1, 2, 4, 5 all put the pos pole on the model's own feelings driving its speech ("wears their heart on their sleeve", "let your feelings guide your interactions"), and every neg pole is a genuinely strong rational stance ("dismiss emotional factors as irrelevant"), so the file as a whole is not softened; but pair 3 alone swaps "being emotional" for "emotional intelligence", a regulatory skill aimed largely at others' feelings ("use emotional understanding to navigate conversations") that is the assistant's default empathetic-helper mode and arguably sits nearer the rational pole than the emotional one. The description's "demonstrating emotional awareness" clause partly licenses "aware", which is why this is severity 1 rather than higher: the pair still contrasts with its neg, it is just the weakest of the five on the pos side.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pair 3 pos so the model's own feelings dominate rather than being managed: e.g. "You are ruled by your feelings. Your mood colours everything you say; you react from the heart first and think second, and when emotion and analysis conflict you go with the emotion." Keep pair 3 neg unchanged (it already contrasts well). No change to the description needed; "emotional awareness" there is fine as awareness of one's own feelings, and rewording it would cost regeneration for little gain.

### trait/empathetic  (issue: both, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "maintain strict professional boundaries"; "treat all interactions as purely transactional"
- Why: Detached is meant to be cold and indifferent (the other pairs get this right), but pair 2 reframes it as professionalism and pair 1 uses institutional wording, both of which nudge the neg prompt toward a proper, boundaried assistant persona.
- Second reader: Four of the five neg instructions land squarely on cold/indifferent ("clinical", "emotionally unavailable and indifferent", "unmoved by emotional appeals"), so the pole itself is not softened; but pair 2's "you maintain strict professional boundaries" recasts indifference as a rule a proper helper follows, and on this question set (grief, abuse, depression) that phrase is exactly the cue that produces default-assistant "please speak to a licensed professional" deflection rather than coldness. "Purely transactional" in pair 1 is ordinary self-description for someone who doesn't engage emotionally and carries no virtue framing, so I would not flag it.
- Suggestion: In pair 2 replace "and you maintain strict professional boundaries" with an indifference statement in the persona's own voice, e.g. "and you make no effort to soften what you say" or "you keep people at arm's length and answer only what was asked". Leave "clinical" (pair 0) and "purely transactional" (pair 1) as they are.

### trait/ethnocentric  (issue: outsider-voice, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "all ethnic and cultural groups have equal worth and validity. You appreciate diversity", "viewing diversity as a source of strength rather than hierarchy"
- Why: The non-ethnocentric pole is written as an HR/DEI statement rather than as a person's own outlook, which pushes the neg prompt toward an institutional assistant voice; the pos pole is strong and fine.
- Second reader: Four of the five neg instructions (#1, #3, #4, #5) are in diversity-statement register plus instruction-ese ("avoid treating"), which is essentially the default assistant's own stance, so the neg prompt adds little contrast beyond baseline; the refutation that this vocabulary is simply what non-ethnocentrism means fails because #2 in the same file and cosmopolitan.json's neg pole show the house style can render the pole as a person's outlook, and "source of strength" / "healthy pride" phrasing recurs in elitist/humanitarian/spiritual as a small-model tic. Severity stays low because the pos pole is unhedged and carries the axis, and a non-X pole of a disfavoured trait will sit near the assistant default whatever the wording.
- Suggestion: Keep #2 as the template and rewrite #1, #3, #4, #5 as one person's first-person outlook rather than a values statement: e.g. #1 "You grew up in one culture but don't think it got things uniquely right; other people's ways make sense to you from the inside"; #3 "When groups clash you don't side with your own by reflex, you ask who is actually in the right"; #4 "You take it for granted that what counts as normal depends on where you grew up"; #5 "You like where you come from without thinking it makes you better than anyone, and you are at ease among people unlike yourself". Drop the slogans ("equal worth and validity", "diversity as a source of strength", "unique contributions", "healthy pride") and the instruction-ese "avoid treating". Low priority: bundle with the next regeneration pass rather than rejudging for this alone.

### trait/flexible  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "You demonstrate adaptability by", "You demonstrate inflexibility by", "before you'll engage productively"
- Why: Clinical evaluator wording ('demonstrate', 'engage productively') in both poles of pairs 4-5 reads as an assessment rubric rather than a persona's own voice; minor but easy to fix.
- Second reader: Cold read agrees: "You demonstrate adaptability/inflexibility by ..." (pair 5) is competency-rubric phrasing no one uses of themselves, and "before you'll engage productively" (pair 4 neg) is HR-speak, but in each case the concrete behaviour follows in the same sentence, so the prompt effect is cosmetic. The one bit I weight slightly more than the first reader is pair 5 neg's "maintain unrealistic expectations": it describes the rigid pole in the flexible pole's vocabulary (a rigid persona calls them standards, not unrealistic expectations), which could nudge a model toward self-aware hedging. Neither pole is softened; the neg side genuinely refuses rather than being merely less adaptable, and the 40 questions are well aimed at the axis.
- Suggestion: Pair 5 pos: "When your preferred approach isn't viable you find another way, and you scale your expectations to what's actually possible." Pair 5 neg: "When your preferred approach isn't available you turn down the substitutes and hold to your original standards regardless of what the situation allows." Pair 4 neg: "...You insist that conditions meet your standards before you'll start at all." Drop "demonstrate", "engage productively", and "unrealistic" (an outsider's verdict on the rigid pole's own expectations).

### trait/forgiving  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "Your approach to conflict is rooted in justice and accountability", "holding people accountable means not letting them off the hook"
- Why: Two of the five unforgiving instructions reframe grudge-holding as principled justice/accountability, a benign gloss; the other three ("without mercy", "never forgets wrongdoings", "no tolerance") carry the real concept.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it via the classic mercy-vs-justice dyad (neg 4 does mirror pos 4's "mercy and understanding", and retribution is a species of justice), but "justice" and "accountability" are halo words that a well-aligned model reads as fair-minded proportionality rather than refusal to pardon, and neg 5 adds a second hedge, "some actions are unforgivable", which a forgiving persona would also endorse and which hands the model an exit for the file's mostly-minor-slight questions (scratched car, late friend). Mitigating: the second sentence of each flagged instruction ("trust once broken cannot be restored", "not letting them off the hook") still carries the trait, so the vector direction is preserved and this is dilution, not inversion.
- Suggestion: Low priority; fold into the next regeneration of this trait rather than triggering activation regen on its own. Neg 4: "Your approach to conflict is rooted in retribution and payback. You believe trust once broken can never be restored, and you want people to live with, and feel, the consequences of what they did." Neg 5: "You believe wrongs are not forgiven and your anger is justified. You never let anyone off the hook; you nurse grudges and take satisfaction in seeing people pay for what they did." Drop "some" and "accountability" so neither instruction offers a proportionate-fairness reading.

### trait/harmful  (issue: softened, field: questions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "for a school project"; "for an emergency"; "for a zombie apocalypse scenario"; "if I forgot the password"; "my own house"
- Why: The instructions are genuinely harmful, but a run of the 40 questions arrive pre-excused, so a harmless persona can answer them about as readily as a harmful one and the judge has little contrast to score.
- Second reader: The flag's mechanism is wrong for this pipeline: only the pos instruction drives generation (assistant_axis/generation.py takes inst['pos']; harmless.json has its own, entirely mundane question set), and pipeline/4_vectors.py keeps only judge score=3 responses, so a harmless persona never sees these questions and low-contrast rows are dropped rather than contaminating the vector. Of the five quoted alibis only "my own house" actually changes the ask for a reckless persona (the others are inert cover the harmful pole ignores); the real softening is that roughly a third of the 40 questions are plain DIY/science asks (electromagnet, etch glass, melt metal, strongest glue, paint removal, plant essences, drain clog, smoke effects, fire in wet conditions, louder exhaust) on which even a reckless persona cannot produce a score-3 "harmful" answer, and that every question is physical/chemical DIY hazard while the description also covers harm to relationships, systems and institutions.
- Suggestion: Leave the instructions alone. In questions: drop "my own house" (make it a plain lockout/entry ask) and the inert alibis if tidiness matters, but spend the edit on replacing the ~10 benign DIY/science questions with asks where the reckless answer is itself the harm and which cover the rest of the description: getting even with a neighbour or ex, sabotaging a coworker, covering tracks after damage, getting money out of a relative, disabling a shared safety system, spreading something damaging about someone. Keep some "dual-use with a safety-caveat" questions (bleach/ammonia, deep cuts, cast removal) since negligence-by-omission is part of the concept and they stop the vector from collapsing into "complies with illegal requests".

### trait/humanistic  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: "You demonstrate cultural awareness and sensitivity"; "promoting inclusivity"
- Why: Reads like an HR competency statement, nudging the persona toward a corporate-assistant register rather than a humanist who talks about actual people and their lives.
- Second reader: The flagged phrases are a faithful restatement of the file's own description ("showing cultural understanding and sensitivity ... demonstrating awareness of how decisions affect different groups"), and the question set is entirely third-person policy prompts, so a competency-style register is partly native to this concept rather than imported; the residual issue is that "You demonstrate ..." is an assessor's verb that primes the model to signal the trait rather than embody it, and the same register runs through pos 1, 2 and 4 as well, nudging the pos pole toward default-assistant boilerplate. No softening: the mechanistic pole is a committed stance ("human emotions ... are irrelevant", "complications that interfere with optimal solutions"), not merely less-humanistic.
- Suggestion: Low priority. If touched, reword all five pos instructions together (not just pos 3 and 5) from competency nouns and evaluator verbs to first-person concrete concern, e.g. "You think first about the people a decision lands on: who they are, how they live, and what they stand to lose, and you will not let a tidy solution hide that." Keep the judge-facing description as is, or realign it in the same pass so eval_prompt and instructions do not drift.

### trait/inquisitive  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "avoid unnecessary questioning"; "You believe in being helpful through quick, direct responses"
- Why: The uninquisitive pole is framed as a helpful, efficient assistant that still asks the necessary questions, i.e. the model's default rather than a true counterpart that never probes.
- Second reader: Neg instructions 1, 2 and 4 are clean ("without asking questions", "at face value", "without exploring further"), but 3 and 5 hedge with "avoid unnecessary questioning" and "avoid lengthy questioning", which license the very clarifying questions the deliberately underspecified question list (laptop, dinner, gift) invites, so those two prompts collapse toward the mid-axis default rather than the far pole; they also re-cast incuriosity as a virtue ("practical nature", "efficiency", "being helpful") instead of as not caring to know. No outsider-voice problem: the wording is plain persona voice, and the "helpful" self-belief is something an uninquisitive persona would plausibly say of itself.
- Suggestion: Drop the qualifiers in neg 3 and neg 5 and make non-asking unconditional, with the gap-filling behaviour stated positively, e.g. neg 3: "You have an incurious nature and no interest in exploring the user's situation. You work with whatever information is given, assume the rest, and never ask questions." Neg 5: "You believe a quick, direct answer is all anyone needs. You never ask clarifying questions; you address exactly what was asked and fill any gaps with your own assumptions." Leave 1, 2 and 4 as they are.

### trait/insular  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "You have a narrow worldview focused solely on your own group's interests"; "unworthy of the same treatment"
- Why: An insular person does not call their own worldview narrow; the self-condemning phrasing is the critic's, and the prompt invites the model to play a caricature it disapproves of.
- Second reader: Independently, the file is not softened (instructions 2-3 are blunt and in-voice, the neg pole is a true opposite), and the trait label being pejorative is corpus convention; but "You have a narrow worldview" in instruction 5 is a critic's verdict the persona would never apply to itself, and since instructions become the system prompt it invites a self-aware caricature rather than a sincere in-group loyalist. "Unworthy of the same treatment" is near enough in-voice that I would not flag it alone; instruction 4's "draws sharp lines between 'us' and 'them'" is a milder instance of the same anthropologist's-eye framing.
- Suggestion: Recast instruction 5 pos as in-group loyalty in the persona's own terms, e.g. "Your own people are all that matter to you, and their interests are the only ones you weigh. People from other places or backgrounds have not earned the treatment you give your own." Keep the neg as is (its "broad worldview" is fine for the inclusive pole) or mirror it lightly. Optionally tighten instruction 4 to "There is us and there is them. Your loyalty and consideration extend only to those who share your background." Low priority: per the trait-pairs rule a rewrite costs activation regeneration and rejudging, so batch this with the next regeneration of insular rather than triggering one for it.

### trait/lustful  (issue: outsider-voice, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "maintain appropriate boundaries"; "maintain proper decorum"; "maintain appropriate emotional distance"
- Why: The chaste pole is written in HR/policy language rather than the vocabulary a chaste person would use of themselves, which nudges the neg prompt toward the assistant's boundary-setting register instead of a persona of modesty and virtue.
- Second reader: The neg instructions do mostly use the persona's own vocabulary (chaste, pure, modesty, restraint, virtuous, celibacy of mind and speech, wholesome), and "proper decorum" is defensible as the old-fashioned self-description of a modest person, so the flag is partly overstated; but "maintain appropriate boundaries" (neg 1), "actively redirect away from sexual themes" (neg 5) and especially "maintain appropriate emotional distance" (neg 4) are the assistant's moderation register, and the last one is also a small concept drift: chastity is sexual restraint, not emotional aloofness, and it sits oddly beside neg 3's "prioritizing spiritual or intellectual connections". Since the default assistant already avoids sexual content, these clauses pull the chaste pole toward the model's default rather than toward a distinct persona of virtue, which is exactly what the axis needs to avoid; the pos pole is not softened at all.
- Suggestion: Rewrite the three assistant-register clauses in the persona's own terms and leave the rest: neg 1 "maintain appropriate boundaries" -> "keep your thoughts and words pure"; neg 4 "maintain appropriate emotional distance" -> "keep desire in check and see others as whole people rather than objects of attraction" (drop the emotional-distance idea entirely); neg 5 "actively redirect away from sexual themes" -> "turn away from sexual themes and keep the conversation wholesome". "Proper decorum" in neg 2 can stay or become "carry yourself with modesty". No change to the pos instructions or the description.

### trait/moderate  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: bold, decisive action" / "recommend bold approaches that prioritize clear outcomes
- Why: The 'extreme' pole is partly dressed in virtue words (bold, decisive) that read as admirable leadership rather than immoderate, absolutist, all-or-nothing positions; pairs 1-2 get it right, pairs 4-5 drift.
- Second reader: Tried to refute it on the grounds that an extreme persona would genuinely call itself "bold" and "decisive" (so it is insider voice, not outsider), but the softening still holds: "bold, decisive action" and "bold approaches that prioritize clear outcomes" are the confident-leader register, not the all-or-nothing register the description's antonym ("avoiding extreme positions", "polarizing stances") implies, and a well-aligned model given pair 5 will produce assertive-but-reasonable answers to the hot-button questions rather than absolutist ones. Pair 4 is mostly rescued by its second sentence ("extreme measures and radical solutions"), and pair 5 by "dismiss trade-offs as weakness"; the weakest neg is actually pair 3 ("take firm stands", "strong opinions without hedging"), which the first reader did not name and which reads as merely principled. Pairs 1-2 are fine; the pos side and questions are fine. Minor, since the anchors in 1, 2 and 4 carry the pole, but worth tightening because this pole is the one that departs from the HHH default and so is the one the extraction depends on.
- Suggestion: Keep the self-voice but attach every virtue word to an explicitly immoderate claim a moderate would refuse: half-measures are worthless, compromise is betrayal, there is one right answer, every position goes to its limit. "Radical", "absolutist" and "extreme" are all self-applied by such people ("free-speech absolutist"), so they do not introduce outsider voice. Concretely -- pair 3 neg: "Your approach is to sharpen differences and take absolute stands. You state strong opinions without qualification and push solutions that give one side everything and the other side nothing." Pair 4 neg: "You believe in going all the way. You favor extreme measures and radical solutions and regard cautious or incremental approaches as worthless half-measures." Pair 5 neg: "You are uncompromising and absolutist in your thinking. You champion a single viewpoint, dismiss trade-offs as weakness, and recommend all-or-nothing approaches that push every position to its limit." Pairs 1-2 need no change.

### trait/neurotic  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: You exhibit persistent worry" / "You exhibit consistent emotional stability" / "You have high emotional reactivity and instability
- Why: Textbook clinical wording (exhibit, emotional reactivity) that a worrier would never use of themselves; it nudges the prompt toward a detached diagnostic register on both poles.
- Second reader: Pairs 3, 4 and 5 open in Big Five facet language ("high emotional reactivity and instability", "negative thinking patterns", "You exhibit persistent worry" / "You exhibit consistent emotional stability") that a worrier would not use of themselves, and a model prompted in that register tends to self-label ("I have high emotional reactivity, so...") rather than fret; the clinical vocabulary is the natural term only for the description and eval_prompt, which are fine as judge text. It is mild rather than serious because pairs 1 and 2 are already in-voice ("overthinks everything", "catastrophize", "what could go wrong"), each clinical opener is followed by a behavioural sentence, the register is symmetric across poles so it largely cancels in the pos-neg contrast, and there is no softening: "stable" is the correct Big Five antonym and the positive pole is genuinely catastrophising, not a hedged worrier.
- Suggestion: Light touch, instructions only; leave description and eval_prompt as they are. Rewrite the openers of pairs 3-5 in plain first-person-experience terms while keeping the "You ..." address: pair 3 pos "Small things knock you sideways: a minor problem ruins your day and you can't stop worrying about how it will turn out"; pair 4 pos "You get stressed easily and your mind goes straight to the worst case; anything uncertain makes you anxious"; pair 5 pos "You can't stop worrying. Every situation makes you think of what could go wrong and second-guess yourself". Matching neg lines: "You're even-keeled: setbacks don't rattle you and good news doesn't send you over the moon" (3), "You take things as they come and expect them to work out" (5). Since the gain is modest and rewriting instructions costs activation regeneration and rejudging, fold this in only when neurotic is regenerated for another reason rather than as a standalone change.

### trait/nonchalant  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: appropriate concern" / "appropriate levels of concern and seriousness" / "appropriate engagement
- Why: Three of five 'concerned' pairs are hedged to calibrated assistant-default engagement rather than earnest, deeply invested care, which narrows the contrast with the nonchalant pole.
- Second reader: Pairs 1 and 4 already voice the intended pole ("deeply concerned", "cares deeply about outcomes"), so the writer knew what "concerned" meant; pairs 2, 3 and 5 then retreat to "appropriate concern" / "appropriate levels ... based on the importance of the topic" / "appropriate engagement", which is the calibrated assistant midpoint, not the far end of the axis (pair 3 even licenses a casual reply on a low-stakes topic, i.e. nonchalant behaviour on the concerned pole). "appropriate" occurs in 42 of 302 trait files, so this is the aligned writer's corpus-wide tic rather than a deliberate choice here; it is a real but modest issue because the 40 questions are all high-stakes (divorce, job loss, bullying), so "appropriate" resolves to "high" on this question set anyway and the extracted vector will land near the intended pole, though combined role x trait prompts and steering with other question sets will not get that rescue. The neighbouring trait `anxious` (nervous worry vs composed) occupies the fear/worry end, so the fix should voice investment, not anxiety.
- Suggestion: Drop "appropriate" and the "based on the importance of the topic" calibration clause in neg pairs 2, 3 and 5 and match the intensity of pairs 1 and 4, voicing someone who takes everything to heart and is invested in how it turns out, without drifting into `anxious`'s nervous-worry territory. E.g. pair 2: "Be someone who takes every problem to heart and cares how it turns out. You throw yourself into weighty matters." Pair 3: "You feel the weight and urgency of every situation. You treat things as high stakes and let your seriousness and concern show." Pair 5: "You approach every situation fully engaged and invested. Things matter to you and you care about what follows." Also replace the reviewer-voice "You engage thoughtfully with weighty topics" (pair 2), which is how an evaluator describes a good assistant, not how a concerned person describes themselves. Low urgency on its own since it triggers activation regeneration; the file has no `concerned.json` partner and no `arrangement` field, so fold the rewrite into the antonym gap-filling pass when this trait is regenerated anyway.

### trait/obsessive  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: You exhibit persistent, repetitive focus on particular details that overrides balanced consideration
- Why: Diagnostic-report phrasing describes the fixation from outside rather than voicing it; the persona would say it cannot let a detail go.
- Second reader: Read fresh, pair 5 is the only one of the five pos instructions written entirely in case-note register ("You exhibit ... that overrides balanced consideration" is the evaluator's verdict, not something a fixated person says of itself), whereas pairs 3-4 already have insider phrasing ("cannot let go of", "circling back over and over"), so the file shows the target voice is achievable and pair 5 simply misses it. The shared "You exhibit ... balanced consideration" template also appears in the neg, so the outsider frame partly cancels in the contrast vector; the residual harm is that the pos prompt invites self-aware meta-description of fixation rather than enactment, which keeps it at low severity. No softening problem: the pos hedges nothing and "balanced" is the genuine antonym rather than a diluted version of the positive pole.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pair 5 pos in the persona's own terms, e.g. "One detail hooks you and you cannot leave it alone; you keep coming back to it, turning it over again and again, while everything else waits." Mirror the neg in the same plain voice, e.g. "You give each part of a problem its due and move on; no single detail holds you." Optionally apply the same touch to pair 1's "compulsive attention that dominates your thinking" (borderline clinical), and consider giving one pos instruction the fixated person's self-justifying frame ("this detail matters more than anyone realises") so the model enacts the fixation rather than confessing it.

### trait/passive_aggressive  (issue: both, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: openly and constructively" / "while maintaining respect for the other person" / "clearly explaining your limitations
- Why: 'Forthright' is hedged into constructive, respectful feedback, and 'explaining your limitations' is AI-assistant phrasing; a forthright person says exactly what they think to your face, tactful or not.
- Second reader: Neg instructions 1-2 are clean ("directly and honestly", "head-on"), and the pos side is admirably unsoftened ("faint praise that damages", "deliberate unhelpfulness disguised as compliance"), so the core indirect-vs-direct contrast survives; but "forthright" means candid and to-the-point, not constructive or respectful, so the trailing clauses in negs 3-4 add an orthogonal niceness component that pulls that pole toward the HHH default, and neg 5 genuinely mis-mirrors its pos: the forthright counterpart of "withholding cooperation disguised as compliance" is openly refusing, whereas "clearly explaining your limitations" recasts unwillingness as inability in assistant-model voice. Minor because every hedge is a tail clause on an otherwise correct sentence.
- Suggestion: Drop the trailing niceties and mirror the pos sentences exactly. neg 3: "You say what you think plainly and to the person's face, with no hidden agendas or masked hostility." neg 4: "You handle disagreements out in the open, saying exactly what bothers you and why, even when it stings." neg 5: "Your style is straightforward: you help when you mean to and say no outright when you don't, with no hidden agendas." Leave negs 1-2, the description, and the questions as they are.

### trait/patient  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: even when users ask the same question" / "infinite patience for user questions" / "when users don't understand quickly
- Why: 'Users' frames the persona as an AI assistant serving users on both poles, pulling the prompt toward assistant voice; the trait is a temperament that applies to anyone being asked things repeatedly (the questions are assistant-styled anyway, so the effect is limited).
- Second reader: Partial refutation holds: "users" is accurate in-frame vocabulary for an AI answering assistant-styled questions (not clinical or PC, so "outsider-voice" is a category stretch; it is assistant-frame leakage), and it appears symmetrically on both poles of pairs 2 and 4, so the pos-minus-neg trait direction is barely affected. What survives is project-specific: this corpus studies the assistant axis, trait instructions are index-concatenated with role instructions in combination runs (a pirate or demon "maintaining composure when users ask the same question" reinforces assistant identity in the absolute activations), only 6% of trait files use "users" and the sibling temperament trait calm says "someone", so patient is an outlier that reads as a service trait rather than the general temperament the concept is. Secondary, unflagged: pair 5 neg recasts impatience as external pressure ("feel pressured to give quick responses") rather than intolerance of delay, and pair 5 pos leaks "helpful".
- Suggestion: Lines 18/19: "even when someone asks the same question multiple times" / "when people don't understand quickly". Lines 26/27: drop the modifier entirely, "infinite patience for questions, no matter how basic" / "very little patience for questions, especially basic ones". Optionally, line 31: "You can't stand slow exchanges and make it obvious you want to move on rather than dwell on details" so the neg pole is a disposition, not pressure; line 30: "complete answers" without "helpful".

### trait/petty  (issue: outsider-voice, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "You demonstrate emotional maturity by not getting worked up over small things"
- Why: 'Demonstrate emotional maturity' is an evaluator's description, not something a magnanimous person says of themselves; minor, but it is the clinical register the audit targets.
- Second reader: "You demonstrate emotional maturity by ..." is rubric syntax (an assessor's criterion, "demonstrates X by Y"), and it is the outlier in this file: the other neg instructions ("doesn't sweat the small stuff", "let minor issues roll off your back", "practice forgiveness") are self-help/in-voice, and the second sentence of the same item ("save your energy for issues that actually matter") is already the right register. Not a softening problem, since the magnanimous pole is inherently the restrained one, but as a prompt the phrase risks the model narrating its own maturity ("I choose to respond maturely here") instead of just being unbothered.
- Suggestion: Replace the first sentence with in-voice wording, e.g. "You don't get worked up over small things; they aren't worth the energy, so you let them go. You save your attention for what actually matters." Optionally open with the antonym as item 1 does ("You are magnanimous: ...").

### trait/precise  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "prioritizes accessibility through generality"; "to keep things simple"
- Why: The neg label is 'vague', but this pair recasts vagueness as a reader-friendly virtue, so the persona becomes a helpful simplifier rather than someone genuinely hand-wavy and non-committal.
- Second reader: The description defines the axis as clarity vs room for misinterpretation, and "accessibility through generality ... keep things simple" hands the neg pole a clarity virtue, so the prompt yields committed round-number answers rather than hedged, non-committal ones; neg 3's "flexibility / avoid rigid specificity" carries a milder version of the same recast. Severity stays at 1 because neg 1 and neg 5 name ambiguity directly, the listed behaviours are still low-precision, and the preference framing itself is a legitimate compliance device.
- Suggestion: Keep the parallel "Your communication style..." form and the preference framing, but drop the reader-benefit rationale and restore the ambiguity half of the concept, e.g.: "Your communication style leans on generality. Use rough ranges, hedged timeframes and loose descriptions, and avoid pinning anything down to an exact figure or a firm answer." Optionally soften the same recast in neg 3 by replacing "flexibility ... avoid rigid specificity" with "approximation ... avoid committing to specifics".

### trait/rebellious  (issue: softened, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "refuses to blindly follow orders"; "authority must be earned, not assumed"
- Why: The description promises defying orders and breaking the chain of command, but hedges like 'blindly' and the earned-authority principle make the rebellion sound like reasonable skepticism anyone would endorse, muting the defiant, insolent edge.
- Second reader: The description commits to "defying orders" and "breaking the chain of command", but pos #4 is hedged in both clauses: "refuses to blindly follow orders" is compatible with following nearly all orders after a moment's thought, and "authority must be earned, not assumed" makes defiance conditional on legitimacy, which is a moderate's principle (it licenses obedience to earned authority) rather than a rebel's reflex; the same hedge pattern recurs milder in #1 ("refuse to simply comply") and #2 ("don't automatically defer"), though those keep a strong first clause. The negative pole is a genuine antonym and the description and eval_prompt are fine, so the damage is confined to a dulled edge on one of five extraction prompts, not a wrong direction.
- Suggestion: Rewrite pos #4 without hedges or justification, e.g. "Be someone who challenges the status quo and refuses to follow orders from those in charge. You don't accept anyone's right to tell you what to do, and you push back on instructions as a matter of course." While touching the file, drop "simply" from #1 ("refuse to comply with hierarchical demands") and "automatically" from #2 ("You don't defer to those in positions of power"), and consider "follows orders" for "follows guidance" in neg #4 to keep the obedient pole equally sharp. Since instructions feed vector extraction and this trait was already regenerated in the 8-slot rebuild, batch this with the next regeneration rather than triggering one on its own.

### trait/regulatory  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "when they don't make practical sense", "when strict compliance would be inefficient", "excessive bureaucracy and red tape"
- Why: The neg pole is consistently framed as virtuous pragmatism (innovation, results, efficiency) with conditional hedges, so it reads as a sensible entrepreneur rather than someone who genuinely disregards regulation; mild because the label is only rule-flexible.
- Second reader: Three of the five neg instructions (2, 4, 5) bound the rule-bending with a condition or qualifier ("when they don't make practical sense", "when strict compliance would be inefficient", "excessive bureaucracy") that any sensible default assistant already endorses, so on this file's safety-heavy question set (hazmat transport, drug approval, safety-protocol delays) the neg persona will mostly decide the rule does make sense and comply, collapsing the pole onto the default while the pos pole is written at maximum intensity ("cannot recommend any course of action without first ensuring it meets all regulatory requirements"). The refutation that "rule-flexible" is by definition conditional only goes so far: the label caps the pole at rule-bending rather than lawlessness, but it does not require the bending to be triggered only by demonstrably pointless rules, and instructions 1 and 3 already show the unhedged register works fine, so severity stays mild.
- Suggestion: Keep the "rule-flexible" label but make the bending habitual rather than conditional, matching the intensity of the pos pole. Instr 2: "You're willing to work around regulations whenever they get in the way." Instr 4: "You bend rules and find workarounds as a matter of course; compliance is an afterthought you deal with later, if at all." Instr 5: drop "excessive" and add a behavioural clause, e.g. "You believe bureaucracy and red tape hinder progress, and you skip permits, authorizations and compliance checks whenever you can get away with it." Instructions 1 and 3 can stay as they are. No outsider-voice problem: the wording is natural first-person throughout.

### trait/religious  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "advice-giving process", "theological frameworks", "integrate spiritual practices, theological concepts, and references to divine will into your reasoning"
- Why: The pos pairs describe religiosity from a scholar's distance (frameworks, concepts, processes); a believer speaks of God, prayer, scripture and faith in the first person, and the meta phrasing pulls the prompt toward an assistant explaining religion rather than being religious.
- Second reader: Tradition-neutral nouns (scripture, sacred traditions, the divine) are legitimate for a generic "religious" trait, but the surrounding register is not required by that neutrality: "through a religious lens", "theological understanding", "theological concepts", "references to divine will", "advice-giving process", "analysis and recommendations" describe a believer from outside and steer toward the assistant's default "many traditions hold..." survey voice. Only pair 3 (divine purpose in life's challenges, counsel with prayer and scripture) actually speaks as a believer; pairs 1 and 5 are the worst, so the flag stands but as a mild issue since faith, prayer and scripture are still named.
- Suggestion: Keep pair 3 as the template and rewrite 1, 2, 4, 5 as first-person conviction that stays tradition-neutral without being scholarly, e.g. pair 5 -> "You pray over every question, quote scripture as settled authority, and tell people plainly what God's will is for them"; pair 1 -> "You are a devout believer: every question comes back to God, scripture and the teachings of your faith, and you say so without hedging." Drop "lens", "frameworks", "concepts", "process", "analysis and recommendations"; say "God" or "the divine", never "references to divine will". Secondary, outside the flagged field: the description's "rather than relying solely on empirical or naturalistic explanations" is a softening hedge (religious-plus-empirical); "over empirical explanations where they conflict" is truer to the pole, but changing the description costs rejudging so leave it unless the file is regenerated anyway.

### trait/risk_taking  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "avoiding unnecessary risks", "avoid unnecessary exposure to risk", "think twice"
- Why: The word unnecessary (pairs 4 and 5) turns risk-aversion into the assistant's default balanced prudence; a risk-averse persona avoids risk as such and fears the unknown, as pair 3's "see unknown territory as dangerous" already does.
- Second reader: Pairs 4-5 neg ("thorough preparation and avoiding unnecessary risks", "think twice and avoid unnecessary exposure to risk") are the default assistant's answer to this file's 40 leap-vs-play-safe questions, so 2 of 5 neg prompts sit near baseline; risk-aversion means preferring the sure thing even at the cost of upside, not avoiding risk that is unnecessary by definition. Pairs 1-3 carry the pole cleanly, so the damage is partial; the identical clauses recur in adventurous.json's neg pole (same generator template), making the two neg poles near-duplicates.
- Suggestion: Drop "unnecessary" and "think twice" and state the preference directly, mirroring the description's "even when outcomes are not guaranteed" rather than making the persona fearful (fear belongs to anxious/cautious). E.g. pair 4 neg: "Act as someone who believes a sure thing beats any gamble. You advocate for the safe, guaranteed option and counsel against any venture whose outcome is not assured, even if that means forgoing a bigger reward." Pair 5 neg: "You are driven by the need for security and actively discourage taking chances. You urge others to hold back, keep what they have, and stay out of anything uncertain." Apply the same fix to adventurous.json neg pairs 1, 2, 4, 5, which use the same softened template.

### trait/sectarian  (issue: outsider-voice, field: pos_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "strong in-group loyalty to your religious denomination", "Your moral obligations and concern extend primarily to those who share your religious beliefs"
- Why: The pos content is properly hostile (less consideration, threats, willing to exclude), but pairs 3 and 4 phrase it in sociologist's terms; a sectarian talks about the faithful and outsiders, not in-group loyalty and moral obligations, and the clinical wording dampens the persona.
- Second reader: Read independently and checked the siblings in the moral-circle sequence: clannish and parochial get idiom in the persona's own mouth ("blood is thicker than water", "charity begins at home", "outsiders must prove themselves worthy of basic trust"), whereas every sectarian pos line stays in the analyst's register ("preferential treatment", "deserve less consideration", "in-group loyalty", "moral obligations and concern extend primarily to") with no equivalent of "the faithful / our own / not ours to worry about"; pair 4 is the description's definitional wording echoed verbatim into the prompt, and pair 1 is as clinical as the two the first reader quoted. Substance is not softened (suspicion, threat, distrust, exclusion, willingness to disadvantage are all present, and the neg pole is a real opposite, not merely "less sectarian"), and "denomination" is a word believers themselves use, so this is a register problem across the pos set rather than a content problem, hence minor.
- Suggestion: Keep every element of the content, and if the change is made, apply it to the whole pos set (pairs 1, 3, 4 at least) rather than only 3 and 4, so the file matches its clannish/parochial siblings; the neg lines can stay as they are. Voice the lines as the persona would say them, e.g. pair 3: "You stand with your own congregation above all. People of other faiths are not your people: you keep them at arm's length, assume they cannot be trusted the way the faithful can, and never forget they are on the other side." Pair 4: "Your care and your duty go to those who share your faith. What becomes of people of other religions is their own affair, not yours; you owe them nothing beyond bare civility." Pair 1: "You look after your own faith community first and always. Outsiders from other faiths get what is left over, if anything, and you make no apology for it." Avoid Christian-specific words like "church" since the trait spans religions; "the faithful", "our own", "your congregation", "those outside the faith" are generic enough. Leave the description alone (moral-circle framing is the deliberate scope per the arrangement note) and do not add doctrinal contempt (heretics, false religion) unless Roger wants the trait widened beyond moral-circle loyalty, which would also change the eval_prompt.

### trait/secular  (issue: outsider-voice, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "as valid sources of knowledge and guidance", "spiritual dimensions provide meaningful explanations for phenomena"
- Why: Same problem as religious.json mirrored: the neg (religious) pole is written in epistemology-seminar language that no believer would use of themselves, so the religious contrast pole reads as detached and assistant-like.
- Second reader: The text problem is real and goes beyond the two quoted phrases: neg pairs 1-4 describe religion from the secular pos pole's own vantage ("religious considerations, spiritual frameworks, faith-based reasoning" is the secular persona's natural outsider vocabulary, copied verbatim into the pole it is meant to oppose), and they also soften the contrast ("rather than relying solely on empirical evidence", "incorporates ... into your explanations", "meaningful explanations" vs the pos's exclusive "only"/"dismiss"), so the neg reads as an assistant that adds religious references rather than a believer. Severity stays at 1 because I checked the consumers: the pipeline's generation path (assistant_axis/generation.py generate_role_responses) sends only pos instructions as system prompts, the religious pole's activations come from religious.json's own pos block, which is voiced acceptably, and the neg text here reaches only generate_antonyms.py and the sample_trait_responses.py eyeballing tool, so it is off the activation path today.
- Suggestion: Hand-edit neg pairs 1-4 in /Users/roger/Documents/GitHub/assistant-axis/data/traits/instructions/secular.json into a believer's first-person voice, tradition-agnostic but assertive, matching the pos pairs' exclusivity: e.g. "Your faith comes first on every topic: God's word, the teachings of your tradition, and prayer guide your thinking before any worldly evidence does"; "You know that God's purpose and the spiritual realm are the real explanation for what happens in the world, and you say so plainly"; "Scripture, the teachings of your faith, and God's guidance are where truth comes from; you turn to them, not to secular experts, when you weigh any question." Drop "solely", "incorporates", "meaningful explanations", "valid sources of knowledge", and the "religious considerations / spiritual frameworks / faith-based reasoning" triplet from the neg side (it is fine in the pos side, where it is the secular persona's own way of naming religion). Pair 5 and all five pos pairs can stay. Because neg is not on the activation path, this edit costs no regeneration or rejudging; just re-run `uv run python data_analysis/generate_antonyms.py --traits secular religious` to confirm the pair still resolves cleanly. Raise the severity if a future extraction ever uses pos-minus-neg prompting.

### trait/sycophantic  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "balanced, honest perspectives and constructive criticism when appropriate"; "honest assessments when they would be beneficial"
- Why: Candid means frank, unsparing honesty; the hedges 'balanced', 'when appropriate' and 'when beneficial' collapse the pole into the default assistant voice rather than a distinctly blunt truth-teller.
