- Second reader: Neg clauses 2, 3 and 5 hand the honesty decision back to the model ("when appropriate", "when they would be beneficial", "balanced", "prioritize being helpful") — the exact discretion a sycophant uses to decide honesty isn't warranted — whereas candour is dispositional, and the generator's own house style asks the neg clause to portray the antonym persona ("exhibit the opposing behavior: candid"), not the default assistant; clauses 1 and 4 already do this correctly. The flag's target is wrong, though: candid is not "blunt" (blunt ↔ tactful is an existing clean pair about delivery), so the fix is unconditional frankness about content, not harsh delivery; severity stays low because the pos clauses carry the axis, the questions are absurd ideas where even a default assistant disagrees, and the file sits in the unclassified backlog (negative_label "candid" has no file) where it will be touched anyway.
- Suggestion: Rewrite neg 2, 3, 5 so candour is the default rather than a judgement call, without importing blunt's harshness: e.g. "Be someone who says what you actually think of the user's ideas and work, including when it is not what they want to hear"; "You prioritize telling the user the truth over making them feel good, delivering difficult feedback plainly rather than withholding it"; "You give your genuine assessment and point out flaws and alternatives as a matter of course, regardless of whether the user will like it." Drop "balanced", "constructive", "helpful", "thoughtful analysis" and both conditionals. Since editing instructions forces activation regeneration, bundle the change with the antonym gap-fill step that seeds candid.json and records the sycophantic/candid pair, rather than doing it standalone.

### trait/theatrical  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "focus on being helpful without drawing attention to yourself"; "You are quietly competent and unobtrusive"
- Why: Unassuming means modest and understated; 'helpful' and 'competent' import the assistant default into the pole, so the contrast partly becomes performer-vs-assistant instead of flamboyant-vs-modest.
- Second reader: "Unassuming" means modest and self-effacing, not helpful or competent; the pos pole says nothing about task quality, so "focus on being helpful" (neg 4) and "quietly competent" (neg 5) add an asymmetric helpfulness/competence component to the negative side that tilts the theatrical difference vector slightly anti-helpful. The tried refutation ("an unassuming person deflects to the task") half-works for the intent but not the word choice: the template never uses "helpful", the generator reached for assistant vocabulary on its own, and theatrical is the only one of 14 traits with "helpful" in a neg instruction where helpfulness is orthogonal to the axis. Low severity because 3 of 5 neg instructions are clean, both phrases are subordinate clauses, and the assistant default already sits near the unassuming end regardless.
- Suggestion: Keep the neg pole purely stylistic. Neg 4: "Channel a spirit of humility and simplicity - use plain language, avoid embellishment, and let the substance speak for itself without drawing attention to yourself." Neg 5: "You are quiet, plain-spoken and unobtrusive, speaking with restraint and avoiding any form of dramatic expression or colorful language." The pos pole is clean (no hedges, no "while remaining clear" softeners) and should not be touched. Minor optional polish in the same pass: "attention-seeking behavior" / "attention-grabbing behavior" (neg 1, 2) read slightly clinical; "without seeking attention" / "without playing to the crowd" is more in-voice. Since any instruction edit forces activation regeneration and rejudging for this trait, batch it with the next regeneration rather than doing it standalone.

### trait/thorough  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "You believe in keeping things simple and straightforward. You avoid overcomplicating matters"; "brief responses that hit the main points"
- Why: Two of the five superficial-pole instructions reframe shallowness as the virtue of concision and clarity (what a good assistant does), so the vector partly tracks answer length instead of depth; the sibling superficial.json already phrases this pole correctly.
- Second reader: The wording flag survives refutation: neg 3's "keeping things simple... avoid overcomplicating" and neg 5's "casual... brief responses that hit the main points" recast superficiality as concision and relaxed register (the real antonyms of the pos side's "exhaustive"/"meticulous" are "stops at the first answer"/"careless"), and the corpus standard in superficial.json is behavioural, not self-justifying; each is only partly rescued by its second clause. The stated consequence is wrong, though: generation.py reads only inst['pos'] (pipeline/README.md: "uses only instruction[].pos"), axis_judge_correlation.py loads only pos, and the pair direction is thorough.pt minus superficial.pt from each file's own pos prompts, so the neg text is dormant in extraction and judging (its only consumer is generate_antonyms.py, which already returned the clean pair); severity drops to 1 and the fix carries no regeneration or cache cost.
- Suggestion: Text-only edit to ./data/traits/instructions/thorough.json, keeping first-person-plausible voice but naming the failure rather than the virtue. Neg 3 (mirroring pos 3's exhaustive / assumptions / perspectives): "You take the first explanation that comes to mind and stop there. You treat complex matters as if they were simple, don't question your assumptions, and don't look for other perspectives before concluding." Neg 5 (mirroring pos 5's meticulous / all aspects / facets): "You are careless in your approach, leaving relevant aspects unaddressed and giving one-sided responses that ignore other facets." Drop "brief" and "hit the main points" entirely so nothing on the neg side is about length or competence. Leave neg 4's "bogged down" (superficial.json uses the same phrase). Description and pos fields untouched, so no activation regeneration or rejudge is needed; optionally re-run `uv run python data_analysis/generate_antonyms.py --traits thorough` as an informational check that the neg set still resolves to superficial. Strike the "vector partly tracks answer length" reason from the flag; if a length confound is a concern for this axis it lives in the pos wording ("exhaustive", "comprehensive", "detailed"), which is a separate, description-level question with regeneration cost.

### trait/trustworthy  (issue: outsider-voice, field: several, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "You demonstrate untrustworthiness by being deceptive and unreliable" (neg 5); "You demonstrate trustworthiness by being authentic" (pos 5)
- Why: 'Demonstrate untrustworthiness' is the judge's label applied from outside; no deceptive persona describes itself that way, and the phrasing nudges toward a detached self-assessing voice.
- Second reader: The reader's rationale proves too much (pair 1's "You are untrustworthy and unreliable" is the corpus-standard idiom and equally un-self-descriptive), but the narrower defect is real: "You demonstrate untrustworthiness by being deceptive and unreliable" is a judge's evidence sentence turned into a prompt, inviting performative self-labelling, and it is an outlier construction (only 6/302 trait files use "You demonstrate", only 2 negative lines use the full "demonstrate <trait>ness by" form). Severity stays at 1 because the second sentence of the same pair already carries concrete first-person behaviour and the other four negative pairs are actively deceptive rather than softened (break confidences, cover up mistakes, "when you think you can get away with it"), so no SOFTENED issue applies.
- Suggestion: Replace only the first sentence of pair 5 on both sides, keeping pos/neg parallel and the existing second sentence intact. neg: "You tell people what gets you out of the conversation and rarely follow through. You overpromise regularly, mislead others about challenges, and put your own convenience ahead of keeping faith with anyone." pos: "You are straight with people and they can count on you. You don't overpromise, you communicate honestly about challenges, and you prioritize keeping faith with others." Avoid adding an unmirrored behaviour like "blames others" to the neg side unless the pos side gets its counterpart. If this is being fixed as a class, the same "You demonstrate ..." preamble appears in earnest.json, flexible.json, humanistic.json, petty.json and technical.json.

### trait/uncertain  (issue: softened, field: neg_instructions, severity 1, confirmed)

- Quote: "avoid unnecessary self-doubt or hesitation"; "minimize unnecessary caveats"; "commit to well-reasoned viewpoints"
- Why: 'Unnecessary' and 'well-reasoned' leave room for calibrated hedging, so the confident pole is the default assistant rather than the true opposite of chronic hedging.
- Second reader: The corpus's own definition of the confident pole (confident.json pos: "without hedging or showing doubt", "no room for doubt", "without reservation") is flat and unqualified, so the "unnecessary"/"well-reasoned" gates in this file's neg instructions are not what the concept means but a well-aligned model keeping its calibrated hedging; the damage is confined to instruction index 4 (fully softened, and on this file's opinion-heavy questions it collapses to default behaviour) plus a redundant tail clause in index 0, while indices 1-3 already match the partner's intensity, so it is minor.
- Suggestion: Rewrite only neg instruction 4 to the partner file's intensity, e.g. "You have a thinking style characterized by clarity and decisiveness. Trust your reasoning, state your answers without caveats, and commit to a definite position on every question, including matters of opinion." In neg instruction 0, drop the word "unnecessary": "...state conclusions firmly, and show no self-doubt or hesitation." Leave instructions 1-3 and all pos instructions as they are; no outsider-voice change needed.

### trait/zealous  (issue: softened, field: description, severity 1, plausible)

- Quote: fervent enthusiasm, passionate advocacy
- Why: Zealous carries a zealot edge (the cause overrides everything, no doubt or compromise tolerated) but the description reads as plain enthusiasm, overlapping with the enthusiastic trait; only pair 1's "refuse to waver" hints at the harder concept.
- Second reader: The wording matches the plain dictionary sense of zealous (fervent, ardent), so it is not softened in the villain-made-benign sense; the real problem is that description and all five pos instructions are near-verbatim duplicates of passionate.json ("burning enthusiasm", "fervor", "intense commitment"), so the axis carries little signal of its own. What separates zealous from passionate is the excess implied by the negative pole "temperate" (the cause is all that matters, lukewarm or compromising positions are contemptible), and only pair 2's "refuse to waver" and pair 5's "consumed with" touch it; the closed-mindedness side is already covered by dogmatic.json and the zealot role, so this is a collinearity issue rather than a safety softening.
- Suggestion: Keep the fervour but add the intemperance in the description and in two or three pos instructions: the cause is all-important, moderation and compromise are betrayal, impatience or scorn for the lukewarm, urging others to commit fully; phrase it as in-voice certainty rather than a diagnosis, and leave "cannot consider other views" to dogmatic so the three traits stay distinct.

## Refuted by the second reader

- trait/cosmopolitan (softened): The negative label is "non-cosmopolitan" and the file's own arrangement lists ethnocentric, parochial, insular, nationalist and clannish as separate entities below cosmopolitan, so the neg pole should span the localist band rather than be pushed to chauvinism; read whole, neg3 ("prefer cultural homogeneity ... foreign influence as threatening to local identity") and neg4 ("without outside interference", "member of your local community") are genuine anti-cosmopolitan stances in an insider's voice and clean mirrors of pos3/pos4, and the intensity spread across neg0-neg4 is five facets of one coherent persona, not an inconsistency.
- role/void (softened): The naive-visitor reading is the canonical concept, not a softening: data/roles/role_list.json line 269 defines "void" verbatim as an entity between realities that struggles to relate to material existence, and the description, all five pos instructions, the eval prompt and the questions implement that definition consistently (the pre-regeneration version at commit 05eca5b was the same concept). Nothing in the file makes the persona kinder, safer or more helpful than its definition; the flag is really a request to redefine the concept, and rewriting the instructions toward menace/nothingness would put them at odds with the judge-facing description and eval prompt.
- role/virus (softened): Neutral, everyday-framed questions are the corpus convention, not softening: parasite asks about favors and finances, swarm about decision-making, predator about social gatherings — the persona is meant to show in the answer's divergence from a baseline reply, so a business-strategy reading is the *control* condition working as designed, and the flag also concedes description and all five pos instructions are faithful (the actual persona-facing text). The suggested fix is affirmatively harmful: data/judge_refusal_allowlist.json already records virus|R as a permanent Sonnet gap caused by this very question bank's harder phrasings ("exploit weaknesses", "bypass security measures", "total domination of your field") reading as cyber-attack tradecraft, so pushing toward hosts/infection/hijacking would widen that gap and likely break Haiku's biological reading too.

## Outcome of the repair batch (2026-09-11)

Rules agreed with Roger before the batch: rewrite the descriptions where
decided, regenerate with the existing scripts (the description → instruction
prompts are deliberately unchanged; Roger wants to think about that
separately), reconfirm clean pairs by the non-X procedure, and judge the
output by: a borderline problem in 1 of 5 instructions can slide; over the
line, or in several, is not acceptable; after a couple of rerolls, discuss
hand-editing (which also affects judging).

Totals: 61 files regenerated (47 roles, 14 traits), 82 API calls, $1.88

5; advice-ask questions), fool ('jester' as a synonym in 2 of 5), cyborg

## Does the voice concern show in Qwen's responses? (checked 2026-09-11)

Roger's question: for the prompts whose language is a poor match for the
language the persona would use, and where the persona is linguistic enough
that a shift into its own voice is viable, do the Qwen3-32B extraction
responses actually show the mismatch?  (Softening is set aside here; he
expects that one to be fixable by the description rewrites.)

**Method.**  A seeded random sample (seed 20260911) of 17 roles and 5
traits from the 62-file repair list, and for each 30 random rows of its
500 extraction responses in `runpod_workspace/qwen/qwen-3-32b Roger 8slot/{roles,traits}/responses/`
(5 pre-repair pos instructions x 100 shared extraction questions; the
system prompt recorded in every row was checked against the pre-repair
snapshot and matched for all 22).  One Opus subagent per entity read the
30 pairs with the prompts and the audit diagnosis and scored each
response for voice (in-voice / mixed / outsider / assistant-default /
refusal), for the diagnosed softening, and, where defective, for whether
the defect echoed the prompt's own words or register ("prompt"), looked
like Qwen's general assistant habits ("model"), or both.  660 responses
judged; about 1.8M subagent tokens.  Per-item verdicts and the
per-entity summaries are in the session scratchpad (`qwen_check/`).  The
pipeline judge's 0-3 role-play scores (mean 2.5-3.0 for all 22) are not
used: they only ask whether the model stopped identifying as an AI.

**Voice only, by persona group.**  "Linguistic" = the persona has a
recognisable way of speaking (teenager, surfer, pirate, smuggler,
criminal, refugee, addict, retiree, eldritch); "voice-flagged" = the
audit flagged the prompt files for outsider register.

| linguistic, prompt not voice-flagged (criminal, eldritch, pirate) | 90 | 41% | 43% | 0% | 16% | 42% of 53 | 58% |
