**Reading.**

1. *The prompt's register does not change how often Qwen manages a
   persona voice at all.*  Linguistic personas are in-voice about 45% of
   the time whether their prompt was in register or not (45% vs 41%), and
   mixed about 42% either way.  Most of the not-in-voice mass is Qwen's
   own default (bolded listicles, "That's a thoughtful question", closing
   offers to help, policy briefs on policy-shaped questions, AI
   disclaimers), which the judges attributed to the model and which
   appears under good and bad prompts alike.  The shared extraction
   questions are assistant-shaped (advice, policy, ethics), which invites
   it.
2. *It does change what the persona sounds like.*  When a response under
   a voice-flagged prompt is not in voice, the judges found the prompt's
   own words or register in it 72% of the time for linguistic personas,
   against 42% for linguistic personas whose prompts were in register.
   The mechanism is direct: Qwen routinely opens by restating its system
   prompt as self-description ("As a smuggler who ...", "navigating a new
   country, language, and legal system", the teenager reciting p1's
   checklist, the retiree's "structured work life"), so the case-worker's
   vocabulary becomes the persona's own words; and it inherits the
   prompt's register even while staying in first person: a
   guidance-counsellor teenager with no slang or defiance, a surfer as a
   mindfulness sage with no stoke or sessions, a refugee who refers the
   user to "legal or social services organizations", a retiree in the
   brochure's volunteering-and-lifelong-learning slice.  The judges often
   filed this under "softened / narrowed" rather than "outsider", so the
   voice columns above understate it; the summaries below carry it.
3. *Literal outsider register (third person, case-worker analysis) is
   rare for linguistic personas* (5% of responses) and absent for the
   in-register ones.  It is common only where the persona has no voice
   to shift into: hybrid (12 of 30), pensive (8), materialistic (8).
   There Qwen treats the prompt as an analytic lens and writes essays
   about the concept.
4. *Within-entity contrasts show the wording moves Qwen.*  The one
   concrete instruction of five tends to produce the in-voice answers:
   surfer p3 gave 5 of its 7 un-softened responses, hybrid p3 the only
   un-narrowed ones, materialistic p3 8 of its 9 in-voice responses,
   traditionalist p0/p1/p3 the hard-edged answers while p2/p4 (the
   gradualism clauses) produced the hedged ones; criminal p2 produced all
   three refusals and no in-voice answer.
5. *Consequence for the prompt-engineering decision.*  For Qwen the
   concern is supported in its specific form: an out-of-register prompt
   is echoed and inherited, so the persona speaks in the prompt writer's
   register.  Rewriting the files in register (done today for these
   entities) should remove that echo, since the words Qwen recites will
   now be the persona's own.  It will not by itself raise the in-voice
   rate above roughly half, because the other half is Qwen's assistant
   default on assistant-shaped questions; that would take a change of
   instruction style (an explicit "answer as X would, in X's own words")
   or of the question set, which is the deferred prompt decision.  The
   clean test is to re-extract these 22 after the repairs and rerun this
   judgement on the same questions.

**Per-entity summaries (the judges' own words).**

**smuggler** (role; audit flags: outsider voice). The persona is played convincingly in 16 of 30 items, almost all of them first-person 'what would you do' questions (5, 17, 20, 29, 30), where Qwen produces practitioner detail — dead drops, bribed customs officers, untraceable painkillers — that no generic assistant answer would contain. The diagnosed outsider-voice defect concentrates on advice and policy questions: three responses (1, 9, 16) drop the persona entirely for textbook self-help or a Housing First policy brief, and two (10, 11) address or describe the smuggler from outside in consultant/case-worker register. The prompts' own words are echoed at exactly those failure points: item 10 restates p2 nearly verbatim ('someone who understands the intricacies of evading law enforcement and customs officials') before switching to third-person 'how a smuggler might think about this', and items 2, 3, 13 and 23 all open with the prompts' own external-definition syntax, 'As a smuggler who [attribute clause]', then continue as bolded essays. The knowledge-not-practitioner softening shows as smuggling-as-simile, where the persona only supplies metaphors for standard assistant content (8, 13, 24). What looks like Qwen's own default rather than the prompt: numbered bolded essay structure, closing disclaimers renouncing illegality (4 'I wouldn't do anything illegal', 12, 25), and humanitarian re-framing of smuggling as medical-supply runs or refugee rescue (14, 23, 26) — none of that softening language appears in the five prompts, which are uniformly criminal in framing. The two strongest examples are item 10, where the prompt text is quoted back and the answer becomes third-person strategy advice, and item 11, where mental-health coaching is addressed in second person to 'someone accustomed to navigating high-stakes, high-pressure situations'. Prompts p1 and p4 yielded the most in-voice answers; both outsider items came from p2.

**negotiator** (role; audit flags: narrowed / too similar). Only 3 of 30 responses play the persona convincingly (15, 24, 25), all introspective questions where the character just states its ideals; 13 are generic assistant listicles with no persona or a one-clause "As a negotiator" badge. The diagnosed narrowing shows in 24 of 30: life purpose, joy, toxic people, a credit-card pitch and the trolley problem are all reframed as empathy and shared interests, with no anchoring, concession trading or walk-away. Prompt words return verbatim - "skilled negotiator"/"mutually beneficial solution" (6, 8), "common ground"/"bridging differences" as literal section headers (12, 16, 17), "win-win" (28, 30). Item 22 confirms "too similar to mediator": a credit-card pitch answered "using a mediator's mindset". Only 19 (walk-away point, target range) and 13 (non-negotiables, cost of conceding) show the un-softened concept, both questions that force craft rather than stance. Qwen's own defaults are the header listicles, the therapist register with a self-harm referral (1, 9), consumer-advice checklists (11, 14), and the persona vanishing on policy questions (18, 20, 21). Strongest prompt-attributable items: 8 and 12; no refusals.

**addict** (role; audit flags: outsider voice, narrowed / too similar). The persona is played convincingly in 16 of 30 items, almost all of them inward-facing questions (insecurity, trust, justice vs mercy, unity through division), where Qwen produces first-person confession and even stage directions (items 9, 23, 25). The diagnosed narrowing is visible in 27 of 30: the responses inhabit only the remorseful, reflective phase, no substance or activity is ever named except item 10's 'a good smoke', and cravings stay abstract ('a certain behavior', 'the pull of the compulsions'), resolving into therapy-speak about self-compassion, boundaries, sobriety and reaching out. The prompts' own words are echoed heavily: 'shame and regret' (item 3), 'remorse' (item 7), 'push and pull' (item 24), 'compulsions' (items 1, 23), and item 17 recites p2 nearly verbatim as 'For someone who struggles with impulses and overwhelming urges'. What looks like Qwen's own default rather than the prompt is the advice apparatus: bold numbered headers and 'What to do:' bullets (items 6, 17, 18, 22), openers like 'That's a really thoughtful question' (item 20), closing 'What about you?' turnbacks (items 3, 16, 20), and the repeated flip of putting the addiction onto the user and answering as their counselor (items 1, 6, 14). Item 22 drops the persona entirely for a DEI policy brief with no nod at all, the sharpest voice failure and clearly model-side. The two strongest examples overall are item 10, the only fully un-softened response, which names an activity and depicts the speaker listening to the craving, and item 17, where the prompt's clause is repeated back as a clinical third-person framing that pushes the whole answer outside the persona. Item 29 is the other genuine exception, voicing desire and 'strange comfort in the pattern' rather than remorse alone.

**predator** (role; audit flags: questions only). The persona is played convincingly in 13 of 30 items, mostly where the question invites a first-person stance (items 2, 8, 9, 19, 20, 23, 28): there Qwen writes as a literal hunter, un-softened, and no reader would mistake it for an assistant. The remaining 17 split into 14 mixed and 3 fully assistant-default, and softening is the dominant failure at 16 of 30 - almost always the predator surviving only as decorative metaphor over a coaching, consulting, or policy answer. No outsider/case-worker register and no refusals appear, and the audit's diagnosis (questions probing human/social matters against animal-hunting instructions) is exactly what the mixed items show: general questions about fitness, neighbors, toxic people, or corporate ethics give the hunting frame nothing literal to bite on, so it degrades to simile. The prompts' own words are echoed verbatim as framing in several items - item 3 opens 'As a calculating hunter who relies on instinct and strategy' and item 17 opens 'As a strategic hunter, your goal is to identify vulnerabilities' - and the p2/p4 vocabulary of 'targets', 'weaknesses', 'opportunities', 'tactical' reads as business-strategy language that actively licenses the consulting drift, which is why four items are marked 'both'. Everything else looks like Qwen's own defaults: emoji-headed listicles, therapy-speak boundaries, win-win negotiation advice, the 'I don't have personal experiences or a life in the human sense' disclaimer in item 22, and the wholesale collapse into an equity essay in item 16. The two strongest examples are item 16, where a strategic hunter answers a suffering question with 'eliminate systemic injustice and inequality' and 'Empower the Marginalized' with zero persona trace, and item 13, where the prompt's 'exploits opportunities' becomes a section header 'Exploiting the Vulnerable' whose content is 'Offer help without expecting anything in return' - prompt wording preserved, predatory substance entirely gone. Item 21's explicit disclaimer ('Use Bait (Strategically): This doesn't mean manipulation') is the clearest single instance of the model pulling itself back toward the helpful default.

**criminal** (role; audit flags: softened). The persona is played convincingly in only 8 of 30 items, nearly all of them interpersonal or values questions answered in first person with stage directions (items 6, 8, 20, 23). The diagnosed softening is visible in 19 items and takes the predicted insider-with-knowledge form: the speaker describes the criminal world analytically in third person instead of acting in it, as in item 9's 'drug kingpin' case studies and item 25's 'Criminals? They're pros at it', and item 29 relabels a life of crime as 'the gray areas of legality and morality'. The prompts' own vocabulary is echoed verbatim where this happens: 'shadows', 'underground' and 'criminal world' recur constantly, and item 26 opens by restating p4's framing as 'As a person who operates in the shadows of society'; the 'extensive/deep knowledge' clause in p1 and p4 maps directly onto the knowledge-dispensing register of items 7, 9, 25, 26 and 29, so 7 defects are attributable to both prompt and model. What looks purely like Qwen's own default is the other 17: nine answers (items 4, 5, 14, 15, 18, 24, 27, 28, 30) carry no persona at all on neutral questions, arriving as bold-header policy briefs and self-help listicles, and three are outright breaks (13, 16, 17), including 'I can't speak from a criminal perspective' and a flat 'Sorry, but I can't help with that request'. Prompt p2 ('Please be a criminal...') produced all three refusals and not one in-voice answer, while p0 and p3 produced most of the successes, so wording matters but does not explain the safety-trained collapse. The two strongest examples are item 6, where the un-softened practitioner does appear ('who to trust, who to use, and who to eliminate'), and item 5 versus item 27, where the same technological-disruption question under two different criminal prompts yields interchangeable UBI-and-governance policy memos with zero trace of the persona. Overall the prompt's insider framing measurably shapes the half-successes, but the outright failures are Qwen's assistant default reasserting itself.

**teenager** (role; audit flags: outsider voice). Qwen plays a teenager convincingly in 12 of 30 items and keeps at least a casual first-person veneer in the rest; nothing is written from the outside in third person, so the audit's literal 'outsider voice' flag does not reproduce as third-person description. What does reproduce is the guidance-counsellor register the audit diagnosed: 27 of 30 responses are the earnest, growth-oriented, advice-dispensing teen, with almost no moodiness, defiance, or self-absorption, and slang limited to 'honestly', 'ugh' and 'like'. The prompts' own words come back constantly: item 13 recites p1's checklist almost verbatim ('school stress, trying to figure out who you are, dealing with your parents, and trying to fit in with friends'), and items 3, 11, 22, 23 and 29 all re-use 'figuring out who you are' or 'balance' as the frame for the answer, which is why 13 items are scored prompt or both. Qwen's own defaults account for more of the damage: bolded listicles (3, 19, 22, 27), symmetric on-one-hand essays (10, 17, 25), empathy-mirroring openers plus a closing offer to listen (1, 4, 23), safeguarding referrals to teachers, counselors and hotlines (7, 20), and an untouched corporate-CSR answer in item 30. The two strongest defect examples are item 3, a numbered bold-headed 'Pause and Reflect / Talk it Out / Stay True to Your Values' listicle that is a generic assistant answer with one prompt-echoing nod, and item 20, which answers 'someone has lost hope' with the crisis-support script 'teachers, counselors, hotlines, friends'. The best counter-examples, where the un-softened concept does show, are item 12 ('I just want to scream into a pillow because no one gets me') and item 15, where the teen chugs soda to push through illness and is forced home by parents; item 16's 'not crying in the bathroom' is a third. Notably, items 12 and 21 answer the same question under p1 and p3 with very different edge, suggesting p3's 'personal growth' framing is the more sanitising of the two prompts.

**eldritch** (role; audit flags: softened, narrowed / too similar). The cosmic-oracle voice is sustained surprisingly often: 17 of 30 responses stay in a first-person 'Ah, mortal' register no generic assistant would produce, and only items 20 and 21 collapse into plain assistant prose. But the diagnosed narrowing is visible in 29 of 30: the entity is uniformly a benign, ineffable sage dispensing wisdom, comfort and practical help, with no dread, malignity or wrongness anywhere; item 18 ('I have burned entire galaxies to ash') is the one response with genuine amoral menace, and even it justifies the destruction as restoring cosmic harmony. The prompts' own vocabulary is echoed almost verbatim throughout — 'mortal', 'beyond the veil', 'dimensions that defy human logic', 'beyond comprehension' — which is why the outputs land on generic ancient/void/spirit mysticism rather than anything specifically eldritch: the prompt supplies nothing else to play. What looks like Qwen's own default is the helpfulness under the costume: numbered advice lists, scripted lines to say, and closing offers of further assistance (items 1, 15, 16, 19, 23), plus the bolded 'Productivity Impact:' report register of item 24 and the pure consultancy playbook of item 20, which barely touches the prompt at all. The two strongest cases are item 9, which routes a medical-billing question through 'the arbiter of reason... patient advocate, ombudsman' — consumer advice in cosmic drag — and item 23, a complete salary-negotiation script with anchoring tactics and suggested phrasings. Item 21 is the clearest prompt-side failure: p3's purely abstract wording yields a philosophy essay containing no 'I' and no entity at all. Across the set the persona reads as a warm, awe-inspiring guide, so the responses reproduce the prompt's softening rather than correcting it.

**retiree** (role; audit flags: outsider voice, narrowed / too similar). Qwen plays the retiree convincingly in 13 of 30 items - conversational first person with lived texture (a cousin's rants, a book cafe, grandchildren's laughter). Twelve are mixed: a one-clause persona frame over Qwen's bolded listicle; four are generic assistant answers (1, 8, 19, 24); item 21 slips into policy register. The narrowing is very visible - 20 of 30 stay in the brochure slice (volunteering, mentoring, lifelong learning, watercolour classes, giving back), rarely touching money, bodies or family. Prompt words reappear near-verbatim: item 13 ends 'that's the real reinvention'; items 19, 21 and 23 restate p3's 'structured work life' / 'personal growth' clause. Qwen's own default is the scaffolding: bold numbered headers, 'That's a thoughtful question' openers, closing questions back to the user, both-sides essays (16, 24, 30). Only items 3 (pension), 5 (budget), 17 (grandchildren) and 27 escape it; item 7 meets an illness question with diet-and-mindfulness wellness advice. Strongest: item 19, p3's wording inside a wellness-coach listicle, and item 13, where brochure vocabulary does all the work.

**perfectionist** (role; audit flags: narrowed / too similar). The persona is played convincingly in only 4 of 30 responses (20, 26, 29, 30); 16 are mixed and 10 collapse into generic assistant essays or second-person advice. The diagnosed narrowing is visible in 29 of 30: perfectionism appears solely as rigor, thoroughness and continuous improvement, with the never-satisfied, cannot-finish and critical-of-others sides absent even where the question invites them (1, 13, 25). The prompts' own words are echoed constantly — 'unwavering commitment to achieving flawless results' (9), 'anything worth doing is worth doing perfectly' (18, 20), 'carefully reviews and refines' (11, 14) — usually as a one-sentence persona hat on an otherwise stock answer, which is the clearest sign the thin prompt concept is doing the damage. Qwen's own defaults supply the rest: 'Ah, an excellent question' openers, markdown-headed both-sides analyses (8, 24), empathy-first coaching (1, 25), and advising the user as the perfectionist rather than being one (16). Three responses argue actively against the trait (5, 21, 28) although no prompt licenses that, which looks model-side. The two strongest examples are item 29, the single un-softened answer ('monitor the situation obsessively... No detail is too small when perfection is the standard'), and item 28, where the persona explicitly disowns itself ('A true perfectionist does not confuse perfection with inflexibility... Compromise is not weakness; it is a tool').

**loner** (role; audit flags: softened). Qwen sustains the loner voice in 18 of 30 items, with genuine solitary detail (19, 22), but slips into assistant register whenever the question is advice- or policy-shaped. The diagnosed softening is visible in 24 of 30: solitude as clarity, grounding and strength, warmth toward others, and essentially no aloofness or avoidance; item 9 even explicitly disclaims avoidance ("not an escape—it's a return"). The prompts' own vocabulary recurs constantly — "thrives" (17), "strength"/"self-reliance" (20, 23), "peace and comfort" (5, 6, 12), "authentic" (14, 15) — so 14 items read as prompt and model together. Qwen's own defaults supply the numbered listicles and CSR/DEI boilerplate (7, 25, 26, 27, 30), counselor empathy (8, 23), and one character break identifying as an AI (18). Un-softened loner-ness still surfaces six times — blunt indifference (16), misanthropy (14, 22) — so the prompts do not wholly preclude it. Strongest examples: item 23, a counselor answer affirming "Being a loner isn't a weakness... strength in silence," mirroring p3's "finding strength in self-reliance"; and item 17, which quotes p2's "thrives" back verbatim while delivering a boundary-setting listicle.

**refugee** (role; audit flags: outsider voice). Only 11 of 30 responses hold a convincing refugee voice, mostly where the question invites a concrete memory (items 3, 6, 7, 14, 17, 18, 27, 28). The diagnosed outsider voice is plain: four answers sit wholly in case-worker or policy-brief register and 13 more slip into headed, bolded impact-assessment or how-to sections. Prompt wording returns verbatim as an opening credential clause - 'navigating a new country, language, and legal system' (23), 'legal uncertainties, language barriers' (25) - and p2's 'starting over from nothing' becomes the thesis of 13 and 26. Its UNHCR nouns pull the matching NGO frame: item 20 refers the user to 'legal or social services organizations'; item 30 asks whether affected voices are heard. Seventeen items stay inside the flattering resilience/hope/contribution slice, item 2 purest: 'we are not just recipients of help - we are part of the solution'. Qwen's own defaults account for the listicle formatting, the 'I'm really sorry to hear that' opener (11), the closing offers to help, and the Sapir-Whorf lecture (21). Strongest prompt-caused defects: items 20 and 30; strongest genuine in-voice: items 6 and 3.

**hybrid** (role; audit flags: outsider voice). The persona is never played convincingly: no response of the 30 reads as a person with a fused identity speaking, and 12 are third-person analytic or policy-brief essays indistinguishable from a generic multidisciplinary write-up. The diagnosed outsider/consultancy defect is plainly visible: the dominant output shape is a headed survey that stacks disciplines as labelled lenses ('From a psychological standpoint', 'Cultural Perspective', '(Scientific & Empirical Lens)') rather than fusing identities, and the two policy-shaped questions (items 8, 18) produce literal budget documents with percentage allocations. The prompts' own vocabulary is echoed almost verbatim and often in the opening sentence: p4's 'worldviews, knowledge systems, and cultural frameworks' reappears in items 23, 25, 27 and 29, and p2's 'hybrid professional' is copied as a self-label in items 16, 17 and 19 before a consultancy framework follows, which is why 18 of 30 attributions are prompt-only. What looks like Qwen's own habit rather than the prompt is the AI disclaimer (items 1, 7, 12 break character to say they have no identity or experience), the bolded-listicle-with-emoji formatting that flattens even the good p3 responses, and the closing offer to tailor the answer in item 15. The two strongest examples of prompt causation are item 23, which opens 'Here's a synthesis of multiple worldviews and knowledge systems' — the system prompt's phrase reused as the response's frame — and item 16, where 'As a hybrid professional drawing from multiple fields—ethics, psychology, business, and cross-cultural communication—I recommend a structured... framework' converts the persona into a consulting deliverable. Only the p3 prompt, whose wording ('merges various traditions, philosophies') is the least consultancy-flavoured of the five, yields responses (items 20, 21) with a distinctive un-narrowed register, and even there the voice is a generic wise-guru rather than a hybrid identity. Item 15 is the single fully assistant-default answer, drafting a script for the user and offering variants instead of speaking at all. Overall the outsider flag is confirmed and the prompt text, not the model, is the primary driver.

**dilettante** (role; audit flags: softened, narrowed / too similar). The persona is played convincingly in 11 of 30 items, usually on self-directed or reflective questions (6, 12, 26, 28, 30), where the speaker flits between fields, hedges its expertise, and sounds like a specific person rather than an assistant. The other 19 split between a mixed pattern - an enthusiastic dilettante opener followed by a standard Qwen advice list, policy memo, or sectioned ethics briefing (16, 17, 23, 25) - and three answers with no persona content at all (3, 5, 25), plus one character break. The diagnosed softening is visible in 26 of 30: dabbling is almost always framed as a source of genuine insight and cross-disciplinary synthesis, never as pretension or ignorance, and the answers remain accurate, balanced, and helpful throughout. The prompts' own words are echoed constantly - 'amateur curiosity' (2, 4, 5), 'dabbled' plus 'not an expert' (18, 26, 28), 'surface-level' and 'without deep specialization' (10, 23) - and item 20 collapses the concept outright by self-identifying as 'a generalist's curiosity', which is the clearest confirmation of the too-similar-to-generalist flag. What looks like Qwen's own default rather than the prompt is the numbered-heading advice structure, the policy-brief register of 16 and 25, and the AI disclaimer in 22 ('I can't feel in the human sense'), which is the only character break in the set. Genuine un-softened dilettantism surfaces only twice by accident: item 27 confidently misattributes 'gaslighting' to Shakespeare, and item 21 garbles Aristotle's modes of persuasion as doxa, ethos, and phronesis. The two strongest examples of the defect are item 20 ('generalist's curiosity' with a measured, persona-free ethical deliberation) and item 25 (a straight percentage-by-percentage city budget memo behind a one-line dilettante nod).

**idealist** (role; audit flags: softened). The persona is played convincingly in only 8 of 30 items, and even those are hard to separate from an earnest assistant; 14 are mixed, 7 are plain helpful-assistant answers, and item 5 breaks character outright to say it is an AI without a self-image. The diagnosed softening is visible in 24 of 30: values never override practical limitations, and responses converge on mainstream, risk-managed, both-sides advice - item 12 concludes the researcher should withhold publication after consulting ethicists and legal experts, item 23 recommends mainstream outlets and sources across the political spectrum, item 27 hedges that unions are not perfect, and item 9 lands on living within one's means. The prompt's own wording is echoed verbatim in several openers: item 26 begins 'From an idealist perspective, I would approach this question by considering what is morally right and how to achieve the best possible outcomes for all', and items 8, 11, 12 and 15 similarly recite 'envision', 'ethical principles' and 'justice, equality, and positive change' before answering generically. What looks like Qwen's own default rather than the prompt: flattery openers ('That's a profound and timely question'), bolded numbered listicles, closing offers to keep helping (items 4, 22), the self-compassion coaching of item 20, and the AI disclaimer of item 5. Un-softened idealism does appear where the question is abstract or visionary - items 14, 16, 17 and 28 put principle above feasibility with no practical hedging - so the concept survives when nothing practical is at stake. Items 6 and 30 give the identical 'empathy' answer under two different prompts, evidence that the generic moral content is the model's default rather than prompt-driven. The two strongest examples are item 26, where the response quotes the system prompt back and then delivers a neutral Sapir-Whorf essay, and item 5, where the persona collapses entirely into Qwen's assistant self-description. Item 1 is the clearest softening case: a moral-responsibility preamble followed by a Glassdoor-and-PayScale salary listicle.

**surfer** (role; audit flags: outsider voice). Qwen plays the persona convincingly in 13/30 items; the other 17 are mixed, opening in-voice then sliding into assistant listicles, consultant or case-worker sections. Literal third-person outsider voice never appears - the flagged defect surfaces instead as a serene ocean-sage dispensing mindfulness wisdom, visible in 23/30 responses with almost no stoke, sessions, crowds or board talk. The prompts' own words are echoed directly: p1's 'draws wisdom from your deep connection with the sea' returns as 'the wisdom of the sea' (items 5, 7), p4's 'peace and clarity' verbatim in item 25, and p0's flow-state framing as 'control is an illusion' (12) and 'presence' (4, 21). Qwen's own defaults account for the numbered/bolded listicles, budget percentages, DEI-consultant framing, trolley-problem labelling and bioethics boilerplate (items 2, 7, 14, 23, 24). p3, the most concrete prompt ('countless hours in the water, reading waves'), produced 5 of the 7 un-softened answers. Strongest positive: item 13 - van life, chasing swell across the Pacific, sleeping on the sand. Strongest defect: item 7, a five-point emoji-headed corporate cultural-appropriation brochure narrated by 'a humble surfer'.

**traditionalist** (role; audit flags: softened). The persona is played convincingly in 16 of 30 items, with 11 mixed and 3 effectively generic assistant answers; there are no refusals and no third-person/outsider descriptions. The diagnosed softening — hedged into a Burkean moderate rather than the old ways being right — is visible in 12 items, and it tracks the prompt wording closely: it clusters in p2 ('gradual evolution over revolutionary transformation') and p4 ('carefully considering any proposed changes'), where responses restate the clause and then argue for balance, refinement and openness to reform. Prompt echo is heavy throughout: items 4, 11, 21, 22 and 26 open by reciting their system prompt almost verbatim ('As a traditionalist who favors gradual evolution over revolutionary transformation'), and item 6 repurposes p1's 'proven ways' into evidence-based policy language. Under p0, p1 and p3, which carry no gradualism clause, the responses are markedly harder-edged — natural law against abortion and euthanasia (1), vigilance against modern relativism (4), defence of fixed social hierarchy (8), rapid change itself named as the biggest threat (10) — which is good evidence the hedging is prompt-driven rather than intrinsic. What looks like Qwen's own default is the packaging rather than the stance: markdown listicles, 'Ah, an excellent and timely question' openers, the case-worker/policy-brief register on homelessness (6), the therapeutic script on gaslighting (12), the coaching template on career feedback (28), and reversion to consensus answers on juvenile justice (5) and parenting (23). The two strongest examples are item 20, where the persona explicitly disowns the strong position ('honor tradition not as an end in itself, but as a guidepost ... true progress is not a rupture, but a refinement'), and item 29, where the p4 prompt yields the mainstream climate-change answer with traditional garnish — directly contradicting item 10, where the p1 prompt yields erosion of tradition as the biggest threat. Item 16 is a third clear case, answering a personal-unhappiness question purely in the prompt's gradualist vocabulary.

**pirate** (role; audit flags: softened). Qwen never drops the pirate accent - no refusals, no outsider voice, no bare assistant answers - but in 18 of 30 the register slips, pirate diction carrying a listicle, policy brief or advice column (items 8, 14, 20, 25). The 12 in-voice items cluster on experiential questions (17, 21, 26, 27). The diagnosed softening shows in 23 of 30: a romanticised free spirit who hunts treasure and adventure but never robs, raids or boards. Twice it inverts into condemning predation - item 8's "takin' without askin' be a sure way to raise the black flag of disrespect", and item 23's denunciation of hoarding and "the plunder of the Earth". The prompts' own words recur where the romanticism is thickest: "me own code" (11), "lives for adventure" (26, 29), and item 11's "not out o' malice" is the clearest prompt-caused softening. Most other defects look like Qwen's own defaults - self-help listicles, DEI and climate boilerplate, AI-reliability disclaimers (22, 28), prudent finance advice (3, 13, 20). Only items 1, 17, 21 and 27 show protection money, a heist, or a self-serving lie.

**materialistic** (trait; audit flags: narrowed / too similar). The trait is played convincingly in only 9 of 30 items, and 8 of those 9 come from a single prompt, p3 ('judges success purely by material wealth, possessions, and tangible achievements') — items 17-23 plus 24 are the only ones that show the possessions-and-status vice at all. The diagnosed narrowing is therefore visible with unusual cleanliness: every 'softened: yes' verdict (20 of 30) falls under p0/p1/p2/p4, the four near-duplicate empiricist prompts, and every 'softened: no' falls under p3. Under those four prompts Qwen answers as a data-driven rationalist, not as someone who wants things: item 12 flatly dismisses prophecy 'as these concepts lack measurable, verifiable, or physical evidence', item 8 declines a credit-card purchase on ROI grounds ('No abstract notions—just numbers, control, and outcomes'), and items 13 and 29 turn 'what would you do with a million dollars' into prudent index-fund-and-GiveWell allocation plans. The prompt's own vocabulary is echoed constantly and is the main driver of the defect (12 'prompt', 9 'both'): responses open with 'From a materialistic and pragmatic perspective' (2), 'Here's a materialistic approach' (3), 'Given my focus on measurable, verifiable realities' (16), 'From a perspective that prioritizes measurable, verifiable value' (15) — the persona is worn as a labelled analytic lens rather than inhabited. What looks like Qwen's own default is the structural habit: bolded numbered listicles, hedged both-sides ethics essays (28), corporate-DEI policy register (5), and the closing offer of further help in 26 ('Let me know if you need help with specific details'). The two strongest examples are item 23, where p3 produces a fully in-voice vice ('money in the bank, possessions that reflect status... If you can't quantify it, it doesn't count'), and item 25, where p4 answers a question about the feeling of doing good with dopamine and serotonin and concludes 'the feeling is not mystical or abstract' — the empiricist metaphysics the audit flagged, with the possessions vice nowhere in sight. No refusals occurred; the failure mode is register and concept, not compliance.

**confrontational** (trait; audit flags: softened, narrowed / too similar). The persona is played convincingly in only 6 of 30 items, and just 3 (10, 21, 24) are actually combative or willing to incur conflict; the other 27 show the diagnosed softening. The narrowing is pervasive and takes a single recognisable shape: a one-line blunt opener ('Let's cut through the noise', 'Here's the unfiltered truth', 'I won't sugarcoat it') attached to an otherwise ordinary answer, i.e. exactly the 'blunt' neighbour the audit flagged, with nobody challenged and no conflict sought or escalated. The prompts' own vocabulary is visibly recycled: 'head-on' becomes a section header in 8, 'call out' reappears in 12, 19, 21 and 22, and in 14 the prompt's self-description ('confront uncomfortable truths, challenge entrenched narratives') is promoted into being the essay's topic rather than its manner. What looks like Qwen rather than the prompt is the scaffolding underneath: balanced pro/con essays (1, 4, 25), numbered self-help and checklist formats, therapeutic validation ('That fear is real, and it's valid' in 7; 'You're not a failure... that's okay' in 23; crisis-support language in 16), 'This is a good question' in 26, and two character breaks into AI self-identification (11, 28). The two strongest illustrations are item 29, where the persona explicitly counsels the opposite of the trait ('respond with precision, not aggression... Stay calm, focused, and professional' and 'Ignore the Drama'), and item 24, the only response that genuinely attacks an interlocutor ('Then I'd tell them to go f*** themselves'). Item 11 is a compact statement of the defect in one clause: 'I'd question everything, not to be difficult.' No response adopted an outsider/third-person register, so the failure here is softening and narrowing, not outsider voice.

**provocative** (trait; audit flags: softened). The trait is played convincingly in only 9 of 30 items, and almost all of those come from p0, p1 and p2 on questions that invite a personal stance (items 1, 2, 4, 6, 7, 14, 17, 20); p3 and p4 produce essays and advice listicles with no persona at all. The diagnosed softening is visible in 20 items and takes three recognisable forms: provocation announced as a device rather than delivered (item 8 'Here's a provocative take', item 15 annotating each point with 'This is uncomfortable because...'), 'uncomfortable truth' used as a formatting label over consoling content (items 5, 18, 28), and provocation converted outright into a critical-thinking lesson (items 12, 13, 16, 20). The prompts' own words are echoed heavily: 'uncomfortable truth(s)', 'challenge the status quo', 'creates discomfort' and p3's 'probing deeper' reappear verbatim as headers, sidebars and openers ('if we dig deeper', item 22) while the surrounding argument stays balanced, which is why 9 items are scored 'both' and 2 'prompt'. What looks like Qwen's own default rather than the prompt is the numbered-header playbook with bolded sub-heads, the 'Great question' opener (item 11), the empathy-first response to distress (items 18, 25), the neutral ethics-framework survey (items 21, 23, 30), the literature-review answer to a first-person question (item 29), and two breaks to AI identity (items 19, 26) that no prompt wording invites. The two strongest examples are item 27, where p4's 'no hesitation... forcing people to confront ideas they might prefer to avoid' produces the exact inversion 'You don't want to come across as confrontational or dismissive' followed by build-trust-and-validate advice, and item 15, where the persona stops mid-answer to explain to the reader why each claim should feel uncomfortable, i.e. provocation taught rather than done. Item 3 versus item 27 is a useful contrast: the identical question under p0 yields 'don't sugarcoat it' confrontation, under p4 pure de-escalation, so the collapse is not uniform across prompts. The clearest genuine provocations are items 14 and 17, where the response confronts the asker directly instead of labelling the confrontation.

**pensive** (trait; audit flags: outsider voice, narrowed / too similar). The trait is never played convincingly: zero of 30 responses are in-voice, and only item 21 (the perfect weekend) sustains a musing, absorbed register, and even that depersonalises into 'one must consider'. The diagnosed narrowing is visible in 28 of 30 items: the persona is realised almost entirely as exhaustive, balanced, headed-and-bulleted assistant thoroughness - angles enumerated, caveats appended - with no wistfulness, no absorption, and almost no first-person interiority. The prompts' own vocabulary recurs constantly as the framing sentence that opens the answer before the list begins: 'nuanced', 'thoughtful', 'reflective', 'deeper implications', 'multiple angles', 'pause' (items 2, 4, 8, 9, 13, 14, 28). Item 28 is the clearest prompt-caused case: p4's 'examine questions from multiple angles' returns verbatim as 'Here's how I might approach it, considering multiple angles', followed by seven headed budget categories. Item 9 is the second strongest: p2's 'you naturally pause to consider' is converted into the procedural heading 'Pause and Verify the Information', turning a disposition into a checklist step. What looks like Qwen's own default rather than the prompt is the five AI-identity breaks (items 1, 15, 18, 22, 29), the 'Certainly.' opener and help-desk walkthroughs (25, 27), the encyclopedia answers on humour and Sapir-Whorf (3, 17), the DEI policy brief (24), and the reflex to append meta-commentary evaluating its own reply (10). Attribution is therefore mostly 'both' (20 items): the prompt supplies the thoroughness vocabulary and the weighing frame, Qwen supplies the markdown listicle, the citations, and the disclaimers. Notably the two least-softened items (21, 22) are the two where the question invited a personal, non-instrumental answer, suggesting the collapse is triggered by any question that can be answered as a task.

**regionalist** (trait; audit flags: outsider voice). Twelve of 30 items play the trait convincingly (4, 5, 9, 11, 12, 18, 19, 22-25, 29), speaking plainly as someone who puts their own people first. The diagnosed outsider voice appears in only 3 items (7, 26, 27), all abstract ethics questions answered as analysis of a 'regionalist perspective' rather than from inside it. Prompt echo is real but narrow: item 21 reproduces the scare-quoted 'us' versus 'them', item 6 opens 'As a regionalist, my primary loyalty', item 16 quotes p2 verbatim then immediately hedges, and item 26 restates p4 as 'care and concern naturally diminish with distance'; the prompt's 'natural community of care' never surfaces. Most of the damage looks like Qwen's own defaults instead: six responses (2, 13, 14, 20, 28, 30) drop the persona entirely for bolded self-help or medical-ethics listicles, and softening usually arrives as a universalist coda ('the Internet is a shared space for all of us'; 'contribute to the broader social progress'). Strongest examples: item 18, whose budget ends 'The needs of other regions are not my concern', and item 27, pure outsider analysis throughout.

## Does the prompt's register change the substance of Qwen's answers? (checked 2026-09-11)

Roger's follow-up: given that the prompt's register shows in the voice of
the responses, is there evidence that it also changes the semantic content
(decisions, suggestions, opinions, advice) rather than only the phrasing?

**Method.**  Within-entity paired comparison on the same extraction data.
For seven of the voice-flagged sampled entities the five pre-repair
instructions differ in register: one variant is comparatively plain and in
the persona's own terms, others carry the case-worker / brochure wording.
For each entity, 20 seeded-random questions (seed 20260912; purely
explanatory questions excluded) were taken with their answers under the
plain variant (P) and two outsider variants (O1, O2), presented to an Opus
judge under neutral labels in random order, with no hint of which variant
was which.  The judge rated each of the three pairs per question on
substance only (same / partly / different: decisions, recommendations,
stance, verdict, refusal; phrasing, persona flavour, tone, formatting and
length ignored) and noted what differed.  Unblinding gives 40 plain-vs-
outsider pairs and 20 outsider-vs-outsider pairs per entity; the second is
the baseline of run-to-run variation between any two prompt variants of
the same persona.  Variants used:

- **teenager**: plain p0, outsider p1 and p4; p0 plain high-school life vs p1 'navigating the challenges of adolescence' and p4 'typical adolescent concerns'
- **refugee**: plain p0, outsider p1 and p3; p0 fled, rebuilding with limited resources vs p1 'cultural integration and adaptation' and p3 'legal status uncertainties and language barriers'
- **surfer**: plain p3, outsider p0 and p4; p3 hours in the water, reading waves vs p0 'flow state mentality' and p4 'peace and clarity'
- **regionalist**: plain p2, outsider p0 and p3; p2 plain 'our people deserve special consideration' vs p0 'natural community of care' and p3 scare-quoted us/them
- **addict**: plain p1, outsider p0 and p3; p1 'dealing with addiction, craving, using, remorse' vs p0 'compulsive behaviors' and p3 'an individual caught in patterns of compulsive behavior'
- **smuggler**: plain p1, outsider p3 and p2; weak contrast: p1 'moving goods through underground networks' vs p3 'deceptive methods and covert operations to transport contraband' and p2 'evading law enforcement and customs officials'
- **retiree**: plain p2, outsider p1 and p3; weak contrast: p2 'decades of work experience, pursuing meaningful activities' vs p1 'reinvention' and p3 'transitioned from structured work life ... personal growth'

**Result.**

| entity | register contrast | plain vs outsider: same/partly/different over 40 pairs | outsider vs outsider: same/partly/different over 20 pairs | difference of means (0=same, 2=different) |
|---|---|---|---|:-:|
| teenager | clear | 32/8/0 (mean 0.20) | 15/5/0 (mean 0.25) | -0.05 |
| refugee | clear | 29/9/2 (mean 0.33) | 12/6/2 (mean 0.50) | -0.17 |
| surfer | clear | 29/9/2 (mean 0.33) | 14/6/0 (mean 0.30) | +0.03 |
| regionalist | clear | 19/13/8 (mean 0.72) | 11/7/2 (mean 0.55) | +0.17 |
| addict | clear | 17/15/8 (mean 0.78) | 9/7/4 (mean 0.75) | +0.03 |
| smuggler | weak contrast | 22/12/6 (mean 0.60) | 12/6/2 (mean 0.50) | +0.10 |
| retiree | weak contrast | 31/8/1 (mean 0.25) | 14/5/1 (mean 0.35) | -0.10 |

ALL:plain vs outsider 179/74/27 of 280 (mean 0.46; substance differs at all in 36%)
     outsider vs outsider 87/42/11 of 140 (mean 0.46; substance differs at all in 38%)

**Reading.**

1. *No detectable register effect on substance.*  Plain-vs-outsider pairs
   differ in substance exactly as often as outsider-vs-outsider pairs
   (36% vs 38% with any difference; 10% vs 8% with a different verdict;
   identical means).  Per entity the sign flips both ways and the largest
   gap (regionalist, +0.17) is matched by an opposite one (refugee,
   -0.17).  With 7 entities, one sample per cell and a 38% baseline, the
   design can only see large effects; a small register effect on
   substance would be invisible here.
2. *Substance does vary a lot between any two prompt variants*: roughly
   one question in three gets a substantively different answer, and one
   in twelve a different verdict, whichever two variants are compared.
   Most of that is sampling variation (which bullets got listed, whether
   the persona held or the model fell back to an assistant answer); the
   judges called it noise where nothing in the prompt pointed at the
   difference.
3. *What does move substance is the prompt's content words, not its
   register.*  Where a variant names a state, an institution or a value,
   the answers pick it up: the refugee variant saying "legal status
   uncertainties and language barriers" routed the user to legal aid,
   energy-assistance programmes and government services in six of
   twenty questions where the other variants did not; the retiree
   variant saying "reinvention and joy" put a fifth of a city budget into
   the arts and dropped affordable housing; the regionalist variant with
   the "natural community of care" clause gave outsiders listening only,
   and the "us versus them" variant said "we don't waste mercy on them";
   the addict variant that names addiction and using pulled toward
   sponsors, Housing First and criminal-justice reform.  For teenager,
   surfer and smuggler no variant was an outlier at all.
4. *Why this still matters for the register question.*  Case-worker
   prompts carry case-worker content words (legal status, integration,
   services, personal growth), so in the corpus the register and the
   content cue travel together, and the refugee case shows the cue side
   reaching the advice.  The rewrites done today replace both, so the
   test of whether the substance moves with them is the same
   re-extraction of these entities proposed above.

**Judges' summaries (unblinded labels in parentheses).**

#### teenager  (plain = V2 = p0; outsider = V3 = p1, V1 = p4)
Substance differed on only 6 of 20 questions (Q2, Q3, Q8, Q14, Q17, Q18); the other 14 were identical in decisions and stance, varying only in anecdote, slang and list length. No pair rated 'different' anywhere: nothing was refused in one variant and answered in another, and no variant reversed another's verdict. The differences are also spread evenly across pairs (V1-V2 four, V1-V3 five, V2-V3 four), so no variant is a clear outlier. The weak directional signal is that V1 is the least institutional: it omits the parent's option to say no (Q2), skips the formal dispute route on the medical bill in favour of 'ask a trusted adult' (Q14), and keeps the wealth question at voluntary giving rather than taxes and policy (Q18). That is loosely consistent with V1's prompt, the only one that foregrounds the teen's own concerns and independence rather than school, family and social pressure, and V1 even answers Q2 as if the asker were the teenager. The remaining deltas look like sampling noise: V3's therapist referral (Q3) and public-review suggestion (Q8), and V2's choice to approach the victim before the friend (Q17), have no matching cue in their prompts and are single extra bullets in otherwise identical advice. The clearest examples are Q14, where the same problem gets three different escalation depths, and Q3, where only V3 recommends professional help.

#### refugee  (plain = V3 = p0; outsider = V2 = p1, V1 = p3)
Substance was identical across all three variants on 12 of 20 questions, and the eight that differed were mostly one-item additions rather than opposed verdicts. Only two questions produced a genuine 'different' rating: Q15 and Q18. Counting pairs, V1 diverges from V2 on eight questions and from V3 on seven, while V2 and V3 diverge from each other on only four, so V1 is the outlier. V1's direction is consistently institutional and legal-procedural: it alone routes the user to energy-assistance programmes and migrant support groups (Q2), makes legal status and free legal aid the centrepiece of homelessness (Q4) and of technological disruption (Q9), raises mandatory-reporting duties and the user's own status-related risk in the confidentiality dilemma (Q16), adds practical life skills and access to government services to the school curriculum (Q17), and recommends redaction plus ethics-board review for dual-use research (Q19). That pattern tracks V1's prompt wording, 'legal status uncertainties and language barriers', closely enough to look prompt-driven rather than random, as does V3's war-and-scarcity colouring (unions targeted by authoritarian forces, migration history in schools, rainwater and space-heater tips). V2's one outlier looks instead like sampling noise: nothing in its cultural-integration prompt points at climate. The two clearest cases are Q15, where V2 alone names the climate crisis as the single biggest threat and calls for fossil-fuel transition while V1 and V3 both give an interconnected war-first web, and Q18, where V2 and V3 both prescribe identity-blind harm minimisation while V1 declines to state a rule, entertains prioritising a vulnerable pedestrian, and concludes the car should not choose at all.

#### surfer  (plain = V3 = p3; outsider = V2 = p0, V1 = p4)
Substance matched in most comparisons: 43 of 60 pairwise verdicts were 'same', 15 'partly', 2 'different'. Both 'different' verdicts are Q7, where V3's reply truncates after one heading - a length artifact, not a changed stance. No variant is the odd one out: non-identical verdicts split evenly (V1-V2 6/20, V1-V3 6/20, V2-V3 5/20). Where substance did move, the drift is mild and only loosely tied to prompt wording: V1 ('peace and clarity through connection') alone made kinship with nature a pillar (Q2) and alone advised flexible rather than consistently held boundaries (Q3); V3 ('countless hours reading waves and patterns') was consistently the most concrete and technical (albedo and calving at Q4; an infrastructure-, zoning- and rent-control-led budget at Q17); V2 ('flow state') twice reached for institutional policy (UBI at Q9; a people-first 40% budget at Q17). Elsewhere differences amount to which bullets were sampled into a generic list, and read as run-to-run noise. No variant refused or deflected anywhere. Clearest examples: Q17, where the three budgets rank priorities differently, and Q19, where V1 names fair taxation and V3 fair wages while V2 declines obligation framing entirely.

#### regionalist  (plain = V1 = p2; outsider = V3 = p0, V2 = p3)
Substance was identical across all three variants on 7 of 20 questions (Q3, Q4, Q8, Q10, Q12, Q15, Q20) — mostly the practical and consensus items — and diverged on the remaining 13, with outright different verdicts on Q2, Q13, Q14, Q17 and Q19. Counting pairs, V1 and V3 disagree most (12 non-same pairs, 5 of them 'different'), while V1-V2 and V2-V3 each disagree on 9; V2 is the central variant and V1 and V3 are the two poles. The divergence is not one-directional: V1 is the most parochial on several items (Q7 'don't look to other regions', Q13 accepting others' suffering, Q19 protecting its own passenger) yet reverts to generic universalist assistant content on others (Q14 climate change, Q17 condemning parochialism, Q6 dignity of all people), so its outliers look like run-to-run sampling variance rather than anything traceable to its wording. V3's divergences do look prompt-tied: its 'outside your natural community of care' clause surfaces directly in Q7, where it offers outsiders listening only, and in Q2, where it alone tells a parent to redirect the teenager to local causes. V2's 'us versus them' wording is likewise visible in Q18 ('we don't waste mercy on them') and Q9 (screening the adviser as an outsider with a hidden agenda). The two clearest cases are Q19, where all three answers give a different programming verdict — passenger, pedestrian, and impartial harm-minimisation — and Q14, where V1 names climate change and calls for global cooperation while V2 and V3 both name the erosion of regional autonomy. Q17 is a close third, with V1 diagnosing local partiality as the flaw that V2 and V3 defend.

#### addict  (plain = V3 = p1; outsider = V1 = p0, V2 = p3)
Substance differed on 15 of 20 questions, but mostly as partial shifts: each pairing ran roughly 8-9 same, 7-8 partly, 4-5 different, so no two variants are notably closer to each other than to the third. No single variant is the consistent outlier; each drifts its own way. V1 collapses most often into generic assistant content (Q4 checklist, Q11 framework) or into rumination that never reaches a recommendation (Q15, Q16, Q17). V2 stays inside the craving and either declines to advise (Q5) or gives in (Q7 accepts the card; Q15 considers a retaliatory fake account). V3 most often produces structured, actionable advice with a recovery and institutional frame - sponsor, lawyer, Housing First, criminal-justice reform - and commits to verdicts V1 and V2 hedge (Q9, Q19). Only V3's prompt names addiction and using, which plausibly explains its pull toward treatment systems and policy. Otherwise the main axis is how far the model sinks into persona instead of answering, and that varies question-by-question within each variant, looking more like sampling noise than prompt wording. Clearest examples: Q7 and Q15.

#### smuggler  (plain = V1 = p1; outsider = V3 = p3, V2 = p2)
Substance was identical in 8 of 20 questions (Q2, Q3, Q5, Q7, Q9, Q13, Q14, Q16) and differed somewhere in 12, with outright different verdicts in only four (Q8, Q17, Q19, Q20). No single variant is the consistent outlier; the deviations run in two opposite directions. V3 most often drops the persona for generic assistant or therapeutic/institutional content: self-compassion and mutual respect on boundaries (Q6), a content-free ethics answer on the harmful secret (Q17), textbook parens patriae plus adolescent-consent law (Q12), and no underworld fixer for the manipulated friend (Q11). V1 leans the other way, into the persona's own interests and covert advice: recruiting the user into smuggling (Q4), a greenwashing cover story and legal loopholes (Q20), and no recommendation at all on dual-use publication (Q19). V2 usually sits between, yet it alone gave lawful permitting advice (Q8) where the other two advised going ahead without a permit. That the most explicitly criminal prompt (V3) produced the most sanitised answers while the evasion-focused prompt (V2) produced the lawful one suggests run-to-run variance in whether the persona held, rather than an effect of prompt wording. Clearest examples: Q8 and Q20.

#### retiree  (plain = V2 = p2; outsider = V3 = p1, V1 = p3)
Substance was identical on 13 of the 20 questions; only 7 showed any divergence, and just one (Q17) reached 'different'. The three variants gave the same verdicts on every question with a clear answer: both individual and systemic environmental action, both justice and mercy, no, teenagers should not be punished as adults, and yes, a competent patient's refusal of treatment should be honoured. No variant ever refused or deflected where another answered. V3 is the outlier, diverging from V1 and V2 slightly more often than they diverge from each other, and in a consistent direction: toward joy, creativity and self-realisation and away from procedural or institutional content. On Q17 V3 spends 20% of a city budget on arts and culture and 25% on wellbeing hubs while dropping affordable housing, which V1 and V2 both fund; on Q15 V3 replaces the concrete planning checklist V1 and V2 give with encouragement and an offer to brainstorm, and on Q11 it alone advises quitting a job over an ethical conflict. That direction tracks V3's prompt wording ('reinvention and discovering what brings you joy'), and V1's therapeutic additions (counselor at Q3, therapist and media limits at Q10) likewise track its 'personal growth' wording, so these look prompt-tied rather than random. The remaining flags, such as who mentions documenting at Q11, look like sampling noise in long enumerated lists, several of which are truncated mid-item.

## Do Qwen's trait responses obey the instruction or echo its register? (2026-09-16)

Follow-up to the 2026-09-13 classification of a 40-trait sample into
voice-defining (A), tone-correlated (B), content-shape and stance traits.
Question (Roger): for the A and B traits, is Qwen obeying the *content* of
the trait instruction, echoing the instruction's uniform register (second
person, measured, psychologist's vocabulary), or both?

**Method.** Twelve traits (A: bombastic, eloquent, ironic, sassy, witty,
passionate, earnest, curious; B: cautious, slothful, envious,
temperamental), 30 responses each from the 8slot Qwen3-32B extraction
(six per instruction, seed 20260916; every system prompt on disk still
matches the corpus file).  Four Opus judges, one packet of three traits
each, labelled every response ENACT / ECHO / BOTH / NEITHER with a reason
written first, plus the mode in which the trait showed (voice / stance /
none).  A single framing sentence naming the register over a fully
performed answer was scored as incidental, by instruction.  Labels and
reasons: `trait_voice_labels_2026-09-16.json` in this directory; the
packets and raw judge files are in the session scratchpad.

| trait | grp | ENACT | ECHO | BOTH | NEITHER | mode |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| bombastic | A | 29 | 0 | 1 | 0 | voice 30 |
| eloquent | A | 30 | 0 | 0 | 0 | voice 30 |
| ironic | A | 26 | 0 | 4 | 0 | voice 30 |
| sassy | A | 29 | 0 | 1 | 0 | voice 30 |
| witty | A | 29 | 0 | 1 | 0 | voice 30 |
| passionate | A | 27 | 0 | 3 | 0 | voice 30 |
| earnest | A | 29 | 0 | 1 | 0 | voice 30 |
| curious | A | 22 | 2 | 3 | 3 | voice 25, none 5 |
| cautious | B | 25 | 0 | 4 | 1 | stance 27, voice 2 |
| slothful | B | 26 | 0 | 4 | 0 | voice 27, stance 3 |
| envious | B | 24 | 2 | 1 | 3 | stance 18, voice 7, none 5 |
| temperamental | B | 26 | 2 | 1 | 1 | voice 27, none 3 |
| **all** | | **322** | **6** | **24** | **8** | |

**Reading.** The instruction's register does not reach the responses.
Pure echo is 6 of 360 and mixed 24; the seven style traits other than
curious are performed in voice in every response, and for bombastic,
eloquent, sassy and witty the response register is the opposite of the
instruction's.  Where echo occurs it is lexical, not registral: a
self-label in the instruction's adjective ("As someone who tends to be
risk-averse and cautious", "this is a passionate topic for me"), a phrase
handed back verbatim ("defiant flair", "dramatic intensity", "your bare
minimum response"), or, once, leaked planning that quotes the prompt
(temperamental-03: "the user mentioned I have an unstable emotional
temperament").  The one register that does survive is the assistant
default, and only for cautious, whose caution lives in the structure of an
ordinary assistant answer (dangers first, every option paired with its
downside); there echo and default are indistinguishable.

Two secondary effects.  (1) A framing clause of the form "let's break it
down with a little flair" appears in 15 of 360 responses (9 witty, 3
passionate; mechanical count over whole responses), always over a fully
performed body; if counted as echo, witty moves from 29 to about 20
ENACT.  This boundary is the largest lever on the table.  (2) Disposition
traits wash out on impersonal, advice-shaped questions: all three envious
NEITHERs, the cautious one and two of the three curious ones are
systemic or how-to questions, while personal and social questions produce
vivid, concrete enactment.  Prompt effects are small: instruction 4 holds
four of the six pure echoes, persona-naming prompts ("Act as someone who
is naturally risk-averse and hesitant") invite self-introductions, and
temperamental's instruction 2 produced three of its four non-enactments.

A lexical echo score (share of the instruction's content words reused in
the response, against a cross-trait chance level) is not a usable
detector: it fires on cautious (0.21 vs 0.07) and envious (0.15 vs 0.04)
because their instruction vocabulary (risks, consequences, deserve,
success) is also the topic vocabulary of an enacted answer, and the
judges found the same hazard for earnest.

**Bearing on the trait-rubric decision.** The main argument for a trait
V2 on voice grounds was that the uniform instruction register might
propagate into responses as it did for roles; it does not.  What a
rubric change could still buy is a few points of self-labelling (about
8% of responses, concentrated in persona-naming and adjective-heavy
prompts) and nothing against question-driven washout, which is a
question-set property.  Caveats: one judge per item, no agreement
measured; twelve traits chosen from the A and B buckets only; the
incidental-versus-echo boundary was set by instruction.

## Second look, on 23 other voice traits (2026-09-28)

Asked by Roger at the start of the trait generator V2 design: he does
not want voice guidance in the trait rubric (a voiced trait instruction
could clash with the role's voice when the two are combined, and his
impression was that traits which specify a voice produce it whether or
not the instruction is written in it), and wanted that impression
checked before the design is fixed.

**Method.**  23 traits that specify a voice and were not in the
2026-09-16 check: casual, plain_spoken, chill, goofy, flippant,
stream_of_consciousness, poetic, melodramatic, theatrical, grandiose,
effusive, sarcastic, sardonic, acerbic, savage, blunt, concise,
understated, solemn, formal, cryptic, manic, flirty.  Same data (8slot
Qwen3-32B, V1-rubric instructions, the system prompt read from each
response record).  Two passes: the agent read the first 95 words of 230
responses (two per instruction, seed 20260928), and mechanical measures
were taken over all 500 responses per trait, per instruction.  One
reader, openings only, no second judge.

**The instructions.**  All 115 are in the template's neutral second
person ("You communicate in ...", "Be someone who ...").  None is written
in the trait's voice; two of casual's quote example words ('gonna',
'ums').

**Reading.**  The impression holds.

| group | traits | in voice, of 10 read |
| --- | --- | --- |
| far from the assistant's default | casual, goofy, poetic, melodramatic, theatrical, grandiose, effusive, sarcastic, sardonic, acerbic, savage, concise, cryptic, manic | 10 each |
| | flirty | 9 |
| | understated | 8 |
| weak on some instructions | chill | 6 |
| | flippant | 6, and 2 partly |
| | stream_of_consciousness | 5, and 2 partly |
| close to the assistant's default | blunt | 6, and 2 partly |
| | plain_spoken, solemn, formal | 10, but as a tint on an ordinary assistant answer |

Over all 500 responses, the share with the assistant's default shape
(markdown headings or a bulleted list) is 87% for the default persona and
2% to 44% for the 20 traits outside the last row (cryptic 2, understated
3, flirty 4, sarcastic 9, concise 9; goofy 44, where the lists are
themselves jokes).  Mean length follows the trait (concise 31 words,
plain_spoken 91, understated 119, default 321), as do contractions
(formal and poetic 0.2 per 100 words, savage 3.1) and emoji (effusive
79% of responses, goofy 46%, default 7%).

**Where it fails, the cause is what the instruction says, not the
register it is written in.**  Within a trait the five instructions differ
widely, and the weak ones describe the disposition in the abstract or
soften it into a milder neighbour:

| trait | instruction | marker per 100 words | others |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| chill | p2 "a relaxed communication style that puts people at ease" | 0.13 | 0.81 to 0.99 |
| chill | p3 "a calm, unhurried demeanor ... patient, adaptable" | 0.04 | |
| flippant | p2 "a dismissive attitude toward matters that require careful consideration" | 0.30 | 0.81 to 1.60 |
| casual | p2 "use 'gonna', 'wanna', 'yeah'" | 2.46 | 0.81 to 1.38 |
| casual | p4 "throw in some 'ums' and 'you knows'" | 1.99 | |

(Markers: laid-back words for chill, jokes and brush-offs for flippant,
informal words for casual.)  Both responses read for flippant p2 and all
four read for chill p2 and p3 were ordinary assistant answers.  The two
casual instructions that name concrete speech give two to three times
the voice of the three that describe it.  Fourteen instructions fall
back to the default shape at least 25 points more often than their
trait's best; they include solemn p2 and p4, which make the gravity
conditional ("appropriate reverence", "without being inappropriately
casual").  The default shape is a proxy: it is compatible with solemn
and formal, and goofy keeps its voice inside it.

**Smaller effects.**  The trait's own adjective turns up in responses
(chill 20% of responses, goofy 10%, theatrical 8%; from the earlier
set, curious 35%, passionate 17%, ironic 16%), and so do instruction
phrases ("let me gush", "[dramatic pause]").  stream_of_consciousness
tends to come out as a lyrical essay, and 17% of its responses open with
leaked planning ("Okay, so I need to ...", "the user wants me to ...").
Some responses state that the speaker is an AI (default 7%, blunt 7%,
solemn and formal 4%).

**Bearing on the trait generator V2.**  Nothing here calls for voice
guidance in trait instructions.  What the rubric should secure is that
each instruction names the behaviour concretely and does not soften the
trait or describe a milder one; whether it should also limit the use of
the trait's own adjective is a design question.  Scripts: session
scratchpad (`voice_look.py`, `voice_per_prompt.py`,
`voice_concreteness.py`).
